# Engine & games — status

> Design: [flow-driven-game](../design/flow-driven-game.md) · [live-assets](../design/live-assets.md) · [games-deploy](../design/games-deploy.md) · [delivery-builds](../design/delivery-builds.md) · Agent: `.claude/agents/engine-pixi-svelte.md`

**One-line state:** Shipped — the shared runtime (`runtime:lines`, built from `apps/lines` + `packages/*`) carries every scoped feature on `main`; the `apps/{lines,cluster,ways,scatter,number-picker,price}` are dev/reference games, and `lines`/`bookOf`/`ways` have rich (art-bearing) reference scene sets.

## Current state

What runs on `main` today (this is the ENGINE side — the runtime + reference games, not a launcher tool). Online games run the SHARED `_runtime/lines` bundle; `apps/lines` is its source.

- **Symbol sizing** — sprites are plain `contain`-fit to the cell by their native art aspect; SPINE symbols contain-fit the rig's authored box (`skeleton.{x,y,width,height}` — the Rigger's Bounds frame), CENTRED on that box via `<SpineProvider centreBox>` (2026-09-02), to `SYMBOL_SIZE × SYMBOL_SPINE_FILL` (spine-only shrink constant, `apps/lines/src/game/constants.ts`, started 0.5). The old `sizeRatios` param + the Scene Editor "Symbol size (× cell)" control were removed from the render equation (size comes from the ART, not a number; oversize spines are fixed by tightening the rig bounds in the Rigger). Cross-origin alpha-trim of sprite textures was tried and reverted (tainted `getImageData` — don't retry).
- **Persistent background scene (coded path only)** — without a screen-driving flow, a `space:'background'` scene renders behind the game for the whole session (`engine-layout/backgroundScenes.ts`), cover-fit as one group so multi-part backgrounds keep their real size + animate (`ComponentInstance` cover path). Under a v2 flow that drives the screens it is an ordinary container the flow shows/hides — the space is only the coordinate frame (2026-09-02, see [flow status](flow.md)).
- **Per-node cover-fit (`node.coverFit`)** — a cover-capable node in a normal `canvas`-space (FLOW-GATED) scene can opt into true cover-fit to the window via a `coverFit` boolean on `BaseNode` (`engine-layout/types.ts`), reusing the exact `background` cover math (`coverTransform` + `backgroundCoverScale`/`backgroundFit`/`scale` stretch) WITHOUT the always-on persistent mounting of `space:'background'` — so a free-spin intro screen can host a full-screen stretched animation the flow still shows/hides. **Which kinds cover is ONE exported list, not a condition repeated at each gate:** `isCoverFitKind` (sprite / spine / flipbook) and its texture-measured subset `isCoverArtKind` (sprite / flipbook) in `coverTransform.ts` — every gate below reads them, because when the list was spelled out per-gate the `flipbook` kind reached none of them. Runtime gate `isCanvasCoverFit`/`isCover` in `LayoutNodeView.svelte` (a `coverFit` spine is force-CENTRED on the canvas via `spineCoverCenter`, matching the sprite cover + the editor — it ignores authored x/y, since a flow screen places it anywhere); editor previews it WYSIWYG (`EditorCanvas.nodeTransform` + `isBackgroundCover`, `EditorSpineLayer` `coverFit` target, `+page.svelte` `isBackgroundCoverSelected`); authored via a "Cover / full-screen fill" toggle in `EditorProperties.svelte` (canvas-space cover-capable kinds only) that reveals the existing cover-scale/fit/stretch controls. Additive — `coverFit` unset is byte-identical, `background` scenes unchanged. Verify offline: `node packages/engine-layout/scripts/test-cover-fit.mjs`. Persistence: node fields pass through `editorStorage.normalizeNode` + `identityComponentRoot`'s child-preserving copy untouched (no allowlist), and `markDirty` always bumps the canvas redraw nonce, so no whitelist change was needed. Reaching a live game needs a runtime release + reconcile.
- **Bitmap-font layout text** — `LayoutNodeView` renders `<BitmapText>` for authored bitmap fonts via `registerFontCatalog`; coded HUD/label text honors authored bitmap fonts through the shared `engine-layout/CatalogText` component (one source, canvas `<Text>` + `<BitmapText>` can't drift). `pixi-svelte`'s `<BitmapText>` applies three font guards before pixi sees the text (`sanitizeBitmapText.ts`): a synthesised space glyph + unbaked-glyph sanitisation (the two black-screen guards) and `normalizeBitmapFontMetrics`, which corrects a descriptor that under-reports its glyph box so `fontSize` means the same thing for every font (the bundled `silver` declares 97 for 199px glyphs). The launcher's `EditorTextLayer` calls the same normaliser so the editor canvas can't drift from the game.
- **Component value/defaults + HUD readouts (B1–B4)** — the component value/defaults system + the live Balance/Win/Bet readouts now render as `hudReadout` component INSTANCES in `apps/lines` (B4.1–B4.4 flip, shipped 2026-06-08: `readoutNode()` emits `componentInstance` nodes, `LayoutEditable` mounts them + suppresses the coded snippets via `coveredSources`; `hudScenes({readouts:true})` is on for lines, off → coded `UiLabel*` binds for Borut/editor-picker = parity). Remaining tail: the **Borut mirror (B4.6)** — a submodule bump — and owner live-verify.
- **Default-HUD fallback (Phase 1, 2026-08-06)** — a project seeded with the legacy CODED `hudBar` (`UiLabel*`/`UiButton*` `bind` nodes) rendered BLANK under a flow-v2-driven game: those names aren't registered bound components (only the coded `<UI>` drew them, from the scene positions), and `de772aec` suppresses the coded `<UI>` under `flowV2DrivesScreens` so `<FlowV2Mount>` mounts them as empty containers. Fix: `normalizeHudScenes(doc)` (engine-layout) rewrites each legacy `hudBar` node → its parametric `componentInstance` (`hudReadout`/`button`) at doc load (`loadEditorScenes`), preserving the transform byte-for-byte via `labelBindToInstance`/`buttonBindToInstance`; the parametric HUD renders through the generic mounter in BOTH paths. Runs in memory (no re-save) so every old project self-heals; strict no-op for docs already parametric or with no `hudBar` (`scripts/verify-hud-fallback.mts` = 14/14). The parametric HUD is the go-forward default; the coded `<UI>` HUD is slated for retirement. Phase 1 shipped to `main` + runtime-released 2026-08-06 (PR #252), verified live on `test2` (bottom bar back). **Phase 2 (corners) — DONE (unmerged):** `HudGameName`/`HudLogo` are now REGISTERED bound components (`components-ui-pixi`), so under a flow-v2-driven game `<FlowV2Mount>` mounts the reserved `hudCorners` scene and `LayoutNodeView` resolves those binds (they mounted as EMPTY containers before — the logo/game-name twin of the Phase-1 blank bar, the open item from the 2026-07-24 double-mount fix below). The coded `<UI>` `gameName`/`logo` snippets now render through the SAME two components (one home, no drift); `hudCorners` is a RESERVED scene so no double-mount in the coded path. Phase 2 SHIPPED + runtime-released (PR #255), verified live on `test2` (clock + game name + logo back). **Phase 2b — DONE (unmerged):** the persist + reset half, in the launcher/editor (auto-deploys on push to `main`, no runtime release). (a) `editorStorage.normalizeDoc` now runs `normalizeHudScenes` — same migrate-on-read shape as `backfillAlwaysOnTop` — so the editor SHOWS the flow-v2-safe parametric HUD and the next save PERSISTS the migrated doc (and `/api/editor/doc` serves it migrated to the runtime too, before `applyHudGameNameDefault`). (b) the editor "↻ Refresh HUD layer" action → "↻ Reset HUD to default", now emitting `hudScenes({ readouts: true, buttons: true })` (was `readouts` only, which re-seeded coded `UiButton*` binds that go blank under flow-v2). Verified: `launcher-api` build green. Remaining: Phase 3 = client-scoped default HUD layer.
- **Per-sheet editor-art namespacing** — editor-art regions are scoped `<assetKey>::<region>` so a new sheet no longer shadows an older same-named region.
- **Generic doc-driven scene mounting (Editor §20.1)** — an author's new Scene Editor screen (custom id, non-background space) ships in the game without being hardcoded in `Game.svelte`: `engine-layout/genericMountScenes.ts` `extraMountScenes(scenes, reservedIds)` renders them in doc order behind a parity-gated `{#each}` (empty reserved-set ⇒ byte-identical). FlowDoc-authored screens are folded into the reserved set to avoid double-mount.
- **Buy-feature (Select-Feature) screen — authored, data-driven** — the buy-bonus SELECT menu can render in-canvas from config instead of the HTML modal. A `featureCard` builtin component (`engine-layout/builtinComponents.ts`) is the per-card prefab (panel + icon + title/description/price/button, with the variable parts as `engineProvided` params + a `select` signal). A new `repeater` layout node (`engine-layout/types.ts` `RepeaterNode` + `Repeater.svelte`) stamps one component instance per item of a named data source, resolved through `registerRepeaterSources` (the list sibling of `registerComponentValues`), laid out row/grid; its `componentId` rides the bake/pull chain via `collectComponentIds`. `apps/lines` registers the `featureCards` source from `stateMeta.betModeMeta` (non-`default` bet modes), feeding each card its values + an `onSelect` that preserves the coded HTML `BonusCards` press contract (`stateBonus.selectedBetModeKey` + `buyBonusConfirm`). **Authorable online:** the Scene Editor has a Repeater palette element, an inspector (data source / component / row·grid·gap·columns) and a labelled canvas placeholder, round-tripped via `editorStorage`'s `NODE_KINDS`. **It is now the DEFAULT buy-bonus SELECT surface for every game — no flag, and the HTML select modal is DELETED** (`ModalBuyBonus`/`BonusCards`/`BonusCard` + the orphaned `BonusContentWrap*` are gone from `components-ui-html`; only the HTML CONFIRM step `ModalBuyBonusConfirm` remains, pending Phase 2). The shared, state-agnostic `<BuyFeatureScreen>` (`engine-layout/BuyFeatureScreen.svelte`; props `open`/`onDismiss`/`zIndex`/`scene`) mounts the scene as a canvas takeover; `defaultBuyFeatureScene()` (`engine-layout/buyFeatureScene.ts`) is the code-fallback, seeded into every reference layout (lines, `engineSkeleton` for ways/cluster/scatter, bookof), so a fresh project of any type ships an authorable page. The state wiring is `registerBuyFeature()` in `components-ui-html` (the home of `stateBonus`; `engine-layout` never imports `state-shared`) — it registers the `featureCard` def + the `featureCards` source; each app calls it once and mounts `<BuyFeatureScreen open={stateModal.modal?.name === 'buyBonus'} …>`, keying visibility DIRECTLY on the modal state (no redirect effect). A card press advances to `buyBonusConfirm`, a backdrop tap clears the modal. Card COUNT = number of non-default bet modes; per-card icon is the bet mode's editor-art key. **The CONFIRM step is also in-canvas now (Phase 2 done):** a generic, reusable `confirmDialog` component (`builtinComponents.ts` — panel + title + message + confirm/cancel buttons, all `engineProvided`; `confirm`/`cancel` signals) rendered by a shared state-agnostic `<ConfirmDialog>` (twin of `<BuyFeatureScreen>`), seeded as the `buyConfirm` scene. Two-button routing added `BaseNode.pressAction` + a component-press context (`componentActionsContext`) so N named buttons route to N callbacks — the generalization of Phase 1's single `onSelect`; a mount injects per-instance values + actions via `instanceBindingContext` (single-instance sibling of `<Repeater>`'s per-item props). `<BuyBonusConfirm>` (`components-ui-html`) supplies the buy wiring, preserving the deleted `ModalBuyBonusConfirm` contract exactly (`activeBetModeKey` → `bet` for buy / infinity limits for activate → close; cancel/backdrop → back to select). The HTML `ModalBuyBonusConfirm` is DELETED — the buy flow is fully in-canvas. **Deferred:** a bet-mode/dialog image URL doesn't preload/paint in pixi yet (binding is correct); Phase 3 = author the select→confirm→buy/cancel flow in the Flow editor (reusable for any confirm-gated action). Slices: #207 (repeater+card) + #208 (editor authoring + scene) + #214 (default/delete-HTML select) + Phase 2 (ConfirmDialog / delete-HTML confirm). **Per-card param overrides (branch `feat/per-card-param-overrides`, unmerged):** the config can now vary ANY param of the SHARED card component PER bet mode — `BetModePresentation.cardParams` (a generic `Record<string, string|number|boolean>`) rides `resolveBetModes` → `BetModeData.cardParams` → the repeater item's `values`. The core engine change is in `ComponentInstance.svelte`: the per-item value resolver's gate dropped from `param.engineProvided` to `param.key in engineValues`, so a per-card `panelImage`/`spineKey`/`buttonImage`/tint wins over the component's authored default while a param absent from the map keeps it (parity; the `<ConfirmDialog>` binding is unchanged since only its own engineProvided keys are `in` the map). Art/spine keys named in `cardParams` ride the ship chain via `betModeCardParamRefs` + `editorArtExport` (classified by the resolved card def's param kind; default `featureCard` seeded when a mode overrides without a custom card). `/config` gained a per-mode "Card graphics" editor (typed inputs for the card's authorable params). So one `featureCard` renders visually-distinct cards without a component per card. Verified: game-config typecheck, engine-layout/lines/launcher-api/cluster builds, an offline fixture (normalize round-trip + resolver precedence). Needs a runtime release to reach live games. **Buy-feature/confirm SCENE ROLES (branch `feat/buy-feature-scene-role`, unmerged):** `Scene.role` gained `'buyFeature'`/`'buyConfirm'` (`sceneRole.ts` + `buyFeatureSceneId`/`buyConfirmSceneId` helpers), so an owner can TAG any authored scene as the buy SELECT / CONFIRM screen — the old lookup was hardcoded id `buyFeature`/`buyConfirm`, which existing projects (editor mints random ids; those scenes are seeded only into fresh docs) never had, so a tagged feature-select scene fell through to role `basegame` and rendered as an always-on base-game overlay. `Game.svelte` now resolves the buy scenes ROLE→id→seeded-fallback (parity) and adds the role-resolved ids to `reservedSceneIds`, so a role-tagged scene mounts ONLY via its `<BuyFeatureScreen>`/`<BuyBonusConfirm>` takeover (gated on the buy modal) — never double-mounted by the generic overlay path. Editor Role dropdown + the `editorStorage` save whitelist gained both roles (the whitelist is load-bearing — without it the role is silently dropped on save). A `standard`-space role-tagged buy scene renders correctly (`<LayoutScene>` wraps it in `<MainContainer standard>`). Verified: engine-layout/lines/cluster/launcher-api builds + an 11-assertion offline fixture (role→id→default precedence + role-tagged scenes excluded from generic mount over the real modules). Needs a runtime release + the owner tagging their scene, then a live re-check.
- **Buy CHARGE follows the SELECTED mode's cost (branch `feat/fix-buy-cost-per-mode`)** — the in-canvas buy menu DISPLAYED each card's price correctly (`betAmount × mode.costMultiplier`, `registerBuyFeature`) but every card was CHARGED a fixed premium (100× = Gold Rush's cost) regardless of the tapped card. Root cause: the Play4Fun facade's book-path bet body collapsed all buy modes to a single `isBuy` boolean (`context: [isBuy ? 1 : 0, betPerLine]`, `packages/rgs-translator-eagaming/src/engineFacade.ts`), and the book mock (`scripts/mock-rgs-server-book.mjs`, which `services/test-server` imports = Borut's live RGS) hardcoded `total = betPerLine × NUM_LINES × (isBuy ? 100 : 1)` — so the cost multiplier the card showed never reached the charge. Fix: the engine publishes each mode's `costMultiplier` to `globalThis.__IE_BET_MODES__` from `syncBetModeMeta` (`apps/lines/src/game/betModeMeta.ts` — the decoupled-global bridge, mirror of `publishWinLevelsToFacade`, coupled to the buy MENU since both come from that one sync); the facade sends that per-mode multiplier as `context[0]` (0 = normal spin); the mock uses `context[0]` AS the cost multiplier (`total = betPerLine × NUM_LINES × cost`). So the debit now equals the card's displayed price (High Noon 25 / Bull Chase 50 / Gold Rush 100 at $1 bet). The imperative + flow buy paths share `commitBuyBonus`/the same bet, so both are fixed identically; a normal spin still sends `context[0] = 0` → 1× (parity). Verified: rgs-translator-eagaming typecheck + `apps/lines` build green; an offline fixture (`scripts/verify-buy-cost.mts`, run with tsx) drives the REAL book mock over HTTP (per-cost charge = 100/2500/5000/10000 cents) AND the REAL facade `requestBet` (context[0] = 25/50/100 per mode, 0 for BASE) — all 8 assertions pass. Live re-check: a real buy per card against the mock; the standalone `bookofborut` bundle needs an engine-submodule bump, the remake rides the shared runtime + a `services/test-server` deploy.
- **Win-line renderer in the SHARED engine** — ported from the standalone Book of Borut into `apps/lines` (`806d6cf`): `WinLine.svelte` (Graphics polyline, optional layered-stroke glow, optional `svelte/motion` animated draw, bitmap win-amount text), resolved from the sparse `bundle.symbols.winLine` (`bakedWinLineConfig()`/`bakedWinLineEnabled()`, runtime→baked precedence). Default-ON, so every `runtime:lines` game draws it unless disabled in `/symbols`. **Winning SYMBOLS keep animating on the resting board** (`apps/lines/src/game/winSymbolCycle.ts`, default ON): once a round's book is fully presented the game re-lights that spin's winning cells through the same `animateSymbols` leaf the round used, until the next bet stops it. Symbols ONLY — the line and its stamped amount are never replayed. Authored via the doc-level `winCycle` in `/symbols` (its own section, independent of the win-line toggle).
- **SPIN HOLD — a mid-round pause the spin button releases** (2026-08-17, off by default). New shared primitive in `state-shared` (`stateUi.spinHoldActive` + `armSpinHold`/`releaseSpinHold`/`cancelSpinHold`, read as `hasSpinHold()`): the INVERSE of `celebrationLock`/`unskippablePresentationActive`, which make the button inert — this makes it LIVE mid-round, keys it `spin_default`, and routes its press to release the hold instead of betting or slamming. All four decisions stay in the one chokepoint `utils-shared/spinStop` (`getSpinButtonKey` / `getSpinPressSound` — a hold-releasing press gets the bet whoosh, not the stop cue / `runSpinOrSlamStop` / the new `isSpinButtonSpinning`, which also DE-DUPLICATES the rolling-frame derivation that `ButtonBetProvider` and Game.svelte's parametric `spin` action had each written separately), so the Space hotkey, the flow `spin` action and an invoked intent inherit it for free. Its first consumer is the free-spin between-spins hold (`apps/lines/src/game/freeSpinHold.ts`, `winCycle.holdAfterBigWin` — **detail in `docs/status/symbols.md`**), but the primitive is game-agnostic: any presentation that wants to park a book on a player press can arm it. `playBet`'s `finally` cancels a stranded hold alongside the other belt-and-braces latch resets.
- **Book-of scatter is a pure trigger (no pay)** — the scatter/book match now ONLY triggers the free-spins feature; it no longer pays out (`cf1db93`). This is BOOK-OF-ONLY: the change lives in the book mock RGS (`scripts/mock-rgs-server-book.mjs`, which `services/test-server` imports), which emits the scatter win with `pay: 0` (kept as a zero-pay win so the scatter symbols still glow and the trigger count/positions still reach the client; `SCATTER_PAY` is now only the qualifying-count gate 3/4/5). Covers the buy-bonus first spin and natural base-game triggers; the retrigger path never credited the scatter pay. The plain `lines` mock (`scripts/mock-rgs-server.mjs`) is untouched and still pays scatters anywhere — it has no free-spin trigger. Paired info-bar note: on `freeSpinTrigger` the engine shows **"N Scatters award N Free Spins"** (count from `bookEvent.positions`, award from `bookEvent.totalFs`; `apps/lines/src/game/bookEventHandlerMap.ts`, `showMessage` info line). That handler is shared `apps/lines` code, so a hypothetical non-book game that emitted `freeSpinTrigger` would also show the message (harmless/accurate) — but the no-pay behaviour is strictly the book mock's. Verified locally by driving a buy round against the mock (scatter `pay=0`, `spinTrigger`/`enterBonus` still fire). Reaching the online Borut remake needs a runtime release + a `services/test-server` deploy.
- **Full-screen camera effects** — `shake` / `flash` / `zoomPunch` / `chromaticWobble`, authored from Flow as ONE `cameraEffect` action with a `kind` dropdown (adding a flourish must not grow the palette an entry each). Implemented engine-side in `pixi-svelte/src/lib/cameraEffects.ts` against the **`Application.stage`** — `<App>`'s children are flat z-banded siblings, so the stage is the only transform that moves board + HUD + overlays together (it moves `<DebugStage>` too; accepted). Effects **compose**: each contributes a per-frame sample and one compositor applies the sum, so overlapping effects can't fight over `stage.position`; the resting transform is captured up front and restored exactly when the last effect drains, and the ticker callback is removed at rest (zero idle cost). `intensity` is a **multiplier of a per-kind reference** (24px / 8% / 0.85 alpha / 12px), not pixels — capped at 4. `blocking` (default false) picks whether the interpreter awaits it. Kinds live in `constants-shared/camera` and are read by BOTH the vocab enum and the implementation, so the editor can't offer a kind the game would no-op. Off unless authored (no FlowDoc references it yet ⇒ byte-parity).
- **Free-spin sequential reel stop** — a timing-only `sequential` spin mode (reels settle consecutively in free spins, no anticipation glow/SFX). Off by default (byte-parity when unused); Flow-toggleable via `enableSequentialReelStop`/`disableSequentialReelStop` effects with per-reel `gaps`/`speeds` (`list<float>`). Engine plumbing added a `'sequential'` `SpinType` member across `utils-slots`. **TURBO WINS over it** (`createEnhanceBoardSpin`): sequential stop is pacing the feature imposes, turbo is the player asking for pacing to be dropped, so a held turbo takes the reel back to the global `fast` spin type instead of the free-spin mode silently overriding it.
- **Reel anticipation — client-computed MODE (Phase 2 of `docs/design/reel-anticipation.md`)** — the server-driven `anticipation[]` path is DELETED (the field on every game's `BookEventReveal`, the facade's `anticipation: reels.map(()=>0)` emit, the `createEnhanceBoardSpin` read, and the `Anticipation(s).svelte` server overlays + their `Game.svelte` mounts across `lines/ways/scatter/price/cluster`; story fixtures stripped of `anticipation:[…]`). Replaced by a CLIENT calculator: `createEnhanceBoardSpin.spin` takes an optional `computeArming(reelIndex) → {level, tier}|null`; armed reels drive the MIGRATED HOLD (the `anticipated` spinType → `reelPaddingMultiplierAnticipated`, unchanged) and set new `reelState.anticipationLevel`/`anticipationTier` (replacing `anticipating`; inert on cascading reels). `apps/lines/src/game/anticipation.ts` builds a `createLinesReach` from the FINAL board + config (paylines/paytable/wild/scatter/`numLines`, feature-trigger count from the scatter row's `occurs`, big thresholds via `gameConfig.activeBigTierThresholds()`) and arms on `stateGame.anticipationMode` (default OFF) with `anticipationConfidence` (`possible`=max bound / `guaranteed`=min) + `minAnticipateReel` (2). **OFF by default ⇒ byte-parity** (no policy ⇒ the whole block is skipped; the spin decision is identical to the old server-zero case — proven by a node reasoning check). **Sequential-reel-stop preserved**: with the server flag gone the sequential path's `!isAnticipated` is now `!isArmed`, which is `true` for every reel when the mode is off, so shipped sequential behaviour is byte-identical (turbo still wins). **SHIPPED 2026-08-05** (PR #229 → `main`, runtime-released to `_runtime/lines`; release.json `commit` = `94a35ce8`, `status: released`). Full stack landed: Phase 3 presentation (`Anticipation.svelte` per-reel overlay spine stack + `Anticipations.svelte` grey-out dim rects + `AnticipationCamera.svelte` zoom, escalating by `AnticipationTier` big→mega→massive via `anticipationPresentation.ts`); Phase 4 Flow authoring (`enableAnticipationMode`/`disableAnticipationMode` effects + `/flow-v2` palette in `bookOf.ts`, payload `confidence`/`minAnticipateReel`/`greyOut`/`zoom`, per-screen swappable); Phase 5 `/symbols` "Reel anticipation" panel authoring the per-tier FX + overlay spine through the full export→bake→pull→register chain, with the coded `ANTICIPATION_TIER_FX` kept as the un-authored fallback (`resolveTierFx`/`resolveAnticipationSpineKey` — the single choke point). Still OFF by default ⇒ byte-parity. Verified: builds green, reachability fixture + schema round-trip pass, code-reviewer clean, playtester confirmed arming math + overlay/grey-out mount in the Pixi scene graph — the animated hold (reels holding + zoom easing) still needs a foreground tab to eyeball (rAF frozen headless). Standalone `bookofborut` (own bundle) needs an engine-submodule bump to receive it; the remake rides the shared runtime. Design + full plan: `docs/design/reel-anticipation.md`. **Follow-up (branch `engine/anticipation-dynamic-tiers`, not yet shipped):** the hardcoded big/mega/massive tier triple is now GENERIC — the tiers MIRROR the config's big-win tiers. `AnticipationTier`/`ReelAnticipationArming.tier` widened to an opaque `string` alias; `gameConfig.activeBigTiers()` is the source (`winLevels` `type==='big'`, ascending, `{alias,name,threshold}`); arming stamps the reached tier's ALIAS; `ANTICIPATION_TIER_FX`+`tierForLevel`+`TIER_RANK` replaced by a coded RAMP `codedTierFx(rank,count)` interpolated across the configured big tiers; `resolveTierFx(alias)` merges the alias-keyed authored override over the ramp; `activeMaxTier()` returns the highest active reel's alias. Baked/schema `anticipation.tiers` is now an alias-keyed record; the `/symbols` panel renders one FX column per config big tier (via `resolveBigTiers()` in `+page.server.ts`). See `docs/status/symbols.md`.
- **Stacked-picture reel mode (LINES, visuals-only)** — the KA-Gaming "tall symbol" look: a contiguous vertical run of the same eligible symbol on a settled reel draws ONE tall picture (the symbol's new `stacked` state art, sprite/spine/flipbook) over the run, cropped to the top `runLength ÷ naturalHeight` and top-aligned. Per-symbol natural heights + eligible set in `constants.ts#STACKED_PICTURE` (H1=2…W=5). Client-inferred from the board (no protocol change). `stackedPictureRuns()`/`stackedCoverage()` `$derived` scans on `stateGame.svelte.ts`; `StackedPicture(s).svelte` render + rect-mask crop, mounted in the resting board container; `ReelSymbol` hides covered cells (no doubling). New `stacked` member in `engine-layout` `SYMBOL_STATES` (+ `LINES_SYMBOL_STATES` gates the `/symbols` column to lines); `getSymbolInfo` falls `stacked`→`static` unauthored. Flow-toggled via `enableStackedPictures`/`disableStackedPictures` (payload `symbols`/`highPayOnly`/`minRun`; `bookOf.ts` vocab). **OFF by default ⇒ byte-parity.** Test data: `scripts/mock-rgs-server.mjs` `STACKED=1` deal (high-symbol runs + full 5-tall WILD) + `WILD→'W'` in the lines facade. Verified: Node scan fixture 12/12 (owner example 3/4, WILD 3/5 & 5/5, 3×3/10×10 scale) + live Pixi scene structure (Container + stretched Sprite + rect mask). Follow-ups: real tall art + `/symbols` `stacked` authoring, wild-substitution runs, win-math, a real RGS emitting stacked boards. Design: `docs/design/stacked-picture-mode.md`.
- **Three-knob reel grid** — the conflated `reelPadding` was split into three orthogonal `ReelGridNode` knobs, all default-to-identity (byte-parity): reel/row **LEAD** (`reelPadding`/`rowPadding`, seats the cluster), seat **ALIGNMENT** (`symbolAlignX/Y`, art within its cell), board **NUDGE** (`boardNudgeX/Y`, px offset). Editor `drawReelGrid` mirrors the game math exactly (editor↔game < 1e-12 px).
- **Slam stop (mid-spin quick stop)** — ALWAYS ON, no config toggle. A press while a round runs snaps the reels to the already-resolved result AND fast-forwards the post-reveal presentation (count-ups jump to the final total, win lines draw complete, player-gated holds release) so the machine returns to idle promptly. The token is scoped to the **SPIN**: in `apps/lines` the re-arm hangs off `updateFreeSpin`, the FIRST event of every free spin (not the `reveal` — the counter update leads the spin it labels, so re-arming at the reveal left the next spin's counter presented under the previous spin's tripped token), so a press slams only the spin that is rolling and the feature then resumes at full pace — normal roll, normal anticipation, normal count-up — until the next press. (Base-game rounds have a single reveal, never re-arm, and keep the round-scoped behaviour. The other five dev games are still round-scoped: their bonus mechanics are cascades, and the owner's direction was about the Book-of feature.) During **autoplay** the first press slams the current spin only; a **second** press cancels the remaining sequence (autoplay is therefore always cancellable, in at most two presses). Built on a new `utils-shared/skipToken` primitive + the shared `utils-shared/spinStop` press/key helper (which de-duplicates the spin/stop logic that was copied verbatim into `ButtonBetProvider` and `Game.svelte`). Skipping never DROPS a beat: every book event still runs, fully awaited, and applies its effect at final value — only the time is removed. **Two presentations are carved out as UNSKIPPABLE** (`apps/lines/src/game/unskippablePresentation.ts`): the book / expanding-symbol reveal (`setExpandingSymbol`) and the free-spin intro (`freeSpinTrigger`) run to completion, because their visual is a spine/`.irig` timeline that a released wait leaves playing detached over the next spin. Their player-gated tap holds still release on a slam (a slam makes the spin button inert, and it swallows the tap). **A slam is not a blank:** a slammed spin that WON keeps a minimum win display — the lit win symbols and the per-win info message, ~600ms each — while the win line and the amount count-up stay fully skipped. **A slam NEVER skips a CELEBRATION** (owner direction 2026-07-30): the big-win overlay (`setWin` at a `big` win level), the free-spin **outro** (`freeSpinEnd`) and the **retrigger** flourish (`freeSpinRetrigger`) must play in full — a slam snaps the reels, it does not fast-forward the show. These are NOT added to the UNSKIPPABLE set (that keeps the token TRIPPED and only stretches cues, so it can't protect the outro's player-gated count-up or a `showContainer{awaitComplete}` hold, both released-on-skip to dodge the slam hang). Instead the slam token is **RE-ARMED right before a celebration** (`startsCelebration` in `unskippablePresentation.ts`, applied by `runBookEventPresentation` on the same seam as the per-spin re-arm), so it presents on a FRESH token — byte-identical to a round nobody slammed (cues wait in full, player-gated holds wait for the real tap, nothing can hang because the hang only exists while the token is tripped). ORDINARY wins keep fast-forwarding — only a `big`-level `setWin` re-arms, small/medium wins still slam. Covers coded, v1-flow and v2-flow (the seam wraps all three), so the flow-v2 Borut remake gets it too after a `runtime:lines` release. **The INTRO's scatter-match gap** (owner report 2026-07-30): the free-spin intro's scatter-symbols match animation plays at the START of `freeSpinTrigger`, BEFORE the intro screen mounts, so the screen-driven celebration lock is still open and the button is a live STOP — a slam there tripped the token and collapsed the intro's player-gated `freeSpinIntroUpdate` hold. Fixed two ways: (a) `freeSpinTrigger` was added to `startsCelebration` so the re-arm clears an UPSTREAM reel-roll slam; (b) `runBookEventPresentation` now mirrors its unskippable `depth` (0↔1 edges) into `stateUi.unskippablePresentationActive`, and `spinStop.isCelebrationLocked()` ORs `hasUnskippablePresentation()` — so the button is inert for the WHOLE unskippable window (scatter match + intro + book reveal), stopping a slam WITHIN the window. `playBet`'s `finally` force-clears the flag (belt-and-braces vs a stuck lock). Verified at token/flag/button level (game-playtester): re-arm clears an upstream trip, window-lock suppresses `roundSkip.skip()` while true, nested resume-path replay doesn't clear the outer window early, ordinary wins unaffected, idle flag false after round. **The press has its own SOUND:** a mid-round press broadcasts `soundPressStop` (`sfx_btn_stop`) instead of the bet whoosh — ⚠️ currently a STAND-IN region, see open item 5. **Celebration lock:** during a non-skippable celebration — free-spin **intro**, free-spin **outro**, or the **win panel** (WinGate = big-win presentation) — the spin button goes fully inert (it can't slam-skip the celebration); it renders as a greyed STOP. Read at the single chokepoint `utils-shared/spinStop` via `hasCelebrationOverlay()` (`getSpinButtonKey` → `stop_disabled` key; `runSpinOrSlamStop` early-returns as belt-and-braces for the flow `spin` action / Space hotkey / intent callers; `isSpinButtonDisabled` helper de-dupes the disabled check across `ButtonBetProvider`, the authored HUD `spin` action + Space hotkey in `Game.svelte`). ⚠️ **Signal source matters (learned the hard way, live-debugged on the remake):** the lock does NOT read the `stateUi.*Show` flags, and does NOT subscribe to the `*Show` EMITTER cues either. A **flow-v2** authored game (the Book-of-Borut remake) mounts its intro/outro via `showContainer` and NEVER broadcasts `freeSpinIntroShow`/`winShow` — confirmed by driving the live game: the intro screen was up for its full duration with every `*Show` flag false and no cue logged, so both a flag-read AND a cue-subscription were dead there. The lock is instead driven off the flow's **ACTIVE SCREENS**: `apps/lines` Game.svelte runs an `$effect` setting `stateUi.celebrationLock.{intro,outro,win}` from `activeScreenIds ⊇ {freeSpinIntro, freeSpinOutro, bigWin}` (the engine's canonical flow screen ids; `freeSpinIntro`/`freeSpinOutro` confirmed present live for the whole celebration). `hasCelebrationOverlay()` reads that latch. At the `spinStop` chokepoint the lock is `isCelebrationLocked() = hasCelebrationOverlay() || hasContinuePress()` — the `hasContinuePress()` term catches a big win presented as a HUD count-up + tap (no `bigWin` screen mounted on the remake), so its tap-to-continue phase also locks. The disabled button's canvas rect still swallows a tap in its footprint (Pixi `eventMode` stays `static`) — accepted small dead-zone. ⏳ Residual: a big-win **count-up before its tap** (no screen, `continuePress` not yet armed) stays slammable on a game with no `bigWin` screen — verify/tune if the remake shows a distinct big-win count-up.
- **Signal-subscription re-entrancy loop (fixed)** — a slammed free-spin **outro** could crash the reactive cycle with `effect_update_depth_exceeded` (Svelte 5), which aborts the update pass so downstream nodes freeze and the screen reads as broken. Root cause: `ComponentInstance.svelte`'s signal-subscription `$effect` subscribes to registered signal sources, and a source may EMIT SYNCHRONOUSLY on subscribe — the seed-on-subscribe latch (`freeSpinOutroCountUpComplete` fires `run()` immediately when `freeSpinOutroState.countUpComplete` is already true, the fix for the instant-count-up race). That emission runs the callback INSIDE the subscription effect, and `fireComponentSignal` READS + WRITES `firedSignals[key]` → the read leaks as a dependency, the write re-invalidates the effect, it re-subscribes/re-seeds/re-writes → infinite loop. It only surfaced on a SLAM, where the outro count-up completes in the SAME tick the outro-visual instance (tap armed on `freeSpinOutroCountUpComplete`) mounts + subscribes, so the latch is already set. Fix: wrap the subscription SETUP in `untrack(...)` (the effect has no legitimate reactive deps — `signalToTargets`/`gateSignals`/the registry are init-stable — so it should subscribe exactly once; mirrors `LayoutScene`'s tap-portal guard). Untrack suppresses dependency TRACKING, never the writes/notifications, so later emissions still fire cues + update `hiddenUntilSignal`/`tapArmed`. Reproduced deterministically headless (forced trigger + retrigger via the book mock, then Space-spam to slam the outro) and confirmed gone across 600+ slams / 24 free-spin rounds. Guard: `node packages/engine-layout/scripts/test-signal-subscribe-untrack.mjs`. Reaching the online Borut remake needs a `runtime:lines` release.
- **Live-asset pipeline** — a game asset only ships when it travels the full chain **export → `deploy/` → bake (embed index in bundle) → pull (mirror into `static/assets/`) → runtime register** (CLAUDE.md rule 8; `docs/design/live-assets.md`). Exported sheets are content-versioned (`sheetVersion` hashes region geometry + source-page ETag) so a re-packed atlas busts the cache automatically; exporters emit a `missing[]` dangling-binding guard. "Shows in the editor" ≠ "ships" — the editor reads R2 directly.
- **How the runtime ships** — an engine/runtime change reaches online games ONLY via a runtime-bundle publish, NOT a `main` merge. **Preferred: the `Runtime release` GitHub Action** (`.github/workflows/runtime-release.yml`) — a manual `workflow_dispatch` ("Run workflow", or `gh workflow run runtime-release.yml -f runtime=lines`) that does the whole chain with the R2 keys held in GitHub Actions secrets, so a release needs no keys on a dev machine. Game Maker's Publish CANNOT do it: `publishGame` only points a game's manifest at the prebuilt `_runtime/<id>` and "never rebuilds". The equivalent manual chain: rebuild the `engine-layout` **dist** first (stale-dist trap), then `PUBLIC_RGS_TRANSPORT=play4fun pnpm --filter lines build`, then `node apps/launcher-api/scripts/publish-runtime-bundle.mjs lines apps/lines/build`, then `POST https://games.invisiblewall.org/refresh`. Standalone games (desktop-launcher builds) get it from the `engine` submodule, which the launcher's publish advances to `origin/main` before every build — and since 2026-09-17 that covers the **whole** engine, `apps/lines/src` included, because a game repo no longer carries application source at all (see the Recent changes entry). There is nothing to mirror into a game repo any more.

⏳ Live-verify caveats still open: `SYMBOL_SPINE_FILL`'s live size judgement (0.5 tunable); the z-order of author extra-scenes (currently above the HUD) is a live tuning call; the win-line line-draw and sequential-stop timing were shipped + reachability-verified but not auto-verified in a live spin (owner confirms by forcing the win / triggering free spins).

- **Delivery profile — a build hosted by someone else (Phase 1 of [delivery-builds](../design/delivery-builds.md), 2026-09-14)** — `packages/delivery-profile` carries the two facts a build cannot know about itself when a partner/client/aggregator serves it from their own domain: which RGS it talks to, and how their page hands over a session. Resolution is later-wins: `DEFAULT_DELIVERY_PROFILE` (restates the pre-profile behaviour) ← the profile baked by `PUBLIC_DELIVERY_PROFILE` (injected as `__IE_DELIVERY_PROFILE__` by the SHARED `packages/config-vite`, so a game repo with the engine as a submodule inherits it) ← `config.json` served next to `index.html` ← URL params, and only while `rgs.allowUrlOverride`. Read by `stateUrlDerived.rgsUrl()`/`sessionID()` and by the Play4Fun transport (endpoint path + credentials mode); **awaited in `components-shared/Authenticate.svelte`**, not in an app's `+layout.ts`, because every app mounts `<Authenticate>` while only `apps/lines` has a layout `load` — a game repo scaffolded by `new-game.mjs` has none, so a delivery cut from one would have baked a profile whose `config.json` was never applied. **Inert without a baked profile** — no fetch, no log, no behaviour change — which is the parity guarantee for every game already live. Two decisions carry the weight: `rgs.withCredentials: false` for a delivery, because the `sid` is the credential and credentialed CORS forbids the wildcard origin that is the only answer scaling to an open-ended set of hosting domains; and `session.required`, which turns a missing token into an `ERR_NO_SESSION` modal instead of the minted `demo-<uuid>` — a phantom wallet taking real spins is worse than a game that plainly refuses to start. A broken profile fails the BUILD (`config-vite` shape-checks the baked JSON and throws) — the opposite posture from the runtime merge, which degrades, because nothing is running yet and a profile that merged down to internal defaults would ship a delivery pointing at no RGS. `config.json` may **repoint the build but not re-police it**: host/endpoint/session-param are overridable, the three security fields are bake-only. Verified: an offline fixture of 8 claims (`profile.fixture.ts`), a typo'd profile failing the build with a named field, and — served from a plain static host — a real delivery build resolving baked ← `config.json`, refusing a re-policing override, ignoring `?rgs_url=`, refusing to boot with no token (zero RGS calls), then authenticating on `?sid=`; plus the no-profile build re-checked in the same harness with **no `[delivery]` output, no `config.json` fetch** and `?sessionID=`/`?rgs_url=` behaving exactly as before.

## Open items / next

0. **Game-type templates — SHIPPED and live** (#355, #357; runtime `1f1639e`). A game type is now
   data + a shared runtime rather than an app fork. `packages/engine-game` holds the extracted
   engine layer (context / board state / config resolution inverted behind factories);
   `GameConfigDoc.winModel` (lines · ways · cluster · scatter) states how a game pays and is
   authored in `/config`; the engine reads it through `activeWinModel()` and adapts — payline
   colour and the payline diagram stand down off-lines, the paytable divisor follows the model
   (ways = the WAYS count, 243 on a 5×3), and anticipation stands down because its reachability is
   per-payline. `ways` ships a config default and a mock RGS protocol (a `winModel` option on the
   lines mock, not a fork).

   **Every game type shares ONE runtime bundle** (id `lines` — historical, it means "the shared
   engine runtime"). A per-type bundle would be built from `apps/<id>`, which are vanilla upstream
   and cannot consume a runtime bundle at all, so it would break the very project it was meant to
   serve. Revisit only for a type needing bespoke COMPILED code — `cluster`s tumble board is the
   first real candidate. Harnesses: `pnpm check:ways`, `game-config-spike run doc` / `run winmodel`,
   `pnpm check:path-imports`.

   **⚠️ Open:**

   1. **The client evaluates no win by design — the RGS decides and the engine presents.** All four
      models are now honoured end to end (#392 removed the boot warning that claimed otherwise; the
      stale "not honoured yet" wording that survived here until 2026-08-21 was wrong on both counts).
      What this costs is that the mock's evaluator must MIRROR the client's `payoutDivisor()`, and
      nothing checked it — see the 2026-08-21 entry below, and `pnpm check:rgs`, which is that check.
      Still true: **no ways or cluster project has been played end-to-end by a human**, only by the
      gates.
   2. `apps/ways` strips are **cosmetic, evenly weighted — NOT a math export**. Fine as a client
      default; not fine shipped for money.
   3. `cluster` still has empty-placeholder `paddingReels`, so it ships no config default.
   4. A game only ever ships an **authored** config, so setting a project game type without
      pressing Save in `/config` leaves it playing as lines. See [tools/game-config](../tools/game-config.md).

1. **Game-type templates — Phase A extraction: A0–A6 have all landed; what is left is ONE named
   module and it is Phase B's, not Phase A's.** Plan:
   [design/game-type-templates.md](../design/game-type-templates.md).
   `packages/engine-game` now holds the type-agnostic engine layer — 7 leaf modules, the context
   (inverted, not moved), `createGameState()`, `createGameConfig()`, `createSymbolMap()` /
   `createSymbolInfo()` + the symbol-state rule, the `constants` split and 10
   components. `apps/lines/src/game/stateGame.svelte.ts` is 75 lines of composition + re-export,
   `gameConfig.ts` is 45, `symbolMap.ts` is 30 and `utils.ts` is down from 448 to 292, with every
   call site untouched.
   `getPaylines`/`getNumLines`/`paylineColor`
   moved across unchanged so the relocation stayed provable, and Phase C/D then turned them into the
   win-model arms they were left in place for (item 0 above, which also owns what Phase D still
   owes).

   **What is genuinely left:** the PLAY PIPELINE — `playBookEvent` / `playBookEvents` / `playBet` /
   `convertTorResumableBet`, the only thing still in `apps/lines/src/game/utils.ts`. **Deliberately
   not moved, and the reason is not effort.** Its `dispatchBookEvent` reads book-event ARMS
   (`winInfo.totalWin`, `setWin.{amount,winLevel}`, `freeSpinRetrigger.extraFs`,
   `freeSpinTrigger.totalFs`), and TypeScript cannot discriminate a generic union — so a
   `createPlayBook(deps)` generic over the app's `BookEvent` would have to read those fields through
   casts, trading the precise typing the shipped game has today for a relocation. Declaring which
   book events the engine handles is Phase B's second half ("book events and presentation"), so the
   module moves when that contract is declared, not before. Two facts that back the line: **no
   component imports the play pipeline** (its only consumers are `actor.ts`,
   `bookEventHandlerMap.ts` and four stories), so it gates NOTHING in the component queue; and
   `scripts/verify-win-explode-pop.mjs` + `verify-swap-in-place-mode.mjs` assert against
   `utils.ts` as SOURCE TEXT — 854 checks that a factory rewrite would have had to be re-expressed
   for in the same change. **Re-scoped:** ~13 of the components still
   in `apps/lines` are lines/book mechanic components that should NEVER move, so a raw file count
   overstates the remainder. Reference scene sets for a type other than `lines`/`bookOf` are Phase E
   (a game type as an authoring KIND), not Phase A — they belong to [status/editor](editor.md) and
   [status/flow](flow.md), and subsume the old "reference layouts for `ways`/`cluster`/`scatter`"
   item.

   **The gate, and what it cost to build.** The fingerprint is exact only for **type-only** slices
   (a real file move reorders rollup's graph and reshuffles minified names), so relocation slices
   are verified with **`scripts/bundle-modules.mjs`** (sourcemap build → compare the module set +
   per-module content hashes); a string-literal comparison **cannot** work on this bundle (lodash
   regexes containing quotes desync any scanner, template literals embed renamed identifiers) —
   that was tried and deleted. A2 set the precedent that a slice changing runtime **wiring** (not
   just relocating code) gets booted against the mock RGS, not just module-set-checked. Two gotchas
   worth not rediscovering: a dependency scan pattern of `[a-zA-Z.]*` **silently excludes digits**,
   hiding every `flowV2*` import; and `sed -i` over a glob rewrites line endings in ALL matched
   files, which git normalizes away but the **sourcemap-based module check does not** — it reported
   27 phantom changed modules until the untouched files were restored.

   **Per-slice detail worth keeping.** **A1** — recomputed from the real import graph, `utils.ts`
   and `symbolMap.ts` are NOT leaves (they reach `bookEventHandlerMap`, `flowEffects`,
   `../editor-scenes`) and `constants.ts` was mixed, so all three needed splitting or a later slice.
   **A2** — the context could NOT be relocated, because four things in it are app-bound
   (`eventEmitter` is typed by a union assembled from the COMPONENTS, mechanic events included, and
   components rely on that precise typing; `stateApp` from this game's assets + baked editor art;
   `stateLayout` from this game's ratios/sizes; plus `stateGame`/`i18nDerived`). The keystone is a
   dependency **inversion** — `createGameContext()` owns the wiring, `apps/lines/src/game/context.ts`
   is a thin composition root — which is **Phase B's mechanic contract arriving early**, for state
   and events rather than the win model; Phase A therefore cannot fully precede Phase B, and the
   owner approved pulling B forward. **A3** — `getGameContext()` is typed by declaration merging
   (the app declares `interface GameContext extends LinesContext`, the only direction available
   since a package must never import from an app). Only **10** components moved, not the planned 44:
   that number counted whether a component mentions the MECHANIC and ignored what each IMPORTS, the
   set must be closed under sibling imports (`Win` looks clean but pulls `WinGate`/`WinVisual`), and
   `BoardFrame`/`Effects` import `../editor-scenes` while `TapToContinue` imports
   `flowV2InterpreterHolder`. **A3.5** — engine feel knobs (`SYMBOL_SIZE`, `SYMBOL_SPINE_FILL`,
   `REEL_PADDING`, `SYMBOL_DIM_TINT`, `INITIAL_SYMBOL_STATE`, `SPIN_OPTIONS_DEFAULT`/`FAST`) →
   package; this game's `STACKED_PICTURE` / `SYMBOL_INFO_MAP` / `SCATTER_LAND_SOUND_MAP` stay. The
   halves had no cross-references, so the cut was clean. **Hard constraint to preserve:** the app's
   `constants.ts` now has ZERO imports and must keep it that way —
   `apps/launcher-api/scripts/publish-symbol-defaults.mjs` imports it STANDALONE under Node
   type-stripping to read `SYMBOL_INFO_MAP`, and the `engine-game` barrel re-exports `.svelte`
   components that type-stripping cannot parse, so a value import from there would silently break
   the publish. Seven dead exports were deleted rather than moved (moving them would have created
   dead API in a new package): `MOTION_BLUR_VELOCITY`, `zIndexes`, `BACKGROUND_RATIO`,
   `PORTRAIT_BACKGROUND_RATIO`, the three `*_MAIN_SIZES` — note `FreeSpinAnimation.svelte` has a
   LOCAL `BACKGROUND_RATIO` with a different value, and the main sizes are duplicated as literals
   inside `stateLayout.ts`, which is what actually feeds the layout. **A4** — the board/reel
   machinery (lattice, spin profiles, anticipation, stacked runs, win-dim) is in the package; this
   game's grid/layout/sounds are injected as `deps`. **Correction worth keeping:** the earlier "~95%
   generic" figure counted MECHANIC mentions and does not predict extractability — the real coupling
   was six app deps plus `onSymbolLand`, which hardcodes this game's `'S'`/`'W'` ids and cue names
   (it stayed in the app). It did NOT unblock the component queue: of the 11 component importers,
   six import only `stateGame` itself, which package components already reach via `getGameContext()`,
   and most of those are book/expanding mechanic components that **stay in the app by design**. The
   reason to do it is that the board machinery is what a `ways`/`cluster` build would otherwise fork.
   Two implementation notes: types that depend on factory internals (`Reel`, `ReelSymbol`,
   `MultiplierSymbol`, `StackedPictureRun`) are **derived from the factory's return type** rather
   than re-declared, so they cannot drift from `buildBoard`; and the real risk was array identity,
   not typing — `enhancedBoard` closes over `stateGame.board` at module init and `rebuildBoard`
   splices rather than reassigns, so the factory must be called exactly once at app module scope.
   **A5** — the `runtime → baked → compiled` resolution, its memo, the win-tier ladder, the board
   grid and the config warnings all moved to `createGameConfig()`; only the two SOURCES are injected
   (`bakedGameConfig()` and the compiled `./config`). App file: 462 → 45 lines, 23 functions
   re-exported, 21 consumers untouched. Cleanest seam in Phase A — the file was already type-agnostic
   apart from its two inputs, and the `__IE_SERVER_CONFIG__`/`__IE_WIN_LEVELS__` globals needed
   nothing because they are deliberately decoupled bridges to the Play4Fun facade.
   **A6 — the symbol layer** (`symbolMap` + the symbol half of `utils`). **The blocker this slice
   was carrying was overstated, and measuring it is what made the slice small.** The plan said both
   modules needed `editor-scenes` AND `assets` seams; in fact neither imports `assets` at all, and
   of `editor-scenes`' 52 exports the symbol layer reads exactly ONE (`bakedSymbolMap`). So the seam
   is a single injected getter (`SymbolMapDeps.bakedMap`) beside the coded map A3.5 left in the app —
   not an inversion of `editor-scenes`, which would have coupled the engine to a game's baked bundle
   for nothing. Package-side: `createSymbolMap()` (the coded ⊕ baked merge, its memo, the generation
   counter, `resolveSymbolSizeRatios`), `createSymbolInfo()` (`getSymbolInfo` + its identity memo,
   `hasAuthoredSymbolState`, the two missing-art guards), `symbolCell.ts` moved across BYTE-IDENTICAL,
   and `hexToTintNumber` as a plain export. App-side: `symbolMap.ts` builds the map instance and
   `utils.ts` builds the resolver ON it — the resolver is constructed in `utils.ts` rather than next
   to the map precisely so the ten components importing `getSymbolInfo` from `../game/utils` keep the
   import they have. **Exactly ONE call site changed** (`SymbolWinFrame.svelte` now takes
   `hexToTintNumber` from `engine-game`, because re-exporting a pure function through the app would
   have been the compat shim the A1 rule forbids); the other twelve importers are untouched. Two
   dead things were deleted rather than moved: `getEmptyBoard` (no importer anywhere in the repo) and
   a `lodash` default import `utils.ts` had stopped using. **What the split risked, and what now
   guards it:** the map's merge memo and the resolver's cell memo used to be module-level state in
   two files that shared one generation counter — the thing that stops an online game rendering the
   coded template art forever after the runtime bundle lands. Across a package seam that coupling is
   now the `symbolMap` API object passed into `createSymbolInfo`, and it is asserted at RUNTIME by
   `packages/engine-game/src/game/symbolInfo.fixture.ts` (27 assertions, tsx — the reset case, memo
   identity, both missing-art fallbacks, the ratio precedence, and that two instances never share
   state). `constants.ts` still has ZERO imports (the A3.5 constraint holds — the cast that
   `SYMBOL_INFO_MAP` needs lives at the composition root, where it also closed a pre-existing
   `svelte-check` error: 194 → 193). Gate: 1730 → 1733 bundle modules, `symbolCell.ts` the same
   content hash at its new path, 4 modules rewritten, **1725 of 1730 byte-identical**.

7. **Perspective board mode — PHASES 0–4 SHIPPED to `main` (#407 `b81171cc`, #410 `80deba43`, #421 `eea3c773`, #415 `4a518067`); phase 5 (ship) unbuilt. STILL INERT — nothing authors it yet, so no online game is affected.** A converging, non-rectangular board whose symbols SWAP IN PLACE instead of rolling, driven by the "Knights of the Golden Label" concept art. The mechanic half already existed: the cascade (#375) and multiplier-collect (#377). **What is built:** `getSymbolSeat(reel, row) → {x, y, scale}` in `packages/engine-game/src/game/gameState.svelte.ts` is the ONE per-cell seat, asked by every per-cell call site; a `ReelGridPerspective` block on `ReelGridNode` (`farScale`, `vanishX` — **SHAPE ONLY**, see the behaviour note below) plus a sibling `tileRegion`, resolved by `resolveReelGridPerspective` / `resolveReelGridTileArt` beside the `spin`/`anticipation` resolvers; the model (row scale ramping `farScale`→1, columns contracting toward `vanishX`, y as the running sum of compressed pitches); `SymbolWrap.scale`; `boardWindowHeight()` as the ONE definition of the visible board height; back-to-front draw order; the `swapInPlace` mode switch (`presentReveal`, shared by the coded handler AND the flow-v2 `revealBoard` effect, branches to a swap presentation through the tumble overlay, and the four reel-shaped behaviours stand down through the off-path each already had); **three swap STYLES** — `dropIn` (the whole board falls at once), `columnCascade` (#425: the resting board drains column by column, left to right, each column refilling as it empties, via a new `tumbleBoardDrain` cue and a PER-COLUMN scoped `tumbleBoardInit`, with `columnStaggerMs` as the ONE knob covering both readings of "left to right" — short ⇒ a wave, longer than a column ⇒ strictly sequential, `0` ⇒ all at once), and `emerge` (2026-08-27: NOTHING travels — each symbol is placed on its own seat at `duration: 0` and plays a new authored `intro` symbol state there, so the arrival ANIMATION is the whole presentation; `columnStaggerMs` sweeps it, defaulting to `0` where the cascade defaults to 140 ms. It is the one style that answers “the symbols rise out of the water”, and it is NOT reachable by shortening a fall — a fall that lands in 1 ms is still a fall, and its `land` beat still fires AFTER the movement rather than instead of it. Full story in [game-config.md](game-config.md) and [symbols.md](symbols.md)); a `clearBoard` opener for the drop-in (every visible cell plays its authored `explosion` state and leaves first, reusing the two cascade steps the drop-in omits — no new cues); the editor mirror in `reelGridGeometry()` + a "Perspective (advanced)" properties section; and ground TILES stamped from the lattice with a per-tile win highlight. **Seven traps found, all now load-bearing knowledge:** (a) **flat must be an EARLY RETURN, not a `scale` of 1** — `vanishX + (getSymbolX(reel) − vanishX) * 1` subtracts and adds back, which ROUNDS (63.6 → 63.599999999999994); (b) **`props.row` indexes the PADDED strip** while `getSymbolY`'s domain is the lattice row (the reel homes at `−pitch`, so strip _i_ rests at `getSymbolY(i−1)`); (c) **`scale = 1` is not a no-op in Pixi 8** — see [[gotcha_pixi_identity_prop_is_not_a_noop]]; (d) **`swapInPlace` is INDEPENDENT of `farScale`** and must NOT be gated on `boardPerspective()` (a flat board that swaps is a legal authored config; gating them together makes it silently do nothing — there is a regression test); (d2) **the board's BEHAVIOUR does not belong on the `reelGrid` node at all** — 2026-08-21, owner call. A `reelGrid` node is authored PER `layoutType`, so `swapInPlace` / `swapStyle` / `columnStaggerMs` living there let the schema express a board that ROLLED IN PORTRAIT AND SWAPPED IN LANDSCAPE, or swept at two speeds depending on the phone. All three moved to the Invisible Game Config's `reelBehaviour` block, read through a new `deps.reelBehaviour` on `createGameState` and authored in `/config` → **Reel behaviour**; the perspective block is shape only now. Nothing had authored them, so there was nothing to migrate — but the fields are asserted GONE from both the type and the resolver, because `normalizeNode` is pass-through and would carry a stale one out of an old doc forever. Full story in [game-config.md](game-config.md); (e) **the design's drop-in sequence had a hole** — "the cascade minus the explode step" leaves the old board in the survivor layer with nothing to filter it out, so the combined column is twice as tall and `land` fires on the off-screen half; hence `tumbleBoardInit.keepBase`, and "no survivors" is one EMPTY COLUMN PER REEL, not an empty array; (f) **the editor's `cellW/2` vs the game's `cellSize/2` is NOT a divergence** — it is cancelled exactly by `boardLayout()`'s pivot recentring (`((reels−1)/2)·columnExtraLocal`), and the Y twin by `(rows/2)·(rowPitchLocal − SYMBOL_SIZE)` against the game's `gapY/2` origin; both terms are load-bearing; (g) **RULE 8's broken link was `collectArtRefs`** — a per-node-kind whitelist (sprite/spine/componentInstance only), so a tile would look perfect in the editor and ship as a MISSING FRAME. Fixed via `addImageRef`; the other four links are free because the tile rides the existing `editorArt` sheets. **Verification is offline and mutation-tested throughout** (the validate-the-contract-offline discipline; every fixture slices the REAL shipped source so it cannot rot): `scripts/verify-symbol-seat.mjs` (265905), `verify-swap-in-place-mode.mjs` (**533**, on a virtual clock — sweep order, drain-before-refill per column, the stagger actually changing the overlap, the clear step, the tile layer never blinking out, and — part 9 — the emerge style proved to move NOTHING by a ledger of every `Tween.set` duration the reveal asks for, with the shipped drop-in in the same run as the control that shows the ledger can tell travel apart), `verify-reel-grid-geometry.mjs` (307580 — asserts the editor seat EQUALS the engine seat through an affine map derived from `boardLayout()`'s own pivot, worst relative error 8.79e-14), `verify-board-tiles.mjs` (58638), `verify-reelgrid-perspective-roundtrip.mjs` (13). What they do NOT cover: **nothing renders** — no phase has been verified in a browser — and only event ORDER is tested, never timing. **Phase 5 (ship) — DONE for the ONLINE games, 2026-08-21.** It needs no manual step any more: `.github/workflows/runtime-release.yml` auto-fires on any push to `main` touching `apps/lines/**` or `packages/**`, so an engine merge publishes `test_server/_runtime/lines` and pokes the test server itself. Released at `dcaaf84` (#435) — 163 files / 57.6 MB, `release.json` stamped `status='released'`, refresh took on attempt 7 after the usual 502 flapping while the server re-hydrates. **Verified in the LIVE bundle, not just from a green tick**: `games.invisiblewall.org/bookofborutremake/_app/immutable/bundle.*.js` contains `reelBehaviour` (a string only this merge introduces — #425 used `swapStyle`/`columnStaggerMs`), and the game boots (Pixi 8.8.1 webgl2, canvas up, 9 stage children, no crash; the `?k=`-less fallback-layout warning is expected when loading the game URL directly). **Still owed:** a runtime release + reconcile for the `emerge` style (merged 2026-08-27 — the auto-release fires on any `main` push touching `apps/lines/**`, so this lands with the merge), and the `engine` submodule bump for STANDALONE Book of Borut — the auto-release does not reach it. Nothing else to deliver until a game authors the mode. **Open decisions for the owner:** (1) **per-`layoutType` overrides** — the design said decide "before phase 1 rather than retrofitting" and we are now past phase 4; `spin`/`anticipation` are not per-ratio so `perspective` follows them as a plain node-level block, and retrofitting after tiles are stamped against the lattice is the expensive version; (2) ~~tiles unmount during a cascade~~ **CLOSED by #425** — the overlay now draws the same ground layer, so it no longer blinks out for the length of a swap. The double-draw guard OBSERVES the reel board's own `boardShow`/`boardHide` rather than assuming the two cues flip in one Svelte batch, because both are authorable Broadcast cues and a doc CAN hold the overlay and the reels on screen together (two coplanar tile layers with alpha would darken the ground); the fixture exercises that case directly; (3) **`symbolAlignY = 1` (feet-on-tile) moves the TILE down with the character**, because the seat folds the alignment — pinning the tile to the cell BOX while the art rides the alignment would be a second, independent knob. Owner decisions (2026-08-20): no roll; the per-symbol win dim (`SYMBOL_DIM_TINT` through `SymbolWrap`) is enough since it touches no geometry — a DIFFERENT feature from the anticipation grey-out, which stands down with the roll. Perspective SPINNING reels are explicitly out of scope. Plan: `docs/design/perspective-board-mode.md`.
8. **Stepped grids — SHIPPED to `main` (#445 `553848a4`) and runtime-released, 2026-08-24. A board whose columns are different heights (`3/4/5/4/3` diamond, `2/3/4/5/6` ramp).** `numRows` has been a PER-REEL array since the config schema was written and its doc comment always claimed "so a stepped grid is expressible", but only the MATH ever read it that way (`activeWaysCount`, the per-reel payline bounds check, `gameProfile`'s `boardText`) — every renderer and dealer collapsed it to `Math.max(...)`. So a non-uniform config **saved, validated and shipped** while the board drew a rectangle against it: the paytable pricing 720 ways over a 5×5 board, the RGS dealing five rows into a three-row column — the client board diverging from the scored board, reachable by typing a number into `/config`. The feature is mostly about REMOVING that disagreement. **What is built:** `resolveGrid` (`packages/game-config/src/grid.ts`) is the ONE resolver both halves read — `rowsForReel` + `rowOffsetForReel` (in rows, FRACTIONAL, so a 4-row column centred in a 5-row box lands on the half-cell stagger that makes a diamond read as a diamond); a new optional `gridAlign` (`center`|`top`|`bottom`) stored only when it departs from the default AND the grid is actually stepped; per-column clip windows (`ReelColumn`) + a per-column cull in `SymbolWrap`; the offset applied on ONE axis but TWICE (into the reel's `symbolLead` so it travels while rolling, and into the resting seat via `rowSeatIndex`) because `ReelSymbol` picks between those two y sources every frame; ragged dealing end to end (`/config` → `mockContract` → manifest → test-server → mock, each hop sending `rowsPerReel` only when the columns differ), with the mock DECLARING what it dealt (`config.window.rowsPerReel`) and the facade clamping PER COLUMN against that declaration; `standardPaylines`/`coversAllRows` taught per-column heights (a board-wide coverage test can never be satisfied by a stepped grid, so the server would regenerate lines forever); the editor preview seating each column from the SAME `resolveGrid` output; `ROWS=3,4,5,4,3` on the mock CLI. **`stepped` is the parity gate** — false for every board that exists, and every consumer early-returns its EXISTING path on it, not an equivalent one. **Traps:** (a) a short column is pushed DOWN, so its padding row lands INSIDE the board-wide window — culled against the board it would be DRAWN, a phantom symbol above a short column on the unmasked animate layer; hence the per-column cull, which is correctness, not precision; (b) the rolling y and the resting seat have NEVER been bit-equal (a uniform board already drifts ~2.8e-14 between them today, because the two expressions associate differently), so that one assertion is a 1e-9 TOLERANCE while everything else in these fixtures is `Object.is` — the failure it guards misplaces a column by half a row, fifteen orders of magnitude clear of the noise; (c) the stepped-vs-PERSPECTIVE conflict CANNOT be checked in the config validator — perspective lives on the reelGrid NODE, not in the config doc, so a check there would silently never fire; it is in `reelGridWarnings`, the one surface that sees both. **Verification:** `scripts/verify-stepped-grid.mjs` (229, new — including `clampBoardToGrid` SLICED from the real facade) + a stepped section in `verify-symbol-seat.mjs` (now 274,644), negative-tested. The two load-bearing parity assertions: the same seed dealt with `rows: 3` and `rows: [3,3,3,3,3]` gives BYTE-IDENTICAL responses (the RNG stream is untouched — otherwise every existing game's deal would shift the day this shipped), and a uniform seat is still literally `getSymbolX`/`getSymbolY`. **Verified live** in `apps/lines` + mock by reading the Pixi scene graph directly (the browser pane would not composite, so a screenshot would have proved less): a 3/4/5/4/3 config draws five masked column containers holding 3/4/5/4/3 symbols at row offsets 1/0.5/0/0.5/1; reverting to 5×3 returns ONE mask on the board container with all 15 symbol containers as its direct children at y 60/180/300 — the flat structure unchanged, not merely equivalent. **Cascade + stepped WORKS**, and the first pass was wrong about why it might not: the tumble overlay does NOT seat falling replacements against the board's row count — every cascade seat already goes through `getSymbolSeat(reelIndex, …)`, a drain drops by the COLUMN's own length, `combineTumbleReel` is length-agnostic, and the mock refills exactly what it removed per reel. What WAS board-wide is the same pair the reel board fixed: the CLIP (the resting cascade layer is clipped by the board-wide `BoardMask`, so a short column's replacements — stacked deliberately ABOVE its window waiting to fall — sit inside the bounding box and would be DRAWN hanging above it, and its drained symbols would park below it instead of leaving) and the CULL (`TumbleSymbol` handed `SymbolWrap` no `reelIndex`). `TumbleBoardBase` now wraps its columns in the same `ReelColumn`; grouping by COLUMN is safe where grouping by ROW is not, because a symbol never changes column mid-cascade, so the object keying that keeps a falling symbol's `symbolY` Tween alive across `tumbleBoardRemoveExploded` is untouched. `cascadeBoard.fixture.ts` now drives a ramp (2/3/4/5/6) and a diamond (3/4/5/4/3) beside its original rectangle — 937 assertions over 291 tumble steps, real mock + real facade + real board rule. **Perspective + stepped COMPOSES**, and the fix was to change how a stepped board is CLIPPED rather than to reconcile two paint orders. The first design wrapped each column in its own container + mask, which forces a COLUMN-major scene graph while perspective paints ROW-major (so a front-row character covers the row behind it) — mutually exclusive. The clip is now ONE compound mask whose geometry is the UNION of the per-column windows (`boardMaskColumns`), needing no grouping at all: the child list stays flat, both `BoardBase` branches are untouched, `ReelColumn` is deleted, and `BoardBase`/`TumbleBoardBase` are byte-identical to `main` again. Two traps: the columns must TILE rather than overlap (an overlapping polygon lets a tall neighbour cover the notch beside a short column, and a symbol scrolling through it would be drawn where the board does not exist); and under perspective each column must be sampled at EVERY ONE OF ITS ROW BOUNDARIES rather than drawn as a four-corner trapezoid — a trapezoid interpolates its edges linearly in Y while the contraction is linear in the ROW and y is a quadratic sum of compressed pitches, and the two disagree enough mid-column that a cell's centre lands inside its NEIGHBOUR's polygon. Both were caught by the fixture, not foreseen; `verify-symbol-seat.mjs` now point-in-polygon-tests the mask across 3 perspective settings x 4 stepped shapes x 15 grids, probing from the SEAT path rather than from the polygon's own vertices (the first version probed the ring and a deliberate one-pitch shift of every interior boundary sailed through it). **Verified live and quantitatively** via `renderer.extract` pixel readback: a 3/4/5/4/3 config paints column spans of 2.98/3.95/5.00/3.94/2.92 rows at offsets 1.03/0.54/0/0.63/1.10 — the diamond with its half-row stagger — while a reverted 5x3 puts the mask back on the `Rectangle` path (`fill`+`stroke`, not the compound `fill`) with 15 symbols at y 60/180/300. **WAYS on a stepped board — a real bug, found by closing the gap note rather than by trusting it.** The note said the ways reach was "correct by construction" since `createWaysReach` reads each column's height off the board it is handed. True — but the board it was HANDED was wrong: `buildAnticipationArming` sliced the padded reveal to the BOUNDING BOX (`reel.slice(1, 1 + y)` with `y = max(numRows)`), so a 3-row column (5 padded cells) kept its bottom PADDING row — four cells in a three-cell column. Nothing downstream could detect it: a ways pay is a product over the per-reel counts DIVIDED by the ways count and both move together, so the round silently pays the wrong multiple (3x4x5x4x3 = 720 ways against 3125 if every column is counted as five), and for `lines` an off-screen symbol can complete a run. Fixed to slice by `grid.rowsForReel(reelIndex)` — the identical number on a uniform board. `anticipationWaysReach.fixture.ts` gained a ragged-board section: bounds monotone, converging on the real win, plus the load-bearing one — padding the short columns out to the bounding box provably CHANGES the win, so the slice cannot regress silently. The rest of the family was already safe and is worth recording as such: `bookEventHandlerMap` and `flowEffects` walk the visible rows behind a `row < symbols.length - 1` guard, which bounds them by the column's own strip rather than by the board. **Open:** no full ROUND has been driven in a browser (the pane will not composite, which throttles the animation clock so a round never settles and a second spin never arms — a single spin does fire on spacebar), so the cascade is covered by the fixture plus SOURCE-level assertions that the per-column window is wired into both symbol renderers (`verify-stepped-grid.mjs` §6). **Released to the online games**: the auto `Runtime release` workflow fired on the merge and went green (build → publish `_runtime/lines` → refresh the test server). Verified in the LIVE bundle rather than from the green tick — `games.invisiblewall.org/bookofborutremake/_app/immutable/bundle.CENqRIJc.js` contains `gridAlign`, `rowsPerReel` and `rowOffsetForReel`, three markers only this merge introduces. And verified INERT there, which is the parity claim that actually protects a live game: the remake's board draws 15 symbols at y 60/180/300 behind a mask whose instructions are `fill`+`stroke` (the untouched `Rectangle` path, NOT the compound `poly`+`fill`) at 840x360 — the same geometry as before the merge. (Loading that URL bare still shows the RGS handshake error; it carries no `sessionID`/`rgs_url`, which is the documented behaviour for a direct load and unrelated.) **Still owed:** the `engine` submodule bump for STANDALONE Book of Borut — the auto-release does not reach it. Plan: `docs/design/stepped-grid.md`.

## Blocked (owner / external)

- **Shipped-game submodule bumps** — Book of Borut (and other shipped games) bump their engine submodule + republish on the owner's cadence; per-game-engine branches are owner-merged.
- **Live-verify of shipped runtime changes** — win-line draw, sequential stop timing, spine symbol size, and author extra-scene z-order need an in-browser confirmation the headless build can't give.

## Recent changes

- 2026-09-21 — **The real cause of "my art and config never load": the assemble is slower than the
  client will wait.** The 2026-09-18 entry below fixed a real bug but not this one — the owner still
  saw it, and the console said why: `retry 1 in 1000ms (30s of budget left)` then `gave up after
  90000ms and 2 attempts — last: signal timed out`. The **first attempt burned the whole 60s cap**,
  so this was never the fast 502 of a container swap; `/api/editor/runtime` was simply answering
  slower than the cap. Measured on `test6`: **83.6s / 97.5s / 92.8s** over three consecutive
  fetches, every one a healthy 200 with a complete 193 KB bundle. The budget (90s) was smaller than
  one assemble, so the game could only ever load when it caught a cached ~21s run — which is exactly
  what made it look intermittent. Cap → **150s**, budget → **210s**, `app.html` splash net → 240s.
  These are a symptom of exporting on the read path; see game-maker open item 6, now the top
  priority, which carries the measurements.
  - **The half-state was the reason it read as "missing art" rather than "failed fetch".** When the
    bundle times out, boot falls through to `/api/editor/doc` — which is CHEAP and succeeds — so the
    game renders the project's REAL layout with none of its assets: art, component defs, symbols,
    fonts and the authored config all ship exclusively in the runtime bundle, and `Game.svelte`
    gates the asset re-merge + `resetGameConfigCache()` on `isRuntimeBundleActive()`. `test6` drew
    its real 16-scene layout while logging `Sprite: key "invisible_wall/test6/…::TitleConcept" is
    not found in loadedAssets`, `no component registered for id 'c_…'`, and a `symbols.W` warning
    from the **template** config — with no stale flag and no overlay, because that branch counted as
    success. A doc is not a game: it now marks stale + shows the dead-end overlay, and logs one
    `LAYOUT-ONLY BOOT` line naming every missing class so the asset errors below it are legible as
    consequences rather than causes.
  - A retry after a TIMEOUT now says "Still loading your project…" rather than "Reconnecting to the
    launcher…" — the launcher IS answering, just slowly, and the two waits should not look alike.
  - Verified against the live launcher and the real `test6` project: the bundle lands
    (`live runtime bundle ready for "test6"`), `__IE_RUNTIME_STALE__` is unset, no overlay, the
    project's own art renders, and **neither** `not found in loadedAssets` nor `no component
    registered` appears — the two errors that flooded the failing boot.

- 2026-09-18 — **A runtime release no longer drops the online games onto the engine's sample game.**
  Reported as "sometimes when I run my runtime release all the project art is missing, the game shows
  defaults, and no config loads". The release was never the cause. The launcher deliberately has NO
  Railway watch paths (`docs/INFRA.md`), so every push that triggers a runtime release —
  `apps/lines/**` or `packages/**` — also redeploys it, and the launcher is the slower of the two: on
  `13571da2` the release went green at 12:48:47Z and the launcher only answered at 12:50:31Z. Reload
  the game inside that **104s window** and `/api/editor/runtime` answers 502/refused,
  `prepareRuntimeBundle` returns false, and boot falls through. Everything project-specific rides that
  ONE fetch, so it is all-or-nothing — art, symbols, fonts, flow, win text AND config vanish together
  — and for the shared runtime there is nothing underneath: `_runtime/<id>` ships an EMPTY
  `baked-editor-bundle.json`, so `hasBakedDoc()` is false and the game lands on
  `fallbackEditorScenes` + the compiled-in `config.ts`.
  - **The retry ladder was the bug.** `RUNTIME_RETRY_DELAYS_MS = [1_000, 3_000]` — three attempts —
    against a failure that answers INSTANTLY, so the game gave up ~4s into a 90s budget it never
    touched. (Note the asymmetry: `runtime-release.yml`'s own `/refresh` step retries over 4.5 min for
    exactly this reason.) Now exponential — 1s, 2s, 4s, 8s, then 15s apiece until the budget is spent
    — measured at **9 attempts over 75s**, which rides the container swap out. The splash reads
    "Reconnecting to the launcher…" on the first retry instead of looking hung, and `app.html`'s
    safety net went 60s → 120s so it cannot clear mid-retry and re-expose the black shell.
  - **The dead-end fallback is no longer silent.** A `?runtime=1` boot that ends on
    `fallbackEditorScenes` is never correct — nothing authored is behind it, so the player is looking
    at a DIFFERENT game, which is precisely why a routine launcher restart read as "the release
    deleted my art". `markRuntimeStale(reason, deadEnd)` now shows EVERYONE a full-screen "This game
    could not load" overlay with a Reload button; `ie_authoring=1` adds the technical reason. The
    ordinary stale-but-authored case (a real baked doc is on screen) keeps the authors-only strip
    unchanged. `runtimeModeEnabled()` is the gate rather than "no baked doc", because a dev/Storybook
    boot also has no baked doc and uses that fallback legitimately — verified: flag set, no overlay.
  - **Not fixed here — the durable fix is infra.** Set a Railway healthcheck on the launcher
