    (`/api/health` exists and is dependency-free) so the old container keeps serving until the new one
    is ready. That closes the window instead of tolerating it; this change only makes the game survive
    it and say so when it can't.
- 2026-09-18 — **every slice-compiling guard now goes through the shared wrapper, so a drifted slice
  names itself instead of dying at `<anonymous_script>:N`.** `verify-board-tiles` adopted
  `scripts/lib/compile-slice.mjs` when it was written; the other **twelve** still called `new Function`
  directly, and **four of them carried their own private copy of the `stripTypeScriptTypes` wrapper**
  (`verify-swap-in-place-mode`, `verify-win-explode-pop`, `verify-test-server-project-pin`,
  `verify-tumble-pattern`). All twelve are converted — 29 compile sites, and the four private copies are
  a net DELETION rather than an addition. **The two fixtures whose stripper does more than strip keep
  their extra step and lose only the duplicated half:** `verify-win-explode-pop` and
  `verify-swap-in-place-mode` reduce runes to their out-of-component value semantics AFTER stripping,
  which is theirs and not the shared wrapper's business; what they no longer each own is Node's stripper,
  its experimental-warning silencer and the attributed failure. `verify-boot-splash` is the one that
  takes `compileSlice` WITHOUT `stripSliceTypes` — `boot-splash.js` ships as JavaScript, so there is
  nothing to strip and only the attribution is worth having.
  - **Proved behaviour-neutral by transcript, not by a green tick.** Every guard's full stdout was
    captured before the change and diffed after: **eleven of twelve byte-identical**, and the twelfth
    (`verify-tumble-pattern`) differs by exactly the two lines of
    `ExperimentalWarning: stripTypeScriptTypes` that the shared wrapper silences — the improvement
    itself. Assertion counts unmoved. Re-diffed a second time after Prettier, because a formatter pass
    over twelve files is its own chance to move something.
  - **Mutation-tested against the bug the wrapper exists for**, old code vs new, same mutation: a
    `Map<string, ReadonlyArray<number>>` local added inside the real `resolveReelGridFromNode` — an
    annotation shape `verify-symbol-overflow`'s old regex set (`: number` / ` as number`) had never
    heard of. **Old: dies before the first assertion. New: 4045 assertions, PASS.** That is case 1 from
    `compile-slice.mjs`'s header reproduced on a second file, which is the claim the rollout rests on.
    A separate anchor-drift mutation was caught EARLIER, by the slice anchor's own `could not locate`
    throw — worth recording, because it shows the two layers do not overlap and neither is redundant.
  - **Not done, and it is the bigger hole:** *none* of the 21 `verify-*` guards run in CI. `lint.yml`
    runs `pnpm lint`, `check:undefined-names`, `gen-flow-vocabulary --check` and `check:rgs`; the
    pre-commit hook runs the secret scanner plus two generator `--check` passes. So a regression in any
    of these only surfaces when a person happens to run one — which is exactly how `verify-board-tiles`
    sat red on `main` for a week and how `verify-swap-in-place-mode` died silently at part 9 three
    separate times. Better attribution shortens the diagnosis; it does not shorten the week. One
    aggregate script wired into `lint.yml` would, and it is independent of this change.

- 2026-09-18 — **`verify-board-tiles.mjs` is green again, and it no longer strips TypeScript with regexes
  of its own.** It had been red on `main` since #734 — `SyntaxError: Unexpected token ':'` out of
  `new Function` at part 5, the rule-8 ship chain. #734 gave `collectArtRefs`'s inner `visit` a SECOND,
  OPTIONAL parameter (`(node: LayoutNode, ownerDef?: ComponentDef): void =>`), and the fixture's own
  annotation-stripping knew one shape: one parameter, no `?`. The annotation survived into the compiled
  text and the file died before part 5 ran a single assertion, while parts 1-4 stayed green and the run
  never printed its count line.
  - **The repair is to stop hand-writing the stripper.** `sliceFunction` now cuts the WHOLE declaration
    — parameter list included, types and all — and hands it to Node's own `stripTypeScriptTypes`
    (`mode: 'strip'`, so every line and column still points at real source), which is the technique
    `verify-win-explode-pop` / `verify-swap-in-place-mode` / `verify-tumble-pattern` /
    `verify-test-server-project-pin` already use. The two guessed annotation names on the
    `gameState.svelte.ts` seat block (`: number`, `: BoardPerspective`) went the same way: a third one
    added upstream is the identical trap, one part over. "The slice's shape moved" stops being a class of
    bug rather than being repaired one annotation at a time — the same lesson as the stub-set guard
    below, and reached the same way.
  - **The recurrence, not the instance: `scripts/lib/compile-slice.mjs`.** `compileSlice({what, names,
    body})` wraps `new Function` so a parse failure reports the FIXTURE, the SLICE and the parser's own
    code frame instead of `<anonymous_script>:54` — a line inside a string nobody can open, naming no
    file and no function. `new Function` will not give that frame up (its SyntaxError carries
    `at new Function (<anonymous>)` and no position; the `<anonymous_script>:54` V8 prints for an
    UNCAUGHT one goes to stderr and never reaches `error.stack`), so the same text is recompiled through
    `vm.compileFunction` under a `filename` naming the slice, purely to harvest it. `stripSliceTypes`
    lives beside it and refuses a slice that is not strippable TypeScript, naming it. Both are wired
    through all five of this fixture's compile sites. **This is deliberately NOT what
    `scripts/lib/stub-set-guard.mjs` covers, and wiring one does not get you the other:** that guard
    type-checks the body as TypeScript, so leftover TypeScript passes it clean and then throws at
    `new Function`; a SyntaxError is not a missing name and will never appear in `tsc`'s undefined-name
    codes.
  - **`staticSpineKeyIsReachable` was free in the slice** — #734 routes the spine branch through it and
    nothing provided it, so the first case to place a spine node would have died on a `ReferenceError`
    (mutation D below, exactly the shape the stub-set guard exists for). It is now IMPORTED, not stubbed
    (`spineBundleKey.ts` is dependency-free on purpose), and part 5 gained three assertions that make it
    load-bearing: a scene-level spine key reports, a key shadowed by a param default sorts into
    `spineFallbackKeys` instead, and it is still exported. Without them the name could be deleted, or
    faked as always-true, and every other assertion would still pass.
  - Counts: **58638 → 58641**, exit 0 (parts 1-4 unchanged at 58628; part 5 10 → 13). On `main` the
    printed total was NOTHING — the process died before the count line. All 16 `verify-*.mjs` now exit
    0; `verify-board-tiles` was the only red one. ESLint and Prettier clean.
  - **Mutation-tested five ways.** (1) Delete the `reelGrid` branch from the real
    `collectArtRefs` — the bug part 5 exists to catch — and **7 of its 13 fire** (`got=[]` for the atlas,
    the used frame, the name-guess pool, the nested walk and the sprite-parity control), exit 1.
    (2) Restore the old narrow single-parameter regex stripper and the run dies at part 5 again, but now
    as `verify-board-tiles / editorArtExport.ts#collectArtRefs: the compiled slice does not parse as
    JavaScript — Unexpected token '=>'` with the offending line and a caret under it. (3) Drift a slice
    anchor 200 chars mid-function and `stripSliceTypes` refuses by name
    (`reelGrid.ts#resolveReelGridTileArt: the slice is not strippable TypeScript — Expected '}'`).
    (4) Fake `staticSpineKeyIsReachable` as always-true → 2 fail. (5) Drop it → `ReferenceError`.
  - ~~**Still owed, and cheap:** the other twelve fixtures that compile slices~~ — **DONE 2026-09-18**,
    see the entry above. All thirteen slice-compiling guards now go through `compileSlice`, and the four
    private `stripTypeScriptTypes` copies are deleted. The stub-set guard is still wired into only two of
    them, deliberately — and `verify-board-tiles` is still not one: four bodies would mean four `tsc`
    spawns against a fixture that runs in 0.17 s, and its one free identifier is imported and asserted.

- 2026-09-18 — **the same guard has now been repaired four times by adding the one name that run happened
  to hit, so the repair is now a shared check instead.** Several offline fixtures cannot import what they
  test (Svelte components, runes files Node will not load), so they SLICE the shipped source and compile it
  with `new Function`, whose parameters ARE the stub set. That list is hand-written and it DRIFTS: the
  component grows a call, the list does not follow. `new Function` compiles a free identifier happily and
  only throws when the line RUNS — inside an async cue handler several awaits deep — so the file dies on a
  bare `ReferenceError` naming one symbol, every check after it asserts nothing, and the fixture goes on
  looking maintained. It has now happened on `verify-swap-in-place-mode.mjs` three times (#622, #625, and
  `bakedArrivalReleaseEnabled` at part 9), on `verify-win-explode-pop.mjs` once (`resolveWinBeatBudget` at
  part 2 of 11) and on `verify-reelgrid-perspective-roundtrip.mjs` (#613).
  - **`scripts/lib/stub-set-guard.mjs`** type-checks the exact body a fixture is about to hand
    `new Function`, with the stub set declared ambient, and reports the "this name does not exist" family
    BEFORE the first case that compiles the slice — `tsc --noResolve` filtered to TS2304/TS2552/TS18004, the
    same technique `scripts/check-undefined-names.mjs` uses for the same bug one layer out. A missing stub is
    a message naming the fixture, the identifier and what to do about it, not a `ReferenceError` from the
    middle of a handler. Both fixtures adopt it, and both stopped passing stubs as the two positional lists
    `new Function` wants: names and values travel in one object now, so inserting a name can no longer bind
    every stub below it to the wrong value.
  - **It found two more the hour it was wired in, on a green `main`.** `overlayShown` and `settleBoard` are
    free in `Board.svelte`'s `boardSettle` / `tumbleBoardShow` / `tumbleBoardHide` handlers and nothing
    provided them — latent, waiting for the first case to drive one of those cues. Declared in the fixture's
    preamble (no cascade is ever up there, so `boardSettle` takes the plain settle path; the adoption path is
    `verify-swap-in-place-mode.mjs`'s).
  - **Two driven cases, so neither flipped stub is decorative.** A winner with no `explosion` bound leaves
    the board without a beat — the live `test6` scatter, one such cell cost every paying spin the whole
    budget — and the arrival-release A/B releases an emerge column in the tick it is seated where the awaited
    one pays `INTRO_BEAT_CAP_MS`, with the same broadcasts, landings, voices and settled board either way.
    Both predicates had been stubbed as constants nobody ever flipped, which is the shape a stub set drifts
    into.
  - Counts: `verify-win-explode-pop` 173 → 178, `verify-swap-in-place-mode` 644 → 676, both exit 0. Mutation-
    tested in both directions: deleting a stub fires the guard by name before the first compiled case (where
    without it the file still dies at part 2 / part 9 on a bare `ReferenceError`), and reverting the shipped
    behaviour turns each file red — 18/178 when the pop stops removing after its beat, 4/178 when the
    unbound-`explosion` skip is deleted, 15/676 when `swapInPlace` is re-coupled to `farScale`, 2/676 when the
    arrival release is ignored.
  - ~~**Still open, and found while checking the neighbours: `scripts/verify-board-tiles.mjs` is RED on
    `main`** since #734~~ — **CLOSED the same day**, see the entry above: the `editorArtExport.ts` slice it
    compiles had grown a TypeScript annotation its own stripping did not know, so the text never reached
    `tsc` and this guard could not have caught it. Repaired by handing the slice to Node's stripper instead
    of regexes, plus `scripts/lib/compile-slice.mjs` for the parse failure this guard's family does not
    cover. `verify-reelgrid-perspective-roundtrip.mjs` was the same shape and is green again since #613.

- 2026-09-18 — **a delivery is now a button, and it hands over a contract rather than a folder.** Producing one was two terminal commands run from a game repo's root, so the only deliveries we could make were ones somebody typed. The desktop launcher (separate repo, `Invisible-Wall-SL/invisible-launcher`) grew **📦 Deliver** beside ☁ Publish — profile picker, alias, build, zip, and a result dialog whose **▶ Play it** runs `serve-embed.mjs`. Pairing those two is the point: the second command is the one people skip, and skipping it means the first person ever to run the build is the operator. It is **not** a publish — nothing uploaded, no card registered, the test server never told. **The online launcher could not host this and the constraint is structural**, worth writing down because it will be asked again: `publishGame.ts` is a data-and-manifest operation that points a project at the prebuilt `_runtime/<id>` bundle, there is no `child_process` anywhere in `apps/launcher-api`, and it runs on Railway — it cannot compile a game. The desktop launcher is the thing that has game repos checked out. **What made the split non-obvious:** a GUI cannot run `build-delivery.mjs` in one shot, because `vite build` regularly finishes writing its output and then never exits (an open sass-embedded/esbuild handle keeps Node alive) and `spawnSync` cannot escape that — the launcher already solves it by watching for settled output and killing the tree. So the engine grew `--print-env` (the three variables a caller's own build must set) and `--skip-build` (package a `build/` someone else made), and the launcher restates nothing: env, profile list and result paths all come from the engine, and the only thing it knows better is the alias, which the partner composes from the display name with spaces removed. Packaging landed in `build-delivery.mjs`, **not** the `publish-game-bundle.mjs` the design doc planned — that script exists to upload to our R2 and register a card, and a delivery does neither. New: `EMBED.md` generated from the baked profile (so it cannot describe a build we did not make), `--zip` rooted at the `gameAlias`, `--alias`, `--list-profiles`, `--json`. The zip is hand-written on `node:zlib` (`scripts/zip-dir.mjs`) because this script runs from a game repo's engine submodule with no per-repo install; verified against Python's `zipfile` and Windows `Expand-Archive` — CRCs valid, contents byte-identical, unicode names intact, STORE chosen wherever deflate would grow a file. **Two defects surfaced by building it end to end.** `run()` passed an args array under `shell: true`, which Node concatenates without escaping (DEP0190): the engine lives at `C:\Invisible Wall SL\…` on the machine that builds deliveries, so `cmd.exe` looked for `C:\Invisible` and the `build-embed` step failed outright — meaning the documented second command had never worked from a spaced path. Shell calls now go as one pre-quoted string and the Node child is spawned directly via `process.execPath`. And a delivery was shipping the SvelteKit `index.html`: embed mode only switches `bundleStrategy`, so the shell still lands in `build/`, and left there it is a URL on the partner's CDN that loads our game outside their page and fails the session check. It is deleted once `build-embed.mjs` has read it. Covered by `verify_delivery_build.py` in the launcher repo (44 checks) — notably that an ordinary build carries none of the delivery env, and that the launcher holds no second copy of those variable names. **Verified against a REAL build** (`test6`, engine submodule advanced to this branch): `pnpm build` → 531 files / 286 MB → `delivery/` holding exactly `game.js` + `_app/` + `assets/` and no `index.html`, while `build/` kept its shell so a retry works; the 279 MB zip reads back through Python's `zipfile` with all 531 CRCs valid, every entry rooted at the alias, and an 80 MB PNG byte-identical. Then played through `serve-embed.mjs`: with no token it renders its art and refuses with a readable "Missing session token … (profile 'operator-embed')" — not the `[object Object]` that bug produced — and **with a token it boots the full game UI inside the operator's `<div id="game">` and POSTs `\{page origin\}/webnode/engine?sid=S0001e&seq=0`**, i.e. same-origin with the PAGE, the baked endpoint, and the session read from `window.params.GameSettings` rather than a URL param. **Zero asset 404s across all 531 files** — the whole point of a harness whose page and bundle paths share nothing. One 404 that is NOT ours is worth recording, though: `config.json` resolves against the PAGE (`/operator/config.json`), so for an embed delivery the operator's override file would have to sit beside the page that serves EVERY game — one file repointing all of them, the same hazard the shared runtime bundle refuses `config.json` for. Pre-existing Phase 1 behaviour, harmless (absent = normal), and not changed here because it changes delivery semantics.

- 2026-09-17 — **a delivery build is now producible and, for the first time, playable before it ships.** Two engine scripts. `build-delivery.mjs` runs a game repo's own `pnpm build` with the three env vars a delivery needs and then writes `game.js` — one command from any game repo, no per-repo wiring, because `new-game.mjs` writes a repo's scripts once at scaffold time and nothing refreshes them, so a script added only to the scaffold would work for games created after today and for none that exist. `serve-embed.mjs` plays the result: a fake operator page at `/operator/`, the game at a CDN-shaped path that **shares nothing with it**, and an RGS proxy so the game's same-origin calls reach a real node. Until this existed a delivery build could not be opened at all — no `index.html`, and it reads its session from `window.params`, so it refuses to boot anywhere but the partner's page. We would have handed over a bundle nobody had ever seen run. **It immediately earned its keep:** pointed at the live node with a spent session, the game showed the player `[object Object]`. `ModalError` only rendered a structured error when it carried BOTH `error` and `message` — our own throws do, so it looked right for years; a partner reports failures as HTTP 200 with `{result, error, errorCode}` and no `message`, so **every** partner-side failure reached the player as a type name. It now searches the known human keys breadth-first (the sentence sits at a different depth on each shape) and falls back to JSON rather than `[object Object]`. Verified both directions: the partner's expired session reads "not authorized", and our own missing-token error still reads as its sentence.

- 2026-09-17 — **audited what the partner's `config` event declares against what we actually read, and most of it reaches nothing.** The facade bridges three fields to the engine (`__IE_SERVER_CONFIG__`: `availablePayLines`, `symbols`, `window`) plus `betOptions`/`gameCost` in `betOptions.ts`. Unread: `oneCreditBuysLines`, `costPerReel`, `maxWays`, `symbolsPay.scatter` — and **`paytable`, which is the one that matters.** It is declared in `Play4FunConfigContext` and named in comments, but never read: the paytable screen comes from the AUTHORED config, so a delivery can show a player one paytable while the operator's server pays another and nothing anywhere notices. Not urgent for Book-of, where the two agree today; it matters the moment a partner changes a paytable on their side, which is exactly the change nobody tells the client team about. Reading it and warning on mismatch at boot — not "fixing" it — would be cheap. **Also recorded rather than dismissed: one spin in four in the first live session sent no `collect`.** The other three closed cleanly at `seq=2` with their own `gid`, the balance reconciled, and a later eight-round session collected every time; it has not reproduced. Worth keeping because the failure it would represent is a round left open that the client thinks is finished — the one that pays nothing while the HUD says otherwise — and the earlier collect bug lived in this same path. Response bodies would settle it; the browser pane only captured request URLs.

- 2026-09-17 — **played the third card against the live partner node, and it shrank the resume gap to almost nothing.** Book of Borut on the runtime with `rgs_profile=2complex` and a real partner session. The buy feature produced **16 requests in 918 ms**: `[bet, play]` at seq 0, ten free spins at seq 2–11 one request each, `collect` at seq 12 — all before the "you win 10 free spins" intro had even appeared. **A round settles in about a second; everything after is animation over a settled outcome.** Verified the hard way by reloading mid-presentation: the balance came back with the full €177.50 feature win banked, despite the client never finishing the animation. So the window in which a round is open is ~1s per spin, not the ~60s a feature takes to play. The inference that had me calling resume our most dangerous gap was wrong in a specific way worth remembering — **a fresh boot against an open round does not replay.** The server issued a fresh round id and a real spin; replay is reached by re-posting within a round the client is still tracking, not by starting over. The one real cost is cosmetic: with a round left open the HUD sat €1.00 below the server's figure, because our two-step balance holds the interim from `requestBet` until `requestEndRound` lands. So the remaining work is to read `config.resume` at boot and continue or close that round — not the replay queue, which only earns its keep for a game where the server waits on player input mid-round (a pickup, a gamble). Book-of does not. **And `seq` is proven at scale:** that 13-position round is the strongest test the implementation has had, and a per-request counter would have mis-numbered every free spin after the first. Also confirmed live: the buy price comes from the server (100× → €100.00 shown and charged), the bet menu is server-authoritative (`BASE` + `OPTION1`), and cross-origin calls work with no preflight.

- 2026-09-17 — **the partner's core libraries closed three questions and opened one real gap.** `p4f-game-core` / `p4f-slotty-core` (the layer under the reference client) confirm three things we had only reasoned our way to. **`simpleRequest` was right for a reason better than the one we gave it:** their transport never sets a `Content-Type` at all, so every request they have ever sent is `text/plain;charset=UTF-8` — a CORS simple request. The 2-complex node does not answer `Access-Control-Allow-Headers` because nothing on their platform has ever sent a preflight for it to answer. **Failures arrive as HTTP 200** with `error` in the body (what `partnerErrorText()` already handles), and `error.action === 'continue'` marks a non-fatal one. And their retry model is an infinite 1s resend behind a global reconnect gate that holds every other request — which is not belt-and-braces, it is what stops a retry storm reordering actions when `seq` is a position. **AND THE GAP IS RESUME** — the boot `config` carries `actions` + `resume`/`replay`; their client recovers the stake from the stored `bet`, takes `platform.gameRound.id`, and thereafter prepends the pending stored actions to every request until it has caught up. We do none of it. I called this the most dangerous gap we had; the live run the same day cut it down to almost nothing — see the entry above. Also recorded: the endpoint is a family (`engine`/`freerounds`/`batchengine`), and neither core reads `window.params` — assembling `serverConfig` from the embed page is each game project's own bootstrap, which no drop includes.

- 2026-09-17 — **the protocol has a reference now, read off the partner's own client instead of inferred — and it corrected us.** `docs/reference/play4fun-protocol.md`. The wire format had only ever lived in a section of `CLAUDE.md` reconstructed from Hot Fruits captures plus a WhatsApp thread; that was right about more than it deserved to be, but silent on most of the vocabulary and guessing at the rest. **`seq`-as-position is confirmed exactly**: their client builds the URL from the counter and only then advances it by the number of stored actions posted, `config` excluded — which is `takeSeq(storedActions)` line for line. **The bet-mode enum was wrong.** The design doc said `context[0]` is `0` base / `1` ante / `2` buy; it is the INDEX into `betOptions` for a betOptions game, or the bet multiplier for a line/way game. Game 2 has two options, so its legal values are 0 and 1 — which reads exactly like that enum if it is the only game you ever see, and a mode→enum map in the profile would have been a wrong abstraction built on a two-element coincidence. **Events run in TWO passes** (`bet`+`playedSpin`, then everything else) because stake and board must settle before any win event is read. The boot `config` carries the grid, paytable, paylines, `betOptions`, `gameCost` and `oneCreditBuysLines` — closing an open question and most of the "stop inventing the bet ladder" work. We are missing `symbolSetInMatrix`, `chooseBonus` and `gamble`, and we carry `tumbleStep`/`multiplierCollect` that their client has never heard of — a client covering gamble, pickups, free rounds and fast play has no cascade vocabulary at all, which is the strongest evidence yet that those two are our mock's invention. Also recorded and unimplemented: their **resume** mechanism, which replays stored actions after a reconnect and is the other half of seq-as-position.

- 2026-09-17 — **a real delivery names no RGS host at all, and the build validator became testable.** The RGS author confirmed their embed reaches the RGS through `RequestController.getBaseUrl()` — a relative path on the page's own origin, named by `GameSettings.service`. So a delivery's RGS is **same-origin**, and the CORS apparatus is moot for exactly the case it was built for: no preflight per spin, no `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` for an open-ended set of client and aggregator domains, no `simpleRequest` dodge, no absolute host baked into an artifact several operators receive. We could not express it: `rgsUrl()` already handled an empty base, but `config-vite` **required** a non-empty `rgs.baseUrl`, so a same-origin delivery was unbuildable — and `service` was read and then ignored because the baked endpoint won. `rgs.source: 'profile' | 'host'` closes both, bake-only like `allowUrlOverride` (an operator's `config.json` may repoint a build between their hosts; it may not change it to "whatever your page says"), and an omitted or empty `baseUrl` on its own still fails, because it has to be a declaration rather than an omission. Verified end-to-end through the embed harness: the game posted to `{page origin}/webnode/engine?sid=…` with the token from the page. **And `deliveryProfileProblems()` moved to its own leaf module** — in `index.js` no fixture could reach it, because the factory runs `sveltekit()`/`lingui()` first and they throw, which is the direct cause of its field list drifting twice. The fixture now imports the list instead of scraping the file for it, compares both directions, and asserts every shipped profile survives its own validator.

- 2026-09-17 — **a build a partner's page can include, and the two paths that were never script-relative.** The RGS author settled the delivery shape: no iframe (their page already sits inside iframes they do not control), a `<div>` plus a `<script src>` whose URL they compose server-side from `brand`/`versionPath`/`gameAlias`, so the filename is fixed and a content hash is impossible. `pnpm --filter lines build:embed` now produces exactly that folder. Most of it was already there and unused: `bundleStrategy: 'inline'` does not replace the single bundle, it copies it into the HTML, and the game's asset URLs already come from `import.meta.url` — which follows the script to the CDN and is precisely what inlining breaks, since an inline script has no `src` and falls back to the document. So the mode stops inlining and `scripts/build-embed.mjs` re-expresses the shell's boot (which global, which stylesheets, which hashed chunk — every one build-stamped, hence generated) as a standalone `game.js`. **Two paths did not follow**, both built as strings rather than from `import.meta.url` and both invisible while the document was ours: `srcBase()`'s page-relative `'assets/'` (boot splash + a project's own sound banks), and the audiosprite's own `src` list, which names its media from the deploy root and hands those to howler to resolve against the document — a delivery would have run **silently**. Verified against a harness serving the page and the game at paths that share nothing, so a document-relative URL cannot pass by accident: the game renders in their div and every asset resolves under the CDN path. The default build gets the identical 404 set, so the two fixes are no-ops for everything we host.

- 2026-09-17 — **an option's KIND comes from the authored config, because `betOptionsName` turned out to be backoffice.** We had been deriving base/ante/buy from the server's option NAME, and the RGS author has now told us that field is from their game config **for backoffice purposes** — not a client-facing declaration. A client switching player-visible behaviour on it would be reading someone else's admin data as a contract, and the distinction is not cosmetic: an ante modifies the bet and spins normally, a buy jumps straight into the feature. Cost cannot settle it either (1.25× _looks_ like an ante, 100× like a buy, but "looks like" is not a contract). So the AUTHORED config decides, via the match this function already makes — the same split as the rest of it: **the server owns the list and the prices, the game owns what each option IS and how it looks** — and it is the right owner anyway, since ante-versus-buy is a UI behaviour the game implements. The name heuristic survives only for an option no authored mode matched, where a guess beats nothing.

- 2026-09-17 — **`session.source` shipped unusable: the BUILD validator rejected it.** `config-vite` fails a build on any field it does not know, and its field list is a second copy of the runtime's. Adding `session.source` to the runtime and not to that copy meant a profile using it — the entire point of the feature — could not be built at all (`unknown field session.source`). Fixed, **and this is the second time**: `rgs.simpleRequest` did exactly the same thing a day earlier, with the same mystifying symptom. The two checks stay separate on purpose (one fails the build, one degrades at runtime), but the FIELD LIST is one fact in two places, so `profile.fixture.ts` now reads `config-vite/index.js` and asserts they agree. Verified both directions: a `source: 'host'` profile builds, and re-introducing the drift makes the fixture fail with `config-vite knows session.source`.

- 2026-09-17 — **a symbol no longer sits frozen on its intro for two seconds after an emerge.** Regression from the one-cell board merge earlier the same day, reported immediately: "after emerging, the symbols stay still and do not play any animation for a second or 2, then they start working correctly". The cell forwarded a renderer's completion only while a cascade SEAT was attached — but a seat says where a cell IS, not who is waiting on it. With "let the next spin start as soon as the symbols are back" on, `tumbleBoardAppear` does not await its intro beats, so `boardSettle` adopts the cells and detaches every seat while those intros are still playing; the completions were swallowed and every arriving symbol spent the full `INTRO_BEAT_CAP_MS` (2 s) parked on its intro before the cap settled it to `static`. The forward is keyed on the STATE now — `win`/`explosion` (the reel board's), `clearReel`/`intro` (a step's), `land` (both) — and on nothing else, so a settled win beat still cannot be re-fired by a looping clip on a cell that has moved on. The old overlay hid this by DESTROYING the cell at `tumbleBoardHide` and mounting a fresh resting one, i.e. the restart bug was masking the freeze. Guarded by a source claim in `verify-win-explode-pop.mjs` — the forward must not mention the seat at all, because a body that consults it passes every behavioural claim (the seat is attached for all of them).
- 2026-09-17 — **A standalone game repo has no `src/` any more, so every desktop build is on the latest engine.** Reported as "I made a build of test 6 from the desktop launcher and it's loading an old engine". It was, and the submodule was innocent: the launcher's publish advances `engine/` to `origin/main` before every build (`git submodule update --init --remote --force --recursive`), so `packages/*` were current — `test6`'s submodule sat on the tip. What was stale was the **game layer**. `scripts/new-game.mjs` stamped a full copy of `apps/lines/src` into each repo at scaffold time, and nothing ever refreshed it: `test6`, scaffolded 2026-09-09, was missing 27 changed files and one whole module (`game/celebrationLock.ts`), so it shipped the pre-refactor inline celebration lock — the bug that leaves turbo and slam dead from boot (#692). **53 of the last 60 engine commits touch `apps/lines/src`**, so this was not an edge case: "build from the desktop" structurally meant "ship a months-old game on a current set of packages", and a desktop build and the shared online runtime bundle — which `runtime-release.yml` builds from that same `apps/lines` on every merge — stopped being two views of one game.

  - **The fix is `kit.files`, not a re-seed.** `config-svelte` now points SvelteKit's `routes`/`appTemplate`/`hooks.server` at the submodule's `apps/lines/src`, so a game repo compiles the engine's source in place. A copy that must be refreshed can be forgotten; a path cannot. New `packages/config-svelte/appSrc.js` owns the single rule — **standalone = the build's cwd is outside the engine checkout** — which is self-describing (a game repo builds from the game dir, an engine app from inside the engine) and needs nothing opted into per repo.
  - **It reaches every EXISTING repo with no per-repo commit, no launcher release and no `.exe` rebuild**, because every game repo's `svelte.config.js` is `import config from 'config-svelte'` and that resolves _through_ the submodule the publish just advanced. Same lever `launcherProfile.ts` uses for the submodule pin.
  - Two things had to follow the source. **`bake-editor-doc.mjs`**: `editor-scenes.ts` imports the baked bundle relatively, so writing it to the game's `src/` would have left the build compiling the engine's checked-in default — a game with no scenes, no fonts, no custom components. `--dest` is now optional and, for a standalone build, contributes only the file NAME; the directory comes from the app source. **`publish-symbol-defaults.mjs`**: its `./src/game/*` defaults now resolve against the app source too (an explicit `--symbols`/`--config` still resolves against the cwd).
  - **`__IE_BUILD__` was a second, silent casualty of the same copying** and is fixed on the way past: the scaffolder hand-wrote each repo a `vite.config.js` that declared `__IE_DEBUG__` but not `__IE_BUILD__`, so every desktop-built game showed a blank on-screen build stamp while the launcher dutifully registered a version to the portal. The define moved into `config-vite`'s shared base (an app that sets its own still wins on `mergeConfig`).
  - A leftover `src/` is now **ignored, and says so** — one `[config-svelte] NOTE` per build naming `git rm -r src` — rather than silently shadowing nothing. `new-game.mjs` no longer seeds `src/` at all (only `static/`, the boot audio + bitmap fonts) and writes no placeholder route.
  - Verified end-to-end on the real `test6` repo against a patched submodule: full `pnpm build` chain green with `src/` deleted **and** with it present-but-ignored; the bake redirect, the symbol-module resolution (`Config filter: gating on …/engine/apps/lines/src/game/config.ts`) and the `__IE_BUILD__` stamp all confirmed in the build log; the notice latched down to 3 lines from 4 (SvelteKit loads the config in child processes, and the env var that would collapse it to one would have to be declared in `turbo.json` as a build input it is not — `turbo/no-undeclared-env-vars` caught that, correctly). Plus `node scripts/verify-engine-app-src.mjs` — 13 assertions over the real `appSrc.js`, install-free, covering the engine-app cases (repo root, `apps/lines`, a package a game's `build:engine` compiles) and the sharp containment case, a game repo being the engine's PARENT directory.
  - ⏳ Owed: the existing repos still carry their dead `src/` (harmless, now announced). Book of Borut additionally carries ~380 lines of genuinely hand-written game code across 6 files (`GlobalMultiplier`, `LoadingScreen`, `FreeSpinRetrigger`, `SymbolSpine`, `I18nTest`, `winLevelMap`) — the owner's call (2026-09-17) is that it becomes an ordinary pipeline project or is removed, so that code is not being preserved. Its repo is also missing 64 files the engine has since added, i.e. it was already far behind.

- 2026-09-17 — **a round left OPEN meant the win was never credited on a server that does not auto-collect.** Found while wiring the balance probe below, and it is the most consequential thing in this batch. Our mocks auto-collect on `play.context: ''`, so a bet's response already carried `gameRoundOver` and `requestEndRound` could answer from a stashed balance. The partner RGS does not: the round stays `updating` and **only an explicit `collect` credits the win** — measured on the live node (999760 after `bet+play`, 999780 after `collect`, win 20) and since **confirmed by the RGS author** as the intended flow, with no case in which the collect should be withheld. `requestEndRound` checked the stash FIRST, which made its own `collect` branch unreachable, so on the partner every round was left open and every win went uncredited **while the HUD showed one anyway, computed by us from arithmetic the wallet never agreed to**. Fixed by inverting the order — an open round is closed first and the `collect`'s own balance is the authoritative final — and by only deriving an interim (`final − win`) when the round actually closed, since otherwise the reported balance IS the interim and subtracting the win again shows a balance the player never had. Verified live: start 10000.00 → bet 0.40 with a 0.5× win → interim 9999.60 → after collect 9999.80, **and an independent balance probe agrees with the server**, which is the part that proves the round closed rather than merely looking right. The auto-collect path is unchanged (`smoke-facade`, `check-balance-flow`, `verify-buy-cost` all green).

- 2026-09-17 — **the operator's embed page is now a source the game reads, not just a wrapper around it.** A partner boots our client from a server-rendered page that resolves the session and the game's settings server-side and hands the browser one `params.GameSettings` object. Three things now come from it instead of being invented:

  - **The session token** — `session.source: 'param' | 'host'` on the delivery profile. Their wrapper never puts the token in the game's URL, so a delivery waiting on a query param would wait forever; `host` reads `params.GameSettings.token` and still falls back to the param, which keeps our own QA links working against a delivery build.
  - **Balance polling** — driven by the operator's own `balanceUpdateInterval` (30s on the 2-complex node), because a player can top up in the casino cashier without leaving the game and the HUD would otherwise keep the balance from boot. New `requestBalance` posts the non-stored empty-body probe and returns undefined in the two cases where asking is wrong: a round is OPEN (the partner answers `not authorized` mid-round, and a poll has no business interrupting a spin) or the request failed (blanking the HUD on a dropped packet is worse than a stale number). The stock `rgs-requests` declares it and returns undefined — the protocol has no balance endpoint — so the poller stands down there rather than inventing a request. **No embed page ⇒ no polling at all**, so every game we run today is untouched.
  - **Jurisdiction flags** — `enableTurbo`, `allowOutcomeBuy`, `showTheoreticalPayback` map onto `disabledTurbo` / `disabledBuyFeature` / `displayRTP`. These are licence decisions, not game decisions, and the facade used to pick them for itself. Only keys the operator actually STATED are applied: `hostBoolean` returns null for an absent key precisely so "the operator said no" can be told from "the operator said nothing", because overriding a default on silence is how a game ends up disabling turbo nobody disabled.
  - **One reader** (`packages/delivery-profile/src/host.ts`) serves all three, so a second copy cannot drift; it reads this window then the parent (a delivered client may run inside the embed page), and a cross-origin parent throws, which is exactly the case where we have no business reading it, so it reads as "no host settings". New `host.fixture.ts` (5 claims / 23 checks); `profile.fixture.ts` grew the `session.source` claim.

- 2026-09-17 — **one component draws a board cell, so a symbol that did not change keeps playing across a cascade.** A cascading board used to change hands twice per board change (`boardHide` → the overlay mounts a CLONE of the standing board → `boardSettle` → `boardShow`), and each hand-over destroyed one component tree and built the other. A new component starts its clip at frame one, so every standing symbol restarted, board-wide, however little about it had changed — reported on `invisible_wall/test6` as "the symbols reset twice on column 3, four times on column 5, before submerging". Measured with a container-keyed probe on the live board: **23 cells per spin re-created around art that had not changed**, in exactly two board-wide clusters. The per-column clear, removal and refill were already correctly scoped — every beat between the clusters touches one column — so the sweep was never the cause; it only spread the hand-overs far enough apart to count them.

  - **`TumbleSymbol.svelte` and `TumbleBoardBase.svelte` are gone.** `BoardBase` reads `stateTumble.active` and renders either the step's combined columns or the reel strips, through the one `ReelSymbol`; `TumbleBoard.svelte` keeps its cue handlers and its transition layer and loses its cells, its ground tiles and its board mask. With one board on screen there is one of each — which also retires the tile layer's double-draw guard.
  - **The step ADOPTS the board's cells rather than cloning them**, and `BoardBase` keys by the cell itself (a survivor whose row shifts is MOVED, not rebuilt). A seat the win pop emptied therefore arrives already `removed` and still `static` without either being re-derived — the clone had to read `boardRemoved()` and got it wrong until it was taught to. Symmetrically, `boardSettle` hands the cells back (`setSymbolsWithReelSymbols`, which detaches the cascade seats and renumbers them) instead of minting a fresh strip; the match is CHECKED, so a doc broadcasting its own board still settles from raw symbols.
  - **Byte-parity for a game that never cascades** is the reason each piece is shaped the way it is: the cascade seat is `null`, `stateTumble.active` is false, and the widened animating-layer predicate gains only `clearReel`/`intro`, two states such a game never reaches. `boardHide` outside a step still hides the board exactly as before; during one it cannot, because the step IS the board.
  - Verified: full monorepo build, 174 + 644 + 111 + 97 checks across the four board fixtures, and the whole `scripts/verify-*` sweep. ⏳ **Owed: a browser pass on a cascading board** — a fixture cannot see whether a survivor's slide reads, whether the mask still clips a cascading symbol, or whether paint order survives under perspective. Needs a runtime release to reach test6. Full record, both probes and the re-measure snippet: [board-cell-continuity](../design/board-cell-continuity.md).
  - **Two offline gates were dead on `main` and are green again.** `verify-swap-in-place-mode.mjs` threw `bakedArrivalReleaseEnabled is not defined` before any part-9 claim could run (parts 9–12 unreachable); `verify-win-explode-pop.mjs` threw `resolveWinBeatBudget is not defined` and ran nothing at all — and behind that crash four of its claims had gone stale against the authored win-beat ceiling. That is the third time a new free identifier in a handler has silently taken one of these files out: the slice throws before the first claim, and a fixture that cannot start looks exactly like one that passes unless someone reads the output.
  - Still red, pre-existing and unrelated: `verify-reelgrid-perspective-roundtrip.mjs` (`editorStorage.ts no longer declares NODE_KINDS`).

- 2026-09-16 — **A `fireCue` can wait for the animation an author-named cue starts — the completion the open signal bus cannot carry.** Supersedes one decision in the 2026-09-09 entry below: `emitComponentSignal` was fired "synchronous and deliberately OUTSIDE the awaited `awaitCue`", which was right about the hazard (a looping cue has no completion) but left `/flow-v2`'s "Wait for this cue to finish" tick a silent no-op on exactly those cues. `SignalSource` is `subscribe(run: () => void)` — there is no channel for a spine to report that it finished — so the wait is **measured** instead: new pure `cueAnimationDurationMs` (`engine-layout/src/lib/cueDuration.ts`), the cue-scoped sibling of `sceneAnimationDurationMs`, walking the MOUNTED scenes for cued `spine`/`flipbook` nodes and returning the longest clip, which `flowV2Runtime`'s `broadcast` folds into the same `awaitCue` race. The open bus itself, its firing order and every existing cue are untouched — an un-named cue measures 0 and does not wait. Full rationale, the four no-hang rules and the looping-cue-waits-one-cycle call are in [flow status](flow.md).
- 2026-09-16 — **the launcher can mint a partner session, so a game card can point at a partner RGS.** `GET /api/partner-session?key=<gameKey>&profile=<profileId>[&game=<id>]` mints on the signed-in user's behalf and 302s into the game (`&json=1` returns `{url, token, gameName}` instead). It exists because the partner's session endpoint is an **admin** one — username, password, and it will mint for ANY player id — so those credentials can never reach a bundle; the launcher holds them (`PARTNER_RGS`, see [INFRA](../INFRA.md)) and hands the game only the token. The redirect target is **not caller-supplied**: it is the game's own stored card URL, looked up by key and rewritten, so `?to=` cannot become an open redirect on an authed endpoint and `runtime=1`/`project`/`k` carry over exactly as `publishGame` wrote them — only `rgs_url`/`sessionID` are dropped for `rgs_profile` + the partner's session param.

  - **Two things the live partner taught us, both now handled.** It refuses a `remote_id` it did not pre-create (`player not found`) — so a per-user id cannot simply be invented; `PARTNER_RGS.players` names the pool it created and launcher users are spread across it by a stable hash, which keeps a user on the same partner wallet between launches while two testers still do not collide. And it reports that failure as **HTTP 200** with an error-shaped body, so `response.ok` proves nothing: the partner's own message is surfaced (`Partner RGS refused the session: player not found: …`) instead of a generic "no session token".
  - `partnerRgs.ts` takes the raw config **as an argument** rather than importing `ENV`, because `$env/dynamic/private` is a SvelteKit virtual module and importing it would make credential-guarding config parsing reachable only through a running server. The route reads env; the module is plain functions over a string and a `fetch`.
  - Verified: a 43-check offline fixture (`apps/launcher-api/partnerRgs.fixture.ts`) over the real module — config normalisation, the pool assignment, the exact mint query, every bad-answer shape, and that an unreachable-partner error does NOT carry the credentials — plus a **live mint against the partner** through the same module (real token, correct launch URL) and a green `launcher-api` build.
  - ⏳ Still owed: `PARTNER_RGS` set on the launcher-api Railway service, and a runtime release carrying `PUBLIC_DELIVERY_PROFILES` before a card actually plays this way.

- 2026-09-16 — **one runtime bundle, several RGSs — `?rgs_profile=<id>` picks which.** Every online game runs the SHARED `_runtime/lines` bundle, so pointing a game at a partner RGS could not be a per-build decision: the bundle now compiles a REGISTRY of delivery profiles (`PUBLIC_DELIVERY_PROFILES`, comma list or `*`; the runtime release sets `*`) and the launch URL selects one. **It is a whitelist** — the URL picks among hosts we shipped, it can never name one — which is what makes it different from re-opening `?rgs_url=`. **`internal` is reserved and always available**: the built-in default (our RGS through `?rgs_url=`), so development mode is a named third option rather than an implied one. An id the build does not carry **refuses loudly** and falls back to ours, because a silent fallback is a game that looks fine while playing somewhere the launch never intended. A **baked** profile still wins outright — a delivery pinned its RGS on purpose and the host page does not get to move it — and a SELECTED profile deliberately does not read `config.json`, since one file beside a shared bundle would repoint every game running it. Precedence: baked → `?rgs_profile=` → default; nothing selected is byte-identical to before. Verified live on all three paths against a registry of `internal, 2complex`: `?rgs_profile=2complex` boots `(selected)` against `https://gs.2-complex.science/webnode/engine` with the real config captured and the server's bet options published; `?rgs_profile=internal` and no param both POST to the local mock at `/rgs/engine` with `?sessionID=` honoured. New fixture `select.fixture.ts` (5 claims / 21 checks) covers the precedence, the reserved id, the loud refusal and the pinned-delivery case.

- 2026-09-16 — **the bet menu is SERVER-AUTHORITATIVE: a game offers the bet options the math declares, and no others.** Previously the menu came from the project's Invisible Game Config and the server's table was only cross-checked, so Book of Borut advertised three buy cards (25× / 50× / 100×) against a game-2 table with ONE buy option — two prices the wallet would refuse. Now the facade publishes the server's options to `globalThis.__IE_SERVER_BET_OPTIONS__` (the same decoupled-global bridge as `__IE_SERVER_CONFIG__`, since the facade is a drop-in for `rgs-requests` and cannot import the app) and `betModeMeta.buildBetModeMeta` builds the menu from it: **the server owns the LIST and the PRICES, the authored config owns the LOOK** (title/copy/art, matched by name, then by equal cost — presentation-only, so a wrong guess costs a label and never a charge). An option the config never authored still renders with a readable label. Ordering is already safe: `syncBetModeMeta()` runs after `<Authenticate>`, so the config event is in. **Option 0 is always keyed `BASE` when the server names nothing** — `activeBetModeKey = 'BASE'` is hardcoded in the engine's machines, and keying it `OPTION0` blanked the balance/win/bet readouts (caught live, fixed). Unnamed non-base options are keyed `OPTION<index>`, which `betOptionIndexFor` resolves positionally, so **every key the menu offers round-trips to its option by construction** — which is what removes the need for `betOptionsName` for correctness. A server declaring no table leaves the authored menu untouched. Verified live: with game 2's `[10, 1000]`, `__IE_SERVER_BET_OPTIONS__` is exactly `BASE` 1× + `OPTION1` 100×, the HUD reads €10,000.00 / €0.00 / €1.00, and the fixture pins the round-trip for named, unnamed and three-option tables.

- 2026-09-16 — **a game is playing live against the partner RGS.** `apps/lines` (book mapping, auto-selected from the server's own symbol vocabulary) plays real rounds on `gs.2-complex.science` game 2 — the Book-of math, alias `BookOfBetOptions`. Launch: `PUBLIC_RGS_TRANSPORT=play4fun PUBLIC_RGS_GAME=book PUBLIC_DELIVERY_PROFILE=2complex` on port 3051 (`.claude/launch.json` → `lines-2complex`), opened at `?sid=<token>`; mint a token with `admin.js?action=create_session&game_id=2&remote_id=playerN` (admin credentials, so server-side only — never in a bundle). Verified end to end: the boot `config` reached the engine mapped into client symbol space (`PIC1→H1 … SCAT→S`, 5×3, 10 paylines, in `__IE_SERVER_CONFIG__`), the real balance rendered (1,000,000 credits × `denom 0.01` = €10,000 — **`denom` confirms 1 credit = 1 cent**, which the amount scaling had only assumed), and three real spins debited €1 each and paid a €0.50 win. **The BET button IS the spin button** in this HUD.

  - **CORS needed a fix on OUR side, not theirs.** The node answers `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` but sends **no `Access-Control-Allow-Headers`**, so an `application/json` POST is refused at the preflight. New profile field `rgs.simpleRequest` posts the identical JSON body as `text/plain;charset=UTF-8`, which is a CORS **simple request** and is never preflighted — it both unblocks this server and removes an OPTIONS round-trip from every spin. `withCredentials: false` is likewise load-bearing: a browser rejects a wildcard origin on a credentialed request.
  - ~~**Open gap — a buy bet cannot be sent to this game yet.**~~ **CLOSED the same day** by the server-authoritative menu (see the entry above): the menu is built FROM the table, so the keys it offers round-trip to their options by construction and a buy sends correctly with or without `betOptionsName`. And the question behind it is settled — see 2026-09-17: `betOptionsName` is a **backoffice** field, not a client contract.
  - Noted behaviour: an out-of-band call on a session with an OPEN round answers `not authorized` (code 118). The game's own flow (collect) is unaffected; it bites when poking a live session with curl.

- 2026-09-16 — **the bet ladder and the buy price now come from the math, not from the client.** `requestAuthenticate` returned an INVENTED $0.10–$100 ladder with `disabledBuyFeature: true`, because no mock ever supplied one. The partner's server declares the real thing in two halves: `betOptions` rides the boot `config` event as **the credit cost of each bet option at multiplier 1, indexed by the first bet-context argument** (`[10, 1000]` ⇒ `[0,M]` costs 10×M base, `[1,M]` costs 1000×M buy), named by `betOptionsName` (`["0:base","1:buybonus"]`); `betMultipliers` + `initialBetMultiplierIndex` ride the operator's embed page (`window.params.GameSettings.config`, read from the parent frame too). **Total stake = `betOptions[x] × M`** — new `packages/rgs-translator-eagaming/src/betOptions.ts`. The facade now derives `betLevels`/`defaultBetLevel`/`betModes`, sets `disabledBuyFeature` from whether the table has 2+ options, and sends `context = [optionIndex, M]`, with M measured against the BASE option so the index alone carries the buy premium. **The option index is matched by NAME and REFUSES rather than guesses:** a game can have more paid modes than the math has options — Borut's three buy cards (25×/50×/100×) against a two-entry table — and guessing "a buy is the dearest option" would silently overcharge two of them while looking healthy. An unresolvable mode keeps the legacy encoding and the boot cross-check reports it (`HIGHNOON: 25× card, no server bet option`), which is a real open question for the partner. A server declaring no `betOptions` (both mocks, every server before this one) is untouched. `requestAuthenticate` also falls back to an explicit non-stored `config` action when the balance probe carries no config — a FALLBACK, not a first move, because our mocks reject unknown actions and the lines mock emits config on a session's first call only. Verified: a new fixture of 6 claims / 32 checks (`betOptions.fixture.ts`), plus `verify-buy-cost` (real book mock + real facade, all four cards) and `smoke-facade` still green.

- 2026-09-16 — **`seq` was a request counter; it is a POSITION, and the difference silently replays the round.** The partner RGS team spelled out the real rule: `seq` is the 0-based index in the round's stored action array at which the posted action(s) are placed, so a request carrying `[bet, play]` advances it by **two** and the next action belongs at `seq=2`. `sessionState.nextSeq()` advanced by one per REQUEST, so from the first `bet+play` onward every action aimed one slot short. That is not a numbering nit: writing to an **already-occupied** position is exactly how the engine exposes REPLAY, so against the real server our `collect` would have re-run the previous step instead of closing the round. It never showed up locally because our mock ignores `seq`. The evidence was already in our own repo — the captured Hot Fruits `collect` in `types.ts` carries `seq=2` after a `bet+play`. Fixed: `takeSeq(storedActions)` advances by the number of STORED actions posted, and `config` + the empty-body balance probe consume no position (the server does not store them). `seqOverride` is unchanged and is now the replay seam — an explicit earlier position replays that step and does not disturb the live one. Covered by a new fixture of 5 claims / 16 checks (`packages/rgs-translator-eagaming/seq.fixture.ts`, run with tsx) driving the REAL fetcher over a stub transport; `smoke-facade` and `verify-buy-cost` (real book mock + real facade) still pass. The inlined copies in `scripts/console-translator-demo.js` + `scripts/demo-overlay.js` were fixed too, since those are pasted at live servers. `CLAUDE.md`'s protocol note was corrected to match. **Clarification from the partner after the fix:** OMITTING `seq` entirely is equivalent — an action with no `seq` is appended, so `[bet,play]` then `[collect]` with no `seq` at all behaves identically to `seq=0` then `seq=2`. So the old counter was not blocking in the way first described; what it did was send _wrong_ values, which is the one option that is not safe. Correct values are kept rather than dropping the param, because `seq` is the prerequisite for replay and for round-state sync.

- 2026-09-15 — **A paying spin's symbol beats became a number the project owns, and a cell that can never report stopped buying the runaway guard.** Owner report on the live `test6` ("Ways on Waves"): "a long delay after a spin, as if the game was waiting for something before releasing the next spin". Instrumented the deployed runtime in the browser: a LOSING round is `reveal → setTotalWin` with a 1-2 ms gap before the next bet, while a PAYING one adds two ~4 s blocks — one inside `winInfo`, one between it and the next book event — repeating once per cascade step (one captured round was still going at 35 s). The `setTimeout` signature named them exactly: at each `winInfo` the game arms one `4000 ms` + one `650 ms` timer **per winning cell** (`Board`'s win beat, `Promise.all([awaitSymbolBeat(…, WIN_BEAT_CAP_MS), waitForTimeout(WIN_BEAT_MIN_MS)])`), then a second burst of `4000 ms` with no `650` partner (`boardExplodeWinSymbols`, whose cap has no floor by design).

  **The numbers were inflated; the structure was not.** Those measurements came from the desktop Browser pane, which renders at **~1 fps even when `document.visibilityState` reads `visible`** — long enough for any animation to lose its race against the 4 s guard, so the caps being paid was an artefact of the harness. The project's own data is the real answer: `test6` binds every symbol's `win` to spine `Pull` and `explosion` to `Take`, and the rig JSON puts those at **2.00 s** and **0.50 s**. `winExplode.enabled` is on and the project cascades, so a three-tumble round narrates three wins ⇒ ~7.5 s of symbol beats before the reels may turn again. Nothing was broken — the art is simply longer than the game wants to spend, and there was no way to say so short of re-authoring ten symbols.

  So `winBeat.maxMs` ("Cap each win at") is a **SHAPER**, deliberately separate from `WIN_BEAT_CAP_MS`, which stays an un-authorable runaway guard because `symbolBeat.ts`'s own header is explicit that a cap a real animation can hit stops being a guard and starts being the timing. `resolveWinBeatBudget(maxMs)` returns the three numbers `Board` races — `capMs`, `minMs`, `unauthoredMs`. Absent ⇒ all three are the constants that shipped before, so a project that never opens the setting is byte-identical; that parity is the whole safety argument, because this is the shared `_runtime/lines` bundle every online game boots. Both clamps are load-bearing: `WIN_BEAT_MIN_MS` is a `Promise.all` **partner**, not a `Math.min`, so an unclamped floor would silently ignore any budget under 650 ms and an author who typed 300 would still wait 650 with nothing anywhere to say why.

  **THE ACTUAL FIELD BUG, found when the owner said the delay was far longer than the explosion animation.** It is not the pace at all: **the pop sits out the whole budget for a winner that has no `explosion` bound.** `explosion` resolves through `resolveSymbolState`, which falls back to `static` — and on a cell that has just finished its win that is the art ALREADY ON SCREEN, so nothing re-mounts. `SymbolSprite` fires `oncomplete` from an `$effect` on `symbolInfo` and `SymbolFlipbook` re-arms its beat the same way; neither effect re-runs when the value it watches has not changed, so the cell can never report. The pop is ONE concurrent `Promise.all`, so a single such winner made every paying spin containing it hold for the full cap with nothing on screen to show for it. Audited `test6` end to end — every bound animation exists in its skeleton and all are short (`Pull` 2.00 s, `Take` 0.50 s, L3's `Idle_copy` 0.46 s) — but the **scatter `S`** binds **no** `explosion`, so a scatter win cost 4 s of dead hold against 0.5 s for an ordinary line win. ⚠️ **Corrected 2026-09-15, same day:** the first write-up of this said "`L4`, `L5` and the scatter `S`", and the owner caught it — _"we do not have any L5 in our test6 game"_. They were right. The in-play set is `paddingReels` (`game-config`'s `symbolsInPlay`), NOT the `symbols` dictionary and NOT the Symbols-tool doc: `test6` deals `H1`-`H5`, `L1`-`L3`, `S`, of which only the scatter lacks an `explosion`. `L4` and `L5` are dead rows in the Symbols doc (`L5` still bound to a placeholder `S_Test_Mockup`) and `W` sits in the config dictionary with no strip — none of the three can land, so none could ever have caused a stall. The mechanism and the fix were right; the example was not, because it was read off the Symbols doc rather than the strips. **That mistake is the general lesson here: a symbol row is not a symbol in play.** It also narrows how often this bit — scatter wins, not most paying spins — so the everyday length of a `test6` round remains the authored `Pull` (2.00 s per win, three times on a three-tumble round), which is what the ceiling is for. Such a cell is now **skipped** rather than shortened: no beat at all, but still marked `removed`, because "and be gone" is the pop's other half and a winner left standing is swept by the next board's `clearReel` — the double pop the feature exists to prevent. Same argument, same shape as the stacked-covered skip immediately above it. The WIN beat keeps the short unauthored beat instead (`hasAuthoredSymbolState`, as `TumbleBoard`'s emerge arrival does): there the cell IS on screen and the win line and stamped amount are scoped to that beat, so a readable minimum is right.

  **Corrected while here:** `symbolBeat.ts` claimed a LOOPING spine "never fires `complete`". It does — `SymbolSpineMain` passes `listener` (not `SpineTrack`'s deliberately one-shot-only `oncomplete` prop) and `propsSyncEffect` assigns it onto the TrackEntry, where Spine fires `complete` at the end of every loop iteration; `SpineTrack` says exactly that where it explains why its own prop is one-shot-only. The false claim mattered because `Symbol.svelte` defaults an unauthored cell's `loop` to TRUE, so "loops ⇒ hangs" condemned most ordinary authoring — five of `test6`'s ten symbols included — and would have sent the next reader hunting a hang that isn't there.

  **Verified:** new `scripts/verify-win-beat-budget.mts` (18 assertions — unset parity, both clamps, the floor boundary, plus a CALL-SITE half that reads the real `Board.svelte` and fails if either beat still passes a bare constant; that half exists because PR #613 was two `verify-*` guards slicing source that had moved, and so guarding nothing) — ALL PASS. `pnpm --filter lines build` clean with no circular-import warnings; eslint clean on both changed files (`apps/lines` carries 17 pre-existing errors, all in `src/stories/**` plus `editor-scenes.ts:26`, none from this change; the repo has no working `svelte-check` — it is not a dependency). ⏳ **NOT browser-verified:** a real paying spin is unreachable in the Browser pane (the reel reveal had not completed after 62 s at ~1 fps), and the authored value cannot be exercised at all until the tool half ships a doc to read. The authoring half is in [symbols status](symbols.md). **Engine change on `main`** — reaches an online game only on a runtime-bundle republish, and standalone Book of Borut only on an `engine` submodule bump.

- 2026-09-14 — **delivery profile + `config.json`** (see Current state; plan in [delivery-builds](../design/delivery-builds.md)). New `packages/delivery-profile`; consumers in `state-shared`, `rgs-translator-eagaming` and `components-shared/Authenticate.svelte`; the bake + its build-time validation live in `packages/config-vite`. Also added `PUBLIC_RGS_GAME` and `PUBLIC_DELIVERY_PROFILE` to `turbo.json`'s `globalEnv` — both change the emitted bundle, and `PUBLIC_RGS_GAME` was already missing, so a cached build could ship the wrong symbol mapping. No runtime release needed until a delivery is actually cut (the feature is inert without a baked profile). **Phase 2 (protocol deltas) and Phase 3 (packaging) are NOT done** — see the design doc.

- 2026-09-14 — **A FLIPBOOK character reacts to a signal too — the cue swaps its CLIP.** Owner-reported days after the spine cue work landed: the character on their background screen had no "default animation" and no "Plays on signal" at all. It was a `flipbook` node, not a `spine` — and the cue system was spine-only by construction (`SpineCue` lives on `SpineNode`; the type comment said "narrowed to spine-only"). Not a niche gap: a character built from FRAMES rather than a rig is the common case for art that never went through the Rigger, and such a node could not react to anything. New `FlipbookCue {signal, clipId, loop?}` on `FlipbookNode.cues` — when the signal fires, the node plays that clip instead of its own. **Everything load-bearing already existed and is reused as-is** (the open signal bus, the scene-level subscription, the palette harvest); three seams widened. **(1)** `ComponentSignalAnim` gains `clipId?` beside a now-optional `animation?` — exactly one is populated per entry, decided by the cued node's kind, and the map is keyed by node id so the two shapes never meet on one entry; `ComponentCuePayload` is split out so a caller can build the payload where `node.kind` still narrows. **(2)** `LayoutNodeView`'s scene-level effect flattens both cue shapes into ONE subscription list _before_ subscribing — the narrowing has to happen where `node.kind` is still in scope, because `node.cues` is a `SpineCue[] | FlipbookCue[]` union that no separate boolean can narrow. **(3)** `flipbookClip` consults the active cue first and falls back to the node's own `clipId`, so a DANGLING cue clip keeps the resting clip rather than blanking the node; the cue's own `loop` outranks the placement's, which still applies underneath. `ComponentInstance` indexes flipbook cues into the same map, so a flipbook inside a component behaves identically. **No `completeSignal` twin, by construction:** a spine one-shot reports completion through `SpineTrack`, but `<Flipbook>` exposes no completion hook — a play-once clip just stops on its last frame. `handsOffToIdle` therefore early-returns on an entry with no `animation`, which is what stops a flipbook payload reading as "cue animation ≠ default" (`undefined` ≠ `'idle'`) and silently un-looping a held clip. Re-firing a cue naming the clip ALREADY playing does not restart it (`<Flipbook>` derives its textures from the clip object, and an unchanged clip is an unchanged object) — which is what keeps a held loop from stuttering. **Rule 8 is satisfied, but only by alignment:** `main` now prunes clips + atlases that nothing "plays" (#644/#645) via `collectClipIds`, a deliberately generic deep walk over any `clipId` key — and a cue's target clip is referenced by NOTHING else in the doc, so a miss there would prune it and the swap would silently resolve to the resting clip. The cue field is named `clipId` precisely so the walk finds it, and the new check asserts that alignment rather than trusting it. Storage needed no change (`normalizeNode` trusts kind+id; `identityComponentRoot` passes children by reference), verified for both a scene and a component def. **Verified:** new `pnpm --filter launcher-api run check:scene-cues` (both cued kinds, nesting, trimming, catalog exclusion, both identity-preserving paths, and clip reachability); `test-cue-signal-bus.mjs` gains a section pinning that a flipbook payload never drives the spine hand-off; every engine-layout harness unregressed; `engine-layout` + `apps/lines` + `launcher-api` build clean; `svelte-check` at 255 / 187 (completed, not OOM) / 66 — each identical to the `main` baseline, none from this change. ⏳ **NOT browser-verified** (same reason as the spine half — it needs an authored doc behind the auth-gated editor). Authoring UI: [editor status](editor.md); palette: [flow status](flow.md).

- 2026-09-09 — **A placed character can hold a looping animation for as long as the game is in some mode, and a Flow can address it by a name the author invented.** Owner ask: an idle character in a screen that switches to a spin animation on the spin press, LOOPS it while the reels roll, and returns to idle — wired from `/flow-v2`. Every piece of the shape already existed (a spine node's `cues[]`, `{signal, animation, loop}`) and none of it could express that ask, for three independent reasons, each fixed at its own seam. **(1) The `loop` tick was a silent no-op in exactly this configuration.** The one-shot→idle hand-off fired on "cue animation ≠ `defaultAnimation`" — which IS the idle-plus-a-held-mode rig — and then forced `loop=false` + `then=defaultAnimation`, so the mode animation played once and dropped back while the author's ticked box said otherwise. The predicate moved out of the `LayoutNodeView` markup into `signalGates.ts` as a pure `handsOffToIdle(cue, defaultAnimation, hasStateAnimation)` (joining `isNodeRevealed`/`isTapArmed`/`wantsCompleteListener` — the same "extract so it is testable and the consumers cannot disagree" move) and now exempts a cue that explicitly asked to loop. An UNTICKED cue is unchanged, so the free-spin-intro pattern (`enter → intro`, then idle) is byte-identical. Because a fired cue is never CLEARED (there is no stop-cue op — `signalAnims[nodeId]` is written and never deleted), a looping cue holds until another cue replaces it: **returning to idle is a second cue that plays the idle clip**, not a stop. **(2) A spine placed straight in a screen could not receive a cue at all.** The cue→animation map is provided by `<ComponentInstance>`, so `cues[]` on a bare scene node was inert and authoring one required first turning the character into a reusable component. `LayoutNodeView` now subscribes such a node's OWN cues into a local `sceneSigAnim`, consulted only when no instance map exists (an owning instance always wins, and the effect returns immediately in that case ⇒ a spine inside a component keeps exactly today's path). Deliberately narrower out there: no `completeSignal` is carried, because there is no per-instance fired-signal bus to fire it on — which is also why `hiddenUntilSignal`/`tapArmAfterSignal` remain component-only. **(3) A cue's signal had to be one of the 13 names the game hardcodes.** `registerComponentSignals.ts` gains an OPEN bus beside the closed registry: `getComponentSignal` is now `registry.get(key) ?? openSource(key)` and never returns `undefined` (the old `undefined` MEANT "skip this cue", which is what made an author-named signal impossible), plus `emitComponentSignal(key)`. One line in `apps/lines`' `flowV2Runtime` `broadcast` fires every flow `fireCue` name into it — synchronous and deliberately OUTSIDE the awaited `awaitCue`, since a looping cue has no completion and awaiting it would hang the exec chain. **A registered name still wins**, so the two names that are both a vocab cue and a catalog signal (`specialBookReveal`/`specialBookHide`) resolve through their emitter subscription exactly as before and cannot double-fire; the two now-dead `if (!source) continue` guards in `ComponentInstance` went with it. **`enter` is RESERVED** (`ENTER_SIGNAL`, skipped by both subscribe loops): it is the one component-LIFECYCLE signal, fired by the instance itself on its visible edge and by no source — it is in `ENGINE_SIGNAL_CATALOG` but NOT in the registry, so the open bus would otherwise have handed it a subscription where before it had none, and an author who typed `enter` as a scene cue name could replay the intro of every mounted component at once. The file's own comment already asserted "the game-registered loop above skips it"; the skip is now explicit rather than an accident of `getComponentSignal` returning `undefined`. Unchanged by design: the emitter has no replay and neither does the open bus, so **a cue fired while its screen is unmounted is still lost** (the standing "a cue needs its host mounted" constraint). **Verified:** new `node scripts/test-cue-signal-bus.mjs` (22 assertions — open-bus fire/repeat/unsubscribe, fan-out, a subscriber detaching mid-fire, registry precedence incl. the no-double-fire trace, teardown of both buses, and the full `handsOffToIdle` matrix with the looping-cue regression pinned); `test-signal-gates`/`test-tap-to-continue`/`test-signal-subscribe-untrack` unregressed; `engine-layout` + `apps/lines` + `launcher-api` build clean; `svelte-check` on `apps/lines` (12 GB heap, completed — not OOM) and on `engine-layout` shows no error from any new identifier, only the pre-existing pile. Booted `apps/lines` against the local mock RGS on the v2 driven seed — loading → tap → basegame with a live HUD, no new console errors. ⏳ **NOT browser-verified: the scene-level cue → `SpineTrack` render path**, which needs an authored doc (auth-gated editor + R2). **`completeSignal` BEATS `loop`** — the one non-obvious rule, and it exists because the naive version of this change could hang a round. `SpineTrack` attaches its completion listener only when `loop` is false, so honouring `loop` on a cue that also names a `completeSignal` would silently drop the hand-off: a `hiddenUntilSignal` sibling would never reveal, a `tapArmAfterSignal` tap would never arm, and a screen held by `showContainer{awaitComplete}` would wait forever. The two fields contradict each other (`completeSignal` is documented as "ignored for a looping cue"), the editor can still SAVE the pair (`updateCue` never clears the value when `loop` is ticked, it only hides the input), and — because the tick was previously dead in this exact configuration — a shipped doc plausibly carries it. So a cue with both is treated as the one-shot it was always run as, which makes the dangerous case byte-identical rather than merely "audited". **Parity risk, the remaining one:** an already-saved cue with `loop` ticked, a distinct `defaultAnimation` and NO `completeSignal` one-shots today and loops after this — no in-repo def carries a `cues:` array, but audit R2-saved component docs before merging. A half-authored cue (blank signal or blank animation) is now skipped at both seams; previously a blank animation won the `?? defaultAnimation` fall-through (`''` is not nullish), unmounted the track and froze the rig on its bind pose. **Engine change on `main` — reaches an online game only on a runtime-bundle republish, and standalone Book of Borut only on an `engine` submodule bump.** The authoring halves are in [editor status](editor.md) (the Scene Editor cue block) and [flow status](flow.md) (author-named cues in the palette).

- 2026-09-09 — **A stepped PERSPECTIVE board's compound mask had a HOLE down one column boundary, and the background painted through it on top of the symbols.** Owner report on the live `test6` ("Ways on Waves"): "a line that draws on top of the symbols… only between the first and second column and only on the right of the first cell", always in the same place, unchanged by a resize. It is the mask. `boardMaskColumns` builds one polygon per column and rests entirely on them TILING EXACTLY — the doc block says so, and the exactness is load-bearing, because an overlap is what lets a tall neighbour swallow the notch beside a short column. Under perspective a shared boundary is a **curve** (x contracts linearly in the ROW while y is the quadratic running sum of compressed pitches), and a polyline reproduces a curve only at the knots it is sampled at. Each column sampled that shared edge at **its own** row boundaries, so two neighbours out of phase described two different edges and left a lens-shaped sliver covered by neither.

  Two independent things put neighbours out of phase, and each alone is invisible — it took both. (1) `rowOffsetForReel` returns `slack / 2`, so a column whose slack is ODD starts half a row off its neighbour. `test6` is `numRows [3,4,4,4,4]` centre-aligned ⇒ offsets `0.5, 0, 0, 0, 0` ⇒ **exactly one** boundary out of phase, which is why exactly one line. (2) `padY` spent the authored symbol overflow straight down the y axis, so a corner kept the x of its own row while moving to the depth of a different one and stepped OFF the curve sideways — and a short column spills from a different row than a tall one. Measured from the five polygons pulled out of the running game: a **0.86 board-local px** hole, open from y≈4 to y≈150 and closed below — the first cell's right edge, exactly as reported.

  Fixed on both axes. `perspectiveRowAtSum` is the closed-form inverse of `perspectiveRowSum`, so the overflow is spent ALONG the curve (which row actually sits that far past?) instead of displacing y — the corner stays on the boundary and the spill is still exactly the authored distance, because the two are inverses. And each edge is now sampled at the UNION of its own knots and the neighbour-across-that-edge's, so the two knot sets are identical wherever the columns overlap, which is the only place the question is asked. Both are **perspective-only**: a flat boundary is a straight vertical line, so knot placement cannot move it and displacing y is exactly right — the flat branch is the one that has always run, byte-for-byte, which matters because this is the shared `_runtime/lines` bundle.
