In phase (`top`/`bottom` alignment, even slack, no overflow) the neighbour's rows land on this column's own, the `Set` collapses them, and the ring is unchanged.

  **The invariant is now asserted, which is the real repair.** Every existing mask probe sat at a CELL CENTRE — half a cell from where columns actually meet — so nothing could ever have seen this. `scripts/verify-symbol-seat.mjs` gains a scanline check that walks each adjacent pair's shared span and fails on any gap (an overlap is fine; only a hole is a bug), across 15 grids × 3 board depths × 4 stepped shapes × overflow on/off. It finds **570 holes on the pre-fix code**, up to 15.3px wide, and zero after. `verify-stepped-grid.mjs` and the 274,644 existing seat assertions stay green; `tsc` reports an identical error set before and after.

- 2026-09-08 — **`scripts/verify-swap-in-place-mode.mjs` had not run past part 2 for the length of a feature, because its TypeScript stripper was hand-written.** The fixture slices the shipped `TumbleBoard.svelte` / `flowEffects.ts` / `stateTumble.svelte.ts` and evaluates them as JavaScript, and the type removal was one regex per annotation the sliced code happened to contain, backed by a guard that threw on anything unrecognised. The explosion → intro Transition work added `type SeatTransition = {…}`, a `scheduleTransition` signature, `$state.raw<SeatTransition[]>` and `new Map<string, ReturnType<typeof setTimeout>>()` to that slice, so every run since died at _"the TumbleBoard helper slice grew a type annotation this fixture cannot strip"_ — before part 3. Parts 3–10 (the drop-in reveal, the column cascade, the stagger knob, the ground tiles, the clear step, the emerge style) asserted nothing at all in that window. Node 24's `stripTypeScriptTypes` (`node:module`) does the removal now, per slice and named in the error when a slice is not erasable; the Svelte rune replacements stay, because runes are not TypeScript. Growing an annotation is no longer a failure mode: verified by adding generics, `satisfies`, a `ReadonlyMap` and `as const` to the sliced component and re-running green.

  Two drifts had accumulated behind the dark parts, both "the fixture no longer stands the component up", not shipped bugs: the handlers call `inTransit` (the overflow transit counter, #614) and read `stateGameDerived.boardSwapsInPlace()` / `boardSwapStyle()` in `tumbleBoardExplode` (the Transition gate), neither of which the harness provided. `inTransit` is SLICED rather than stubbed — it awaits the movement it wraps, so a stand-in that forgot to would un-order every beat the fixture measures; the style is derived from whichever presentation is being driven. Every run leaves the transition unauthored (`bakedSymbolTransition() → undefined`), which is the shipped default and is now stated in the file's "what it cannot prove" list. **553 checks green**, and they bite: mutation-tested by making the slide instant (6 fails), reversing the cascade sweep (13), dropping the `!reelBoardShown` term from the tile guard (2) and rebuilding the settled cells instead of passing identity (50).

- 2026-09-08 — **Symbol overflow, fixed twice over: a latched mask that made SPINS worse than before it existed, and the animated states it never reached.** Owner report: "the padding is working for the static reel … but when the symbols are animating because of the intro state or the explosion state, or the spin state, then they still get cut". Three defects, and the first is a regression the same-day feature commit shipped.

  1. **THE LATCH.** `BoardMask` wrote `y={overflow.y === 0 ? undefined : -overflow.y}`, on the theory that skipping the prop was the parity-safe way to leave an un-authored board untouched. It is the opposite: `propsSyncEffect` applies a prop only `if (props[key] !== undefined)`, so undefined means KEEP THE PREVIOUS VALUE. An authored board therefore latched — the first settle wrote `y = -overflowY` and nothing ever wrote it back, so on the next spin `height` shrank to the tight window while `y` stayed high and the mask sat wholly `overflowY` px too high: that much cut off the bottom of the reels, and an equal strip uncovered above the top for the strip to show through. Now always a number (`ObservablePoint`'s setter is guarded, so writing the 0 an un-authored board already holds never dirties its transform).
  2. **THE GATE ASKED THE WRONG QUESTION.** It read `motion === 'stopped'`, but `preSpinSlideDownLoop` awaits its FIRST slide — a full reel-length through both window bounds — before assigning `motion = 'spinning'`, because that same statement flips every symbol to its `spin` art and moving it would change what every game draws during the opening slide. So the mask was wide open for the opening ~300 ms of every spin. `createReelForSpinning` now carries an explicit `rolling` flag, set before the pre-spin strip is even built and cleared beside `motion = 'stopped'`; `boardOverflow` gates on that.
  3. **THE ANIMATED STATES LIVE ON A BOARD THAT NEVER ASKED.** `symbolState = 'intro'` has exactly one assignment in the repo (`TumbleBoard.svelte`) and `tumbleExplosion` one — both on the swap/cascade overlay, which mounted `<BoardMask />` flagless. The exclusion's stated reason ("a cascade's symbols cross the window edge on their way in") is false for precisely those two beats: the explosion is set on symbols already resting on their seats, and the emerge sets `intro` immediately before a `symbolY.set(…, { duration: 0 })` whose own comment reads "Nothing travels; the arrival is the animation, not the movement." The reel-motion gate could not have been reused there anyway — `motion`/`rolling` are written only by a reel's spin loop, and a swap board never spins, so every reel reads settled mid-fall. The overlay now answers for itself with a transit COUNTER (a boolean would be cleared by whichever staggered column finished first) bracketing exactly the three beats where a symbol travels: drain, the slide-down fall, and the appear's survivor-vacate phase. `boardOverflowAuthored` is the ungated accessor it spends — split from `boardOverflow` rather than given a flag, so a future caller cannot pass the wrong one and silently uncover a rolling strip.
     The fixture's blind spot is the lesson worth keeping: it read the four mask props as VALUES, and `y: undefined` compares equal to "no y prop" — parity, seemingly. A prop is not a value, it is an ASSIGNMENT with its own skip rule. `scripts/verify-symbol-overflow.mjs` now replays a full settle→spin→settle→spin round through the shipped `propsSyncEffect` semantics against ONE reused container, and fails 137 assertions against the shipped bug.

- 2026-09-08 — **The board mask can be grown past the reel window once the board settles (`boardOverflow`), so overhanging symbol art is not cut off.** `BoardMask` gains an opt-in `allowOverflow` prop; `Board.svelte` passes it, `TumbleBoard.svelte` does not. The authored px comes off the `reelGrid` node (`overflowX`/`overflowY`), converts to board-local in `boardGeometry` the same way the cell edges do, and is handed to `boardMaskColumns(overflowX, overflowY)` for a stepped board. **The gate is the design**: the mask is what hides a rolling strip, and `SymbolWrap` culls a symbol at its CENTRE — so a widened mask mid-spin reveals the half of each entering cell that the window exists to clip. Hence "every reel `stopped`", not "each reel for itself" (one rectangle spans all columns) and not "always". `boardOverflow` returns a shared frozen `{x:0,y:0}` when unauthored or unsettled, so the common answer allocates nothing and reads as an unchanged reference to the consuming `$derived`. Full story + the authoring UI in [editor status](editor.md); parity pinned by `scripts/verify-symbol-overflow.mjs`.

- 2026-09-04 — **A win overlay's “coin fountain” toggle could not switch the coins off, because a SECOND, unauthored win overlay was drawing them** (`basegameOverlaysScene` in `apps/lines/src/components/Game.svelte`). Owner report on the live `test6`: `showCoins` unticked on the placed Win Overlay, coins still rain. The instance param was saved and honoured — its doc carries `showCoins: false` and `WinVisual` reads it correctly. The coins came from a different `WinVisual`. `test6`'s doc has no `basegameOverlays` scene at all (its win art is a `win` componentInstance on the flow screen `s_00acdk3f`), so `Game.svelte` fell back to the reference layout's copy of that scene, which — with `WIN_INSTANCE = false` — binds the OFF-path composer `Win`: the gate PLUS a param-less `WinVisual`. Outside a `componentInstance` there is no param context, so that second visual runs at the component's DEFAULTS (`showCoins` defaults true) and emits the fountain board-centred in the REFERENCE layout's coordinates, i.e. nowhere near the authored art. Same fallback also made `basegameOverlaysHasCodedWinGate` true, so `resolveWinMount` stood the intended headless flow driver down and the coded gate drove the count-up instead. Fix: when the doc places its own win visual anywhere (`collectComponentIds(…).includes('win')`), the overlays scene's `bind:Win` node is degraded to `bind:WinGate` — the GATE half alone (count-up + `winState` + round-block, which the authored visual reads), no duplicate art. That is exactly the `WIN_INSTANCE`-ON shape the gate/visual split was designed for; it was simply never reached by a doc that authors the visual while leaving `basegameOverlays` at the reference default or omitting it. No `Win` bind, or no authored win visual ⇒ the same scene object back ⇒ byte-identical (Book of Borut authored the gate out, so it is untouched; a doc-less boot keeps the composer). **Verified** against the live `test6` doc, running `apps/lines` locally with `?runtime=1&project=test6&editorDocBase=…` and `PUBLIC_RGS_TRANSPORT=play4fun`: before, a real `max`-tier win logged `overlays binds [Win, Transition]`, a `WinVisual {boundToInstance: false, paramKeys: 0}` and `WinCoins {emit: true}`; after, `[WinGate, Transition]`, the only `WinVisual` is the authored `{boundToInstance: true, paramKeys: 18}`, no `WinCoins` across many wins, and the BIG/SUPER/MEGA presentations render unchanged. **Needs a runtime release** to reach online games.

- 2026-09-03 — **Incident: the #566 release blanked every online board for ~20 min.** `RiggedEffect.svelte` used the new `getContextSpineLoadScale()` without importing it; nothing in the pipeline catches an undefined identifier in a Svelte script (`no-undef` is off in `eslint-config-custom`, `svelte-package` and `vite build` do not resolve it), so the auto runtime release shipped a `ReferenceError` thrown at mount that took the reel tree down — HUD + background drew, symbols did not. Hotfix #567 (one import line). Its first release attempt then failed in **Publish to `_runtime/lines`** on a transient R2 `InternalError`; the re-run of the failed job published and the test server was refreshed by hand. Two process rules now in the runbook: run svelte-check UNFILTERED and BOOT the game locally before merging anything under `packages/pixi-svelte`, `packages/engine-*` or `apps/lines/src`; and after every runtime release check the served `bundle.<hash>.js` AND the live console, not just the workflow colour.
- 2026-09-03 — **Content bound INTO a rig (Flipbook clips, FX) now scales with the rig's LOAD scale, so a carrier symbol is the same size on the board as in /symbols and the Rigger.** Reported as "still a size mismatch between game and editor" right after the Bounds fix shipped. `R_Lobster` (test6 H4) has no attachments — its picture is the `f_lobster` clip bound to a slot. A symbol bundle is read at `SYMBOL_SPINE_LOAD_SCALE` (2): the reader scales bone POSITIONS and attachment geometry by it but never a bone's own scaleX/scaleY, and spine-pixi's slot objects and `<SpineBoneAttach followScale>` follow only the bone's scale. So the frame filled the cell (the 2 × `SYMBOL_SPINE_FILL` 0.5 pair) while the clip inside drew at HALF — and the tools, which load every rig at 1, showed it full. Measured headlessly on the deployed bundle: bone2 sits at y 26 at load 1 and y 51 at load 2 with scale 0.75 in both. `<SpineProvider>` now publishes its bundle's load scale in context (`setContextSpineLoadScale`); `<RiggedFlipbook>` / `<RiggedEffect>` multiply their content by it, and `<SpineBoneAttach rigUnits>` re-applies it on the bone path, where the bone-follow ratio would otherwise cancel it. Rigs with real attachments are byte-identical (the reader always scaled their art). NOT touched: an Invisible FX effect pinned to a game rig through `EffectLayer` / `placement.space: 'bone'` — the same class of gap, but a different authoring contract (`/fx`), left for its own change.
- 2026-09-02 — **A spine symbol is centred on its authored BOX, not its skeleton origin.** `<SpineProvider centreBox>` (pixi-svelte) pivots a cell-fitted rig on the centre of `skeleton.{x,y,width,height}` — load-scale-aware and y-flipped (`spineBoxPivot`) — and `SymbolSpineMain`, `StackedPicture` and the paytable `InfoOverlay` opt in. Every Spine-editor rig we ship centres that box on the origin, so they are byte-identical; a Rigger rig's box (the measured setup-pose extent, or a hand-dragged frame) is not, and the game used to size it right and place it wrong. Full story in [rigger status](rigger.md). Placed scene spines are untouched (origin-at-position, matching the Scene Editor). Ships to online games via the automatic runtime release.
- 2026-09-01 — **A win bound to SPRITE art lasted one frame, so most wins — and their amount text — were never seen** (`WIN_BEAT_MIN_MS` in `symbolBeat.ts`, applied in `Board.svelte`'s `boardWithAnimateSymbols`). Owner report: "the win amount text in the middle doesn't show when the reels tumble and I get a second win". The tumble was incidental. `SymbolSprite` reports `oncomplete` from an `$effect` the moment its art changes, so a `sprite` bound to the `win` state resolves the beat in the same tick — and everything scoped to that beat is torn down with it, including the win's stamped amount text. Measured on the live `test6` at full frame rate, one cascading spin: `winInfo L2 -> 2ms` (sprite, no text), `winInfo H4 -> 961ms` (flipbook, "€0.05" drawn at board centre), `winInfo H3 -> 2ms` (sprite, no text). Eight of that project's ten symbols were sprite-bound, so eight wins in ten flashed for a single frame — which is also what the earlier "not every win is showing up correctly" report was. The win beat is now bounded at BOTH ends: `WIN_BEAT_CAP_MS` (4000) remains the runaway guard, and `WIN_BEAT_MIN_MS` (650 — the same length and the same reasoning as the stacked-cell `STACKED_WIN_HOLD_MS` one branch away) is the floor. It runs CONCURRENTLY with the animation (`Promise.all`), so authored art longer than the floor still sets the pace and is unchanged — verified 2ms→655ms, 961ms→970ms, 2000ms→2005ms, never-reports→4007ms (still capped). A sprite bound to `win` is a legitimate authoring choice; it simply carries no duration of its own, so the engine supplies one. **Engine-wide by owner direction** — it re-times sprite wins on every game including the shipped Book of Borut (instant → 650ms). Reaches online games automatically: `.github/workflows/runtime-release.yml` auto-releases the `lines` runtime on any push to `main` touching `apps/lines/**` or `packages/**`.

- 2026-08-31 — **A project's authored symbol dictionary is now what the mock deals AND what it pays.** Owner report on the live `test6` (ways): the symbols set in `/config` do not match the ones in `/symbols` or the ones on the board. Measured over 15 live rounds — config declares 11, has 9 on its strips, and exactly 7 were ever dealt. Two independent causes, fixed together because either alone is useless. (1) **The wire vocabulary could not express three of them.** The captured Play4Fun set has seven line symbols to the engine's ten, so `linesMapping` covered `H1–H4`/`L1`/`L2`/`L5` and `H5`/`L3`/`L4` had NO server name — `projectLineSymbols` filters that table down and can never add, so a config that put `H5` or `L3` on its strips was silently never dealt them. `PIC8`/`PIC9`/`PIC10` are appended for exactly those three (`EXTENDED_LINE_SYMBOLS` in the mock, reachable ONLY through an explicit project pool); `PIC1–PIC7` keep their verified meaning, so Hot Fruits and Book of Borut are untouched and a real server simply never sends the new names. (2) **The project's paytable travelled for the `scatter` model alone**, so every lines/ways/cluster game paid captured Hot Fruits values whatever `/config` authored — and the newly-mapped symbols had no price row at all, since `PAY_TABLE` has none. `symbolPaytable` now rides for every model and every evaluator prefers it per-symbol (`payRowOf`), falling back to the mock's own table. Two follow-ons the change forced: the evaluators iterated the module-level `LINE_SYMBOLS` rather than the instance pool (invisible while a pool could only be a SUBSET, wrong the moment it can contain a name that list lacks) — they now take `poolOf(opts)`; and an authored paytable is bet-multipliers, routinely fractional (`test6` prices `L1` at `0.4`), so a 3-of-a-kind at a 1-cent stake paid `0.4` cents straight into `session.balance` — `payCents` rounds every payout with a one-cent floor, inert for the integer captured table. The `config`/`spinStart` events also advertise the real pool + price list instead of the module defaults. **Verified:** `node scripts/cascade-chain.fixture.mjs` gained cases driving a pool that needs the extended names (dealt, paid at authored multipliers, and the wire quoting them) and a cluster game on a SPARSE authored paytable; a byte-parity harness confirms lines/ways/cluster/scatter/wild/stacked games with no project pool and no project paytable are IDENTICAL to before (a project that STATES a pool legitimately differs: its `config`/`spinStart` events now advertise that pool and its price list rather than the module defaults, while boards, wins and payouts are unchanged); and `pnpm --filter game-config-spike run scattercrosscheck` / `wayscrosscheck` — the harnesses that assert the mock scores exactly what the RTP verifier scores — pass, which is what keeps the payout rounding at the mock's wire boundary rather than inside the shared evaluators. Simulated against `test6`'s real contract: `H5` and `L3` now deal and pay, zero fractional payouts. **⚠️ DEPLOY ORDER:** the launcher auto-deploys from `main` and will start publishing pools containing `PIC8/9/10`, which an un-updated test server would deal and an un-updated runtime could not render — deploy `services/test-server` and cut a **runtime release** together with the merge, not after it.

- 2026-08-31 — **The win meter now climbs with every win instead of jumping once at the end of the round** (`advanceWinMeter`, `apps/lines/src/game/utils.ts`). Owner report on the live `test6` (ways + tumble): a cascade narrates win after win and the Win box reads 0.00 for the whole chain. Every `winInfo` already carries `totalWin` — the round's payout INCLUDING that win, accumulated by the Play4Fun facade's `runningTotal` across a spin's several paying lines AND across every board a cascade scores — and nothing consumed it: `stateBet.winBookEventAmount` was written only by `setTotalWin`, at `gameEnd`, so the meter snapped to the total after the board had already settled. The advance sits at the `dispatchBookEvent` seam beside `recordWinCycleWins`, for the same reason: a flow-owned `winInfo` never reaches the coded handler map, and the meter must behave the same on a flow-driven game as on a coded one. Forward-only (`playBet` zeroes it per round), so a book that omits `totalWin` cannot walk it backwards; `setTotalWin` still assigns the closing figure. Side effect worth having: `winBookEventAmount` now genuinely holds the accumulated feature total DURING a free-spin outro, which is what the `totalWin` value source in `Game.svelte` already claimed. Verified offline — `cascadeCollect.fixture.ts` gained "the climbing meter lands on the round total" (1059 assertions green). **Needs a runtime release** to reach online games.

- 2026-08-31 — **A losing spin on a ways game tumbled anyway, exploding symbols that never paid** (`nativeCascade` in `scripts/mock-rgs-server.mjs`, `cascadeEnabledFor` in `services/test-server/server.mjs`). Same owner report, same game. The mock decides whether a dead spin still tumbles from `nativeCascade`, which asked WHICH WIN MODEL is in play (`cluster`/`scatter`) — a question that stopped being the right one the moment a project could author `cascade: true` in its own Game Config. A `ways` game that declared a tumble therefore fell through to the DEMO pass, which picks the most COMMON symbol on the board and blows every one of its cells away, twice, paying nothing. Measured against the live `test6` RGS: **20 of 20 dead spins tumbled**, each reading to the player as a win that paid nothing. The question is now WHO TURNED THE CASCADE ON, and the test server — the side that knows — answers it: `cascadeEnabledFor` returns `{ on, demo }` and passes `cascadeDemo` alongside `cascade`, with only the `CASCADE_GAMES` / `CASCADE=1` env override marked as a demo. An authored or protocol-implied cascade is the game's MECHANIC and no longer fires on a spin that paid nothing. Regression held by `node scripts/cascade-chain.fixture.mjs`, which gained a ways case ("an authored cascade chains" + "a dead spin never tumbles"; 1213 assertions green) and now expresses the demo path as `cascadeDemo: true`. **Needs a `services/test-server` deploy** — it is the mock RGS every launcher-hosted game plays against.

- 2026-08-27 — **The big win could not be dismissed once the count-up landed — two hold-and-tap surfaces on one presentation, and the mask runs only the top one.** Owner report on the live remake: the progressive tier-stepping works, but the tap after the total lands does nothing and the overlay sits there. Cause is the premise `#456` rested on. It arms an engine-side hold on the landing tap (`winState.awaitingDismiss`, so the total is readable instead of flashing past in the 300ms settle) and mounts a dismiss press for it, reasoning that a container owning the tap would have absorbed the landing tap already — so a hold can only exist where the gate owns the press. That is false for the arming a win container actually uses: `tapArmAfterSignal: 'winCountUpComplete'`. Its tap surface does not exist DURING the count-up — which is _why_ tier-stepping reaches `CountUpInteraction` at all — so the landing tap arms the gate's hold, and the authored tap then registers a flush later, ON TOP of the gate's dismiss press. `<ContinuePressMask>` runs the top-registered press alone, so every later tap went to the container, where it only latched a completion the flow had not reached yet, while the one press that could release the gate sat underneath it unreachable. The overlay ignored the player for the full `DISMISS_HOLD_CAP_MS` (10s), then concluded on the cap and vanished at once as the flow consumed the latch.

  - **Where the flow already holds, the gate stands down.** The remake's flow authors the same beat itself — `winUpdate` → `showContainer{awaitComplete}` → `hideContainer` — so the hold and the dismiss tap were always there; `#456` added a second, rival copy on top. `winState.flowHoldsPresentation` is the name-agnostic "a `showContainer{awaitComplete}` container is on screen" test the celebration lock already computes (`winAwaitTargets` ∩ shown), published from the same `Game.svelte` effect and read by both of `WinGate`'s landing taps. The lock keeps ORing the coded `bigWin` id; the gate deliberately does not — a v1/coded `bigWin` screen is a mounted scene that holds nothing, so folding the id in would suppress the gate's hold on exactly the path that needs it. A flow that authors no hold is untouched: the gate holds and dismisses as `#456` shipped it.
  - **Either surface can now end the hold** (`releaseWinDismissHold`, called from `TapToContinue`). Belt-and-braces under the stand-down: it makes the two presses unable to deadlock each other whichever way the registration order falls. It sets the same latch `dismissNow` does, so the outro stays skipped on a deliberate dismiss (`#295`), and leaves `awaitingDismiss` set so `WinAnimation`'s `holdOutro` does not start an exit clip in the beat the overlay is leaving.
  - Diagnosed offline against the LIVE artifacts rather than by playing for a big win: the deployed `release.json` commit (which contains `#456`), and the remake's `/api/editor/runtime` doc — its wired `setWin` Game Signals pin (⇒ `resolveWinMount` = the headless `driver`), its `showContainer{awaitComplete}` on the win container, and that container's `tapArmAfterSignal`. Pinned in `packages/engine-game/fixtures/winTapLand.fixture.ts` (`node --experimental-strip-types …`), which now models the ignored-tap window and asserts the four wiring halves against source — the timeline model alone cannot see them. ⏳ **Not browser-verified**: reproducing it needs a real big win on the live remake.
  - **Engine change on `main`** — reaches the online remake only on a `runtime:lines` republish.

- 2026-08-26 — **A project's own sounds now travel the full asset chain into a running game** (`engine-layout/bakedSounds.ts`, `EnableSound.svelte`, `LoadedAudio.format`). The catalog becomes one audio BANK per file, appended after the shipped audiosprite so a same-named project sound overrides it. Detail in [status/sound](sound.md).

- 2026-08-26 — **The sound player holds a LIST of audio banks, not one `Howl`** (`packages/utils-sound`). The precondition for a project having sounds of its own; last-wins on name, one `Howl` per bank, per-bank unload. Detail in [status/sound](sound.md).

- 2026-08-26 — **A placed FLIPBOOK clip now cover-fits like every other background art node.** Owner report: a clip dropped on a `background (cover-fit)` screen did not fill the window in the game, and stayed drag-resizable in the editor — unlike the sprite/spine sitting next to it. Cause was not the cover math but its GATE: five separate places each spelled out the cover-capable kinds as `sprite | spine` (+ `componentInstance` for the background auto-cover), so the `flipbook` kind — added later — reached none of them. A `background`-space clip fell through the editor's `nodeTransform` to the plain `game`-space mapping and through `LayoutNodeView`'s `bg` derive to its authored transform, i.e. it was treated as an ordinary sized node in both surfaces. Fix: the kind list is now the exported `isCoverFitKind` / `isCoverArtKind` pair in `coverTransform.ts` and every gate reads it (runtime `isCanvasCoverFit` + `bgTexture`/`bg`; editor `nodeTransform` ×2, `isBackgroundCover`, `isBackgroundCoverSelected`, `canCoverFit`). The clip measures its FIRST frame — the frame `EditorCanvas.naturalSize` already sizes off, so preview == game, and a cover recomputed per frame would pulse the backdrop at `fps`. `flipbookCoverRef` resolves that frame with the same scoped→bare precedence `resolveClipFrames` uses. Additive: the sprite/spine/componentInstance paths are byte-identical and the "Cover / full-screen fill" toggle now also offers itself to a clip on a flow-gated `canvas` screen. **An existing background clip that was hand-scaled to fit must have its `scale.x`/`scale.y` reset to 1** — under the cover path `node.scale` is the free per-axis STRETCH multiplied onto the fitted cover, so a 2.1× hand-scale now over-covers by 2.1×. Verify offline: `node packages/engine-layout/scripts/test-cover-fit.mjs` (now asserts the kind membership directly, so a future kind cannot be half-added again) and `pnpm --filter flipbook-spike run cover`. Reaching a live game needs a runtime release + reconcile; the editor half ships with the launcher.

- 2026-08-25 — **`<AnimatedSprite>` stopped itself one frame in, so every flipbook in the engine rendered a STILL FRAME.** PIXI's `textures` setter ends in `gotoAndStop(0)`, and `propsSyncEffect` re-assigns EVERY prop whenever ANY tracked prop changes — so the first unrelated change after mount (alpha, x, a resize, a `loadedAssets` update) froze playback, permanently, because the `play` effect only re-runs when `play` changes. `textures` is now assigned only on a real frame-list change (a CONTENT comparison — the upstream array is a `$derived` rebuilt every recompute), carrying playback across it. Affects BOTH flipbook consumers: scene `flipbook` nodes and Symbols cells. Extracted decision + fixture: `pixi-svelte/src/lib/animatedSpriteFrames.ts`, `node --experimental-strip-types packages/pixi-svelte/fixtures/animatedSpriteFrames.fixture.ts` (it reproduces the old still frame as well as pinning the fix). Diagnosed in the LIVE game rather than from the code — see [flipbook status](flipbook.md) for the reading, including the hidden-tab trap that made the first diagnosis wrong. **Needs a runtime release** to reach online games.

- 2026-08-25 — **`LayoutNode` gained a `flipbook` kind** — a placed Invisible Flipbook clip, mounted by `LayoutNodeView` as `<Flipbook>` in the shape of the `sprite` branch (size folded into `width`/`height`, per-placement `fps`/`loop` overriding the clip's own). `resolveTransform`'s SIZED + TINTED lists, `sceneDuration`'s walk (through the `flipbookMs` seam that was wired ahead of it, now taking the placement's `loop`), and the new `flipbookCycleMs` in `registerFlipbooks` are the rest of the engine half. Additive: a doc with no `flipbook` node is byte-identical. Also new here: **`LAYOUT_NODE_KINDS`**, an exported runtime list of the union's kinds — the launcher's doc normalizer now derives its accepted-kind set from it instead of hand-copying, which is what stops a future kind being silently dropped on save. **Reaching online games needs a runtime release** (the reader is in `packages/*`); standalone game repos need an engine submodule bump only — no per-game `src/` mirror. Authoring UI, editor playback and the full story: [flipbook status](flipbook.md) · [editor status](editor.md). Fixture: `pnpm --filter flipbook-spike run scene-node`.

- 2026-08-25 — **26 of the 53 sounds in every game's audiosprite had never been played by any code path, the cascade pop loudest among them — and none of them were bindable without an engine edit.** Owner report: the tumble explosion is silent, and the sounds for it are visibly there in the Flow editor's library. Both halves were true, and they were the same bug seen from two ends. The Flow dropdown is generated from `apps/lines/src/game/sound.ts`'s `SoundName` union (`soundEnums.generated.ts`), which lists NAMES; the wiring was a hardcoded string literal at each presentation beat, spread over ten files. So a moment with no literal was silent forever, and it LOOKED bound. `tumble_win_1…5` — a five-rung escalation ladder cut for exactly the cascade pop — has shipped in the audiosprite since the fork with nothing playing a rung of it; `tumble_win_5` was not even in the type union. An audit of the sprite against the runtime code found the same for the whole multiplier family, `sfx_reel_stop_2…5` (every reel played `_1`), `sfx_symbols_landing`, `sfx_royals_landing`, `sfx_scatter_reveal` and `sfx_scatter_win`.

  - **The unit of authorship is now a SLOT** — a named presentation moment — declared once in a catalogue (`packages/game-config/src/sounds.ts`): `tumbleExplosion`, `reelStop`, `symbolLand`, `royalLand`, `scatterLand`, `wildLand`, `wildExplode`. The ENGINE owns when a slot fires, the CONFIG owns what it plays, and the Invisible Symbols doc owns a per-symbol exception. Every slot ships a coded default, so a project that authors nothing gets the full sound set — **the default is inverted from every other optional block in this schema**, and deliberately: elsewhere "absent" means "what the engine did before", and here that was _nothing_, so defaulting to the old behaviour would ship the fix switched off.
  - **The catalogue stays LIVE.** `/config` stores only DEPARTURES (`normalizeSounds` drops a binding equal to the default), so a project that opens the panel, looks, and saves does not freeze its ladder to that day's copy — the same freeze the shared compiled config already inflicted on every online game once.
  - **Ladders** are indexed by a number the firing site supplies and CLAMP at the top: the cascade STEP for `tumbleExplosion` (reset each `reveal`), the REEL INDEX for `reelStop`, the running scatter count for `scatterLand`. A six-reel board plays rung 5 twice rather than restarting the climb or falling silent.
  - **Picture vs royal is decided by PAYTABLE RANK, never by symbol id** (`landSlotForSymbol`): at-or-below the median of the paying dictionary is a royal. `H*`/`L*` is a convention of the templates we ship, not a contract — a math team's `CHERRY`/`BELL` set would fall off a prefix test in silence. The same routing answers "is this a scatter" for the scatter counter, replacing a hardcoded `'S'`; `SCATTER_LAND_SOUND_MAP` is deleted.
  - **The pop fires once per cascade STEP, not per exploding cell**, and stands down for the board CLEAR. `tumbleBoardExplode` serves two moments that look identical — the cascade removing cells that paid, and `clearOutgoingSymbols` emptying the board before a drop-in reveal — and in the second nothing has won. The clear only runs under a `reveal`, so "is the event being dispatched right now a `tumbleBoard`" separates them exactly, with no new field on the cue and nothing for a flow author to pass. That rule lives in `apps/lines/src/game/cascadeSoundStep.ts`, import-free so it is exercisable offline; `trackCascadeStep` is called from `dispatchBookEvent`, the one seam all three dispatch paths cross (the same reason `recordWinCycleWins` lives there) — a flow-owned `tumbleBoard` never reaches the coded handler map.
  - Files: new `game-config/src/sounds.ts` + `sounds` on `GameConfigDoc`; `engine-game` `activeSounds()` + a `sounds` dep on `createGameState` driving the reel-stop ladder; new `apps/lines/src/game/soundBindings.ts` (the one resolution point, and the one cast between authored `string` names and the compile-time `SoundEffectName` union); `symbolSounds` through the Symbols doc → export → bake → `bakedSymbolSounds()`. `tumble_win_5` added to the union and the Flow enums regenerated.
  - **Verified live** (`apps/lines` + mock RGS, `Howl.prototype.play` instrumented in a real Chrome tab): one spin now plays `sfx_reel_stop_1,2,3,4,5` in order plus `sfx_royals_landing`, `sfx_symbols_landing` and `sfx_scatter_stop_1` — four of those had never played at all, and the ladder previously played rung 1 five times. Plus 36 offline claims in `packages/game-config/sounds.fixture.ts`, 12 in `apps/lines/src/game/cascadeSoundStep.fixture.ts`, and 13 doc-contract claims in `apps/launcher-api/scripts/check-sound-bindings.ts` (`pnpm --filter launcher-api check:sound-bindings`, which asserts the SAVE path's whitelist rebuild rather than only the Zod parse — the launcher build type-checks nothing). ⏳ **The cascade pop itself is not browser-verified**: no mock emits `tumbleBoard`, and `apps/lines` does not cascade. Its gate is covered by the fixture; the audible check belongs on a cascading project.
  - **Engine change on `main`** — reaches an online/remake game only on a `runtime:lines` republish + `engine` submodule bump.

- 2026-08-25 — **A published build shipped tens of MB of atlas pages no atlas referenced, because the puller's prune skipped the one subtree that churns every export.** `pull-project-assets.mjs` mirrors R2 `deploy/` into `static/assets/` and then prunes the export-generated subtrees back to the listing — but the list was `editor-art`, `editor-fonts`, `editor-symbols`, `effects`, `clips`, and NOT `_pages`. `PageStore` writes every shared atlas page content-addressed (`_pages/<hash>.<ext>` + its `.ktx2` twin), so a re-pack or a re-encode lands the page at a NEW hash and R2 drops the old one — while the superseded LOCAL copy simply stayed. Nothing else removes it: `_pages/` is untracked, so the launcher's pre-build `git reset --hard origin/main` leaves it, and `adapter-static` then copies all of `static/` into `build/` verbatim. Stale pages accumulated one generation per pull and every one of them shipped.

  **`_pages` and `_boot` joined `GENERATED_SUBTREES`.** Both are 100% export-written (`PageStore`, `bootSplashExport`) and neither is ever committed by a game, so converging them on the deploy listing is the same safe operation the other five already get — and the mirror runs BEFORE the prune, so the current generation lands first. Measured on the Book of Borut Remake checkout: the prune goes from 27.3 MB (17 files) to 84.8 MB (56 files) — **57.5 MB of dead pages that used to ship**.

  Worth knowing about the rest of the tree: this closes a stale-file leak, NOT a general "only ship what the game uses" guarantee. Reachability filtering exists for editor art, symbols and FX effects (and Rollup tree-shakes the JS), but the puller mirrors `deploy/` verbatim, fonts export the whole catalog, flipbook clips ship in full by design, and anything committed under a game's `static/` ships whether or not the game mounts it. The 🗜 Optimize pass trims format twins and spare audio formats but deliberately only REPORTS art referenced by no descriptor.

- 2026-08-25 — **The tap on the LAST big-win tier dismissed the presentation instead of landing on it, so the player never saw the amount they had just tapped to see.** Owner report on the live remake, and the other half of the `#282` tap grammar: mid-chain a tap means "next tier, faster"; on the FINAL tier there is nothing left to step to, so it meant "slam and go". `#295` had deliberately armed the outro-skip there — the LAST tap used to sit through the full tier outro before hiding — which left the whole appearance of the total to the fixed `roundSkip.wait(300)` settle. The number arrived and vanished in the same beat.

  **A tap on the final tier now only LANDS.** `resolveWinTap` (new, `packages/engine-game/src/game/winEscalation.ts`, beside `tierHasExit`) is the one rule: step while a next tier exists, otherwise land — and `hold` on the land is simply whether a chain is presenting. On a hold the gate sets `winState.awaitingDismiss`, `WinAnimation` keeps the final tier's idle looping instead of flipping to its outro (a new `holdOutro`, which returns BEFORE `chainConcluded` so the collapse still runs untouched if the hold lapses), and `concludePresentation` waits. The DISMISS press then behaves exactly as `#295` made it: it sets `escalationOutroComplete`, so the overlay hides instantly with the outro skipped. Two taps, each doing one thing.

  **The hold is bounded, by a cap sized for a human rather than for art.** `waitForEscalationOutro` now picks `DISMISS_HOLD_CAP_MS` (10000) over `ESCALATION_OUTRO_CAP_MS` (4000) when it is waiting on a person, and skips the mis-authored-outro warning in that case — a lapsed hold is a player who walked away, not a broken rig. Ten seconds is far longer than reading a number takes and still short of the tier idle's own 12000ms loop.

  **Headless (`resolveWinMount` ⇒ `'driver'`, the flow owning `setWin` — what the remake and `test6` run) holds too**, which is the only reason this reaches a real game; the post-count-up `<PressToContinue hidePrompt>` mounts for it while the hold is up. That is not a second tap surface competing with an authored container: a container's own `tapToContinue` registers a continue press, which mounts the canvas-top `<ContinuePressMask>` and absorbs the tap wherever it lands — so where a container owns the tap, `CountUpInteraction` never sees one, no tap ever lands the count, and no hold is ever armed. Only a presentation whose taps actually reach the gate can hold, which is exactly the presentation the gate can also dismiss.

  **And the other half of a step was dead on every project that authors its config.** Found while verifying the above, on `test6`: a step is two things — the walk jumps to the tapped tier's ART, and the COUNT is sought to that tier's amount — and the second half had never run on an authored game. `WinVisual` derives each tier's seek target as `threshold × BOOK_AMOUNT_MULTIPLIER` off the presentation object, but `tierToWinLevelData` (the authored-config projection, `game/gameConfig.ts`) did not carry `threshold` through, so `?? 0` gave `escalationBoundaries: [0,0,0,0,0]`. Nothing failed and nothing logged: `jumpTo` clamps FORWARD ONLY, so seeking to 0 is a no-op — the tap stepped the tier art while the number carried on counting from wherever it was. The coded `winLevelMap` rows carry a threshold, which is exactly why the un-authored dev games never showed it and only real projects did. `tierToWinLevelData` now carries it, and `WinLevelData.threshold`'s doc no longer says a runtime-built tier omits it.

  **Verified:** `packages/engine-game/fixtures/winTapLand.fixture.ts` (new, `node --experimental-strip-types …`) drives a 5-tier chain through the real extracted decision imported from source and asserts BEFORE/AFTER — the old landing tap leaves the total on screen for **300ms**, the new one holds it until the dismiss tap; stepping is identical tap-for-tap with the hold on or off; a deliberate dismiss still skips the outro; an abandoned hold concludes on the dismiss cap; and an un-escalating win is byte-identical (`{kind:'land', hold:false}` — a plain win has no tiers and nothing to look at, so holding it would only make small wins sticky). For the seek target it models `jumpTo`'s forward-only clamp — a dropped threshold zeroes every boundary and leaves the count at 4891, a carried one snaps it to 8000 — and then asserts the WIRE against the SOURCE (`scripts/verify-stepped-grid.mjs` §6's technique), because the projection is a closure inside `createGameConfig` and the derivation is a Svelte template: neither can be imported and executed from Node, and re-dropping the field would leave every data-level assertion green. Negative-tested — deleting `threshold: tier.threshold` fails the fixture. `apps/lines` builds, the touched files lint clean, and the pre-existing `winEscalationOutro` fixture still passes. **NOT verified in a browser:** the escalation path was reached live in `apps/lines` against the mock (a 100x MAX-WIN spin, `escalationActive` with 5 tiers, taps stepping the walk 0→1→2→3) but the final-tier tap itself was never exercised — the automation browser leaves the tab hidden, so rAF is throttled to ~1 frame per 10s and the count-up never runs unattended. One live spin on the remake would close it.

- 2026-08-24 — **A big win stepped its tiers but would not dismiss: the escalation waited on an outro `complete` that could never arrive, with no tap surface left to cut it short.** Owner report on the Book of Borut remake. The engine had not regressed — `release.json` showed the live shared bundle at `main` HEAD, and `CountUpInteraction.svelte` / `WinAnimation.svelte` were byte-identical to the `#295` state verified working on 2026-08-11. The cause was in the authored data hitting two holes in the engine at once.

  **Hole 1 — an outro authored as the idle never restarts the track, so the round waits out the idle instead.** The remake points every tier's outro at that tier's own idle clip (`bigOutro: Tier1_Idle`, `superwinOutro: Tier2_Idle`, `megaOutro: Tier3_Idle`) — what an author does when the rig has no exit and the dropdown insists on a name. `<SpineTrack>` applies by VALUE (`shouldApplySpineAnimation`: `animationName !== track.animationName`), so the idle→outro switch was a no-op: the running entry kept going and its `complete` arrived at the end of the OLD cycle. Measured on the reference `bigwin` rig, that is not a rounding error — every `*_win_exit` is **467ms** and every `*_win_idle` is **12000ms**. The gate sat blocked for a full idle cycle after the number had landed.

  Two fixes, because they are two different mistakes. `WinAnimation` now passes `<SpineTrack>` a **re-apply token** (`stepIndex * 3 + phase`, injective over the walk) so any transition restarts the track even when the next phase names the clip already playing — this also fixes the general case of two adjacent tiers sharing a clip, where the second tier silently never played. And the "is there an exit at all" rule moved to **`packages/engine-game/src/game/winEscalation.ts`** (`tierHasExit`), which now treats an outro equal to that tier's idle as NO exit, exactly like the empty name the old guard already covered: the chain reports done immediately and leaves the idle on screen until the overlay hides, rather than pretending an exit is playing.

  **Hole 2 — the flow path had no dismiss surface after the count-up.** `WinGate` mounts `<CountUpInteraction>` only `{#if !countUpCompleted}` and suppresses the coded `<PressToContinue>` under v2 (`codedPressOwned`), so between the count landing and the outro finishing there was NOTHING to tap — `dismissNow()` (`#295`) existed but was wired only to the press that never mounts there. It now mounts `<PressToContinue hidePrompt>` in that window when `!headless`, which is the ownership line `resolveWinMount` already draws (headless ⇒ the flow owns `setWin` and the authored container owns dim/art/tap). `PressToContinue` rather than another `CountUpInteraction` deliberately: it registers with `registerContinuePress`, so the canvas-top `<ContinuePressMask>` mounts and absorbs the tap wherever the pointer rests — including over live HUD chrome, which hit-tests above the overlay and would otherwise swallow it (the `#400` narrowing). `hidePrompt` keeps the coded `MM_pressanywhere` sprite off an authored screen, which was the only reason the press was suppressed there.

  **And the wait itself is now bounded.** `waitForEscalationOutro` races `ESCALATION_OUTRO_CAP_MS` (4000) and warns when it blows — the same runaway-guard doctrine `game/symbolBeat.ts` applies to the win beat, for the same reason: it BLOCKS THE ROUND. Sized off the art like `WIN_BEAT_CAP_MS`: ~8.5× a real 467ms exit, still a third of an idle cycle. The race settles through one guarded `settle()` so the loser cannot resolve twice, cannot warn about an outro that did land, and cannot leave the watcher `$effect.root` running for the session on every capped win.

  **Verified:** `packages/engine-game/fixtures/winEscalationOutro.fixture.ts` (`node --experimental-strip-types …`) drives the remake's real authoring through both extracted decisions, imported from source, and asserts BEFORE/AFTER — the old path blocks **12000ms** and never re-applies the same-named outro, the new one blocks **0ms** with the walk itself unchanged; a rig with real exits plays byte-identical animations and still ends on its **467ms** exit; and a shared-clip tier that the old track skipped entirely now plays. `apps/lines` builds, `engine-game` lints clean, the touched files lint + prettier clean, the pre-existing `spineTrackReplay` fixture still passes. Live in `apps/lines` against the mock: boots clean and **no `effect_update_depth_exceeded`** — the `#396` failure class these new effects could have reintroduced (`chainConcluded` is a plain `let`, not `$state`, for exactly that reason).

  **NOT live-verified end to end.** The Browser pane reports `document.hidden` even when fronted, so rAF is frozen, `FadeContainer`'s alpha tween never opens, and the win overlay's count-up cannot run — the same wall `#442` documented. The flow-path branch (Hole 2) additionally needs a v2 doc that drives screens, i.e. the remake's project token. Worth one confirming mega win on the remake after the runtime release.

  **The authoring is still worth fixing too:** the remake's tiers should point their outro at a real exit clip (or leave it empty). The engine now behaves correctly either way, but an authored exit is what makes the celebration actually play out.

- 2026-08-24 — **`createInterruptible().add()` could strand its promise forever — a latent stuck win presentation / reel.** `packages/utils-shared/interruptible.ts` passed an `async` function as a `Promise` executor (ESLint `no-async-promise-executor`). A rejection from `targetToWait()` inside an async executor is NOT forwarded to `reject` — it became an unhandled rejection and the returned promise never settled, so every caller awaiting it hung until (and unless) an `interrupt()` happened to arrive. The four call sites — `WinCountUpProvider` (count-up + the value-reached settle), `OnHotkey` (hold-to-fast-forward), `createReelForSpinning` (slide-down), `createReelForCascading` (fall-in delay) — all simply continue the sequence afterwards and none catches, so the failure mode was a permanently frozen presentation. Fixed by dropping the async executor and chaining: the wait now ALWAYS settles, a throwing target settles as `{ interrupted: false }` (the wait is over; nothing cut it short) and the error is `console.error`'d rather than swallowed. **Rejecting was considered and rejected**: no call site catches, so a rejection would leave the sequence equally stuck, only louder. `targetToWait` is still invoked SYNCHRONOUSLY inside `add()`, so animation timing is unchanged. Behaviourally inert today — no current target can reject (`waitForTimeout` and svelte `Tween.set` never do) — this is a guard for the next one that can. Verified: clean under `no-async-promise-executor`, prettier + `tsc --strict` clean, a 5-case harness over the compiled helper (normal / interrupt / async rejection / sync throw / late-interrupt-after-settle) with the old code proven to HANG on the rejection case, `pnpm --filter lines build` green, and a live A/B in `apps/lines` against the mock RGS (stock vs fixed: identical — bet placed, balance debited, zero console errors).

  **Written 2026-08-19 and never merged** — it sat on an un-PR'd branch until a branch audit found it.

- 2026-08-24 — **The swap-in-place fixture had stopped asserting anything: it crashed on a slice marker before its first check, so the cascade contract it exists to protect was unguarded on `main`.** `node scripts/verify-swap-in-place-mode.mjs` threw `could not find "\tconst CASCADE_BEAT_CAP_MS ="`. Nothing was renamed — the constant was EXTRACTED. #442 moved the race and the cap into `game/symbolBeat.ts` (`TRANSIT_BEAT_CAP_MS`), and the marker went with it.

  **Two extractions had broken it, and the loud one hid the quiet one.** Re-pointing the marker got the slice evaluating again and immediately surfaced the earlier breakage: #443's `PAD_ROWS_ABOVE` had left the component for `game/tumbleBoardLayout.ts` too, so the slice referenced an undefined binding. The fixture had therefore been dead since #443, and #442 only changed which line it died on.

  Both modules are now SLICED rather than restated — `awaitSymbolBeat` + `TRANSIT_BEAT_CAP_MS`, and `PAD_ROWS_ABOVE` + `combineTumbleReel` — so the fixture still drives the shipped race against the shipped 650ms and stacks each column by the shipped rule. `combineTumbleReel` especially: a fixture holding its own copy of that rule would assert the settle contract cell for cell against a board the shipped one no longer produces, which is exactly the bug #443 fixed. All five names joined the declared-names guard, and the annotation guard grew `T` for the generic.

  **Two checks were genuinely wrong and were updated, not silenced.** They encoded the pre-#443 stacking (`adding` at index 0); the shipped column is `[pad, …refills…, …survivors…, pad]`. They now walk it top-down and pin the pad's position, which is the fact that regressed.

  **412 checks, exit 0 — and mutation-tested, since a fixture that only stops throwing proves nothing.** Renaming `TRANSIT_BEAT_CAP_MS` or `awaitBeat` at the source fails loudly with the named marker; `PAD_ROWS_ABOVE = 2` fails 2 checks; reverting `combineTumbleReel` to `[...adding, ...base]` fails the two stacking checks. The other six `verify-*.mjs` fixtures were run and all pass — this was the only rotted one.

- 2026-08-21 — **The win beat awaited an animation that a symbol is not obliged to play, so an ordinary paying spin could freeze the round outright.** `Board.svelte`'s `boardWithAnimateSymbols` set each lit cell to `win` and awaited its `oncomplete` with no bound. But a symbol only reports completion when its state actually PLAYS something: `SymbolSprite` fires from an `$effect` on `symbolInfo`, `Symbol.svelte` renders NOTHING when the state resolves to no art (`missingArt`), `SymbolWrap` mounts nothing for a seat out of frame or on the other layer, and a spine whose bound `animationName` is absent — or loops — never fires `complete`. Any one of those turns the beat's `Promise.all` into a promise that never settles: `winInfo` never finishes, the spin button stays disabled, and the game reads as frozen. Only a slam released it, and only incidentally — `awaitCue` races the round-skip token, which is the sole thing that bounded the wait.

  **Reproduced live**, running `apps/lines` against the mock: a 4-of-a-kind held its lit frames and the `$100.00` stamp on screen with `Win $0.00` and STOP still enabled, indefinitely.

  The fix is the guard the cascade already had. `TumbleBoard` raced its explode/land beats against `CASCADE_BEAT_CAP_MS` and `MultiplierBoard` raced its collect against a second, separately-declared copy of the same 650ms — while the win beat, the one every online game hits on every paying spin, had none. All three now go through **`apps/lines/src/game/symbolBeat.ts`**: one `awaitSymbolBeat(arm, capMs)` race, and the caps named by ROLE rather than per-component — `TRANSIT_BEAT_CAP_MS` (650) for a beat that is a step on the way somewhere, `WIN_BEAT_CAP_MS` (4000) for the win animation itself. `Board.svelte`'s existing `STACKED_WIN_HOLD_MS` stays as it was: a covered cell provably mounts no `<Symbol>`, so it holds a fixed beat rather than racing one.

  **Why the win cap is 4000 and not 650.** A cap short enough to trim an authored win animation would silently re-time every shipped game, and this ships in the shared `_runtime/lines` bundle. Measured across the reference symbol spines (`static/assets/spines/symbols{,2,3}`, 51 animations): the longest of any kind is exactly **2000ms** — every picture/royal win, every multiplier pay, the wild — and `timeScale` is only ever 1 or 2, so turbo makes them faster, never slower. 4000ms is double the longest authored beat. A truncated animation is the worse failure of the two: silent, and on every paying spin. A blown guard is loud, rare and diagnosable.

  **Verified against the loaded module in the browser:** a beat whose animation reports at 2000ms resolves in **2012ms** (the animation wins — pacing is unchanged), and a cell that can never report resolves in **4018ms** instead of never. The running game logged 14 real win beats through the same helper, each bounded, where before each was a hang. `apps/lines` builds; eslint + prettier clean.

- 2026-08-21 — **After a tumble the client was showing a DIFFERENT board from the one the server scored, so the next step's win frames landed on symbols that never paid.** Owner reported it on a ways game (`test3`): a cascade pays, the refills fall, and the frames that come back sit on mismatched symbols on non-adjacent reels, with a row that looks like it belongs to the previous spin still in the grid. This is the SAME report that was closed on 2026-08-21 as the 12-step cap (below) — that entry says "the positions were correct", and they were: every step's cells were checked against the board **the server** produced. Nobody ever checked the board the CLIENT ended up with.

  **The cause is one line of stacking.** `tumbleBoardCombined` merged the two cascade layers as `[...adding, ...base]`. A cascade never explodes a padding row — the server only indexes visible cells — so BOTH pads survive into `base`, and stacking the refills on top of the whole thing left the column as `[…new…, pad, …survivors…, pad]` with the top pad stranded in the middle. The refills sit one row too high, the survivors one row too low, and the stranded pad is a stale duplicate of the pre-tumble top row sitting inside the visible window. It only shows on reels that actually lost cells, which is why it reads as "some wins wrong, some missing" rather than as a board-wide shift. Two more failures rode along on it: the slide aimed the refills one row too high (pushing the topmost out of the window), and `tumbleBoardSlideDown`'s "visible rows only" guard (`index > 0 && index < length - 1`) skipped a real symbol's `land` and played one for the pad.

  The rule and the padding contract it turns on now live in one place, `apps/lines/src/game/tumbleBoardLayout.ts` — `PAD_ROWS_ABOVE` plus `combineTumbleReel`, which splices the refills in BELOW the top pad. `TumbleBoard`'s seat offset reads the same constant, so the two cannot drift apart again. A column with no survivors (the drop-in / column-cascade reveals, `keepBase: false`) reduces to the adding layer exactly as before.

  **It is a plain TS module on purpose: a rune-bearing `.svelte.ts` cannot be imported by a Node fixture, and that is precisely why this shipped wrong for months.** `cascadeBoard.fixture.ts` now drives the real mock, the real facade (`padReel` + the +1 row shift) and the real `combineTumbleReel` over 60 ways rounds, walking each round IN BOOK ORDER and asserting, at every `winInfo` after a tumble, that the client is showing the symbol the server says paid there — plus that a step never changes the board's shape and that no win lands on a padding row. **179 assertions across 100 tumble steps, green; 29 of them FAIL on the old stacking**, so it is a regression test rather than a description. A whole-board parity sweep over 525 steps put it beyond doubt: the old rule was wrong on **525 of 525**, the new one on none. Only one line is restated in the fixture (the exploded-cell filter, which lives inside a Svelte component); everything either side of it is the shipped code.

  eslint + prettier clean. **Needs a runtime release** — it is client-side, so it reaches the online games via the auto-release on merge to `main`, not via a test-server deploy.

- 2026-08-21 — **A scatter pay dimmed the WHOLE board and lit none of it.** Owner reported a cluster game going dark on a win with no win overlay. The dim is real and correct machinery (`/symbols` → win cycle → "Dim non-winning symbols"): it lights the paying cells and darkens the rest. The paying cells were empty.

  **The mock stripped them.** `evaluateScatters` was the ONLY evaluator that wrapped its cells — `context: { positions }` where `evaluateClusters` / `evaluateWays` / `evaluateScatterPays` and the whole book mock emit a bare array. The facade's `winPositions` reads `Array.isArray(context)` then `context.payline`, so the wrapper matched neither and every SCAT win reached the client with `positions: []`.

  **The wrapper was load-bearing, which is why it survived.** It was chosen so `payingCells` — the cells a cascade blows up — would skip the scatters, and its comment said exactly that: "the SCAT trigger win carries neither shape, so it contributes nothing". It did the job. It just did it by making the win unreadable to everyone, the client included. The exclusion now lives in `payingCells` keyed off `mode === 'scatter' && what === 'SCAT'` — what the win IS, not what its context looks like — and the positions go out as a bare array.

  **Three things were broken at once, and only one was visible.** With no positions: the win-dim set was empty, so on a scatter-ONLY paying spin `setWinDim(true, {})` darkened all 36 cells and lit zero; `boardWithAnimateSymbols` got no cells, so no symbol entered its `win` state and no highlight drew; and `scatterTriggerPositions` was empty, so a free-spin TRIGGER animated no scatters either. A cluster game surfaced it first because scatter-only paying spins are ordinary there and there is no payline to carry the set — on a lines game a line win almost always covered for it.

  **Guarded engine-side too:** `refreshWinDim` now returns instead of activating on an empty lit set. Dimming 100% of the board is never a correct win presentation, whatever sent the win.

  Verified offline against the real mock over the real reader: pre-fix, 8/8 scatter rounds arrived with 0 positions and 7 of them would have darkened the entire board; post-fix all 8 carry their cells. Cascade safety re-checked over 93 tumble steps — no SCAT cell is ever blown off the board. **Reaches the live test server on the next deploy from `main`;** the `refreshWinDim` guard needs a Runtime release.

- 2026-08-21 — **The cascade's 12-step cap was shaping the game, so a chain that would have settled came to rest still paying.** Owner reported the win frames looking wrong after a tumble: frames on mixed symbols, on a board that had stopped. The positions were correct — every step's cells were verified against the board that step produced — and the cause was the cap. A cascade ends when a board pays nothing; a cap low enough to be hit ends it somewhere else, and the somewhere-else is the one state a cascade must never rest in.

  **The measurement that settled it:** a mock configured 8x8 with `minCount 4` runs **40 tumbles and then settles on its own**. The old cap cut that off at 12 — truncating a chain that had a natural end, three times too early. So the bound was never protecting against runaway chains in any real sense; it was cutting normal ones short.

  `CASCADE_MAX_STEPS = 12` becomes **`CASCADE_RUNAWAY_GUARD = 200`**: a bound that stops an infinite loop and nothing else. Reaching it is no longer reported as a truncation but as what it is — a MATH problem — naming `scattermath` as the tool that measures it. A cap cannot fix broken math, it can only hide it, and hiding it is what cost a day of hunting a presentation bug that was a config bug.

  **The guard path is now tested**, which matters because it is the one branch that only runs when a config is broken and is therefore the most likely to rot unnoticed: `cascade-chain.fixture.mjs` stands up a mock with two symbols on a 64-cell board and a threshold of 4 — every refill pays again, forever — and asserts the round TERMINATES (200 steps, ~30ms) rather than looping. A mock that loops there takes the test server down for every game on it.

  The verifier keeps its OWN, lower bound (`RUNAWAY_AT = 25`) and says so: following the engine's 200 would spend 200 tumbles per spin on exactly the configs it exists to condemn, and report "average chain 200" where "these never settle" is the useful sentence. On the committed template: 1.13 tumbles average, 0.00% still paying at 25. On `test5`: 24.62 average, **88.98%** still paying at 25.

  700 chain assertions green; eslint clean. **Reaches the live test server on the next deploy from `main`;** no runtime release is needed (the mock is server-side).

- 2026-08-21 — **A symbol with no art took the whole board down, and the multiplier work walked straight into it.** Owner's live `test5` threw `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'static')` from inside a render and the reels vanished. Cause: `getSymbolInfo` indexes `map[rawSymbol.name][state]` with no guard, so a name the symbol map has never heard of throws mid-render — and an exception thrown while rendering unmounts the board, so the player loses the REELS over one cell.

  **The gap is reachable by ordinary authoring**, which is what makes this worth fixing rather than avoiding. `getActiveSymbolInfoMap` merges the coded map with the baked overrides as a UNION of both key sets, so a config can name a symbol — a multiplier, a new picture — and have it dealt long before anyone binds art for it in `/symbols`. `warnOnGameConfigIssues` has always REPORTED this at boot as an error; it was detected and not survivable, which is the worst of both.

  **Now it renders nothing and warns once** (naming the symbol), and `Symbol.svelte` takes a `missingArt` branch before the `{:else}` arm — which is the SPINE renderer, so falling through would have handed it an undefined bundle: the same crash one layer down. A value the symbol carries (a multiplier) still draws, because that is the part the player needs to read.

  **What put a nameless symbol on the board was #411's own gate.** It asked the Game Config whether a multiplier symbol was declared and IN PLAY, and stopped there. `test5` declares `M` on its strips with no art behind it, so every `MULT` cell the collect fixture dealt was a cell the project could not draw. The publish gate now also requires the symbols doc to bind `M.static` — the exact state a resting board renders and the exact one that threw. The engine no longer crashes either way, but dealing an invisible symbol is still wrong: a blank cell that pays is worse than no cell at all.

  **The lesson is about the SHAPE of the gate.** "Is this a multiplier game?" was answered from the math contract alone, when the question the mock actually needed answered was "can this project SHOW a multiplier?" — a fact that lives in a different doc. Config-declares and art-exists are two different truths, and a feature that deals a symbol needs both. Verified: `svelte-check` reports no errors in `utils.ts`, `Symbol.svelte` or any `getSymbolInfo` consumer (the 182 it does report are pre-existing `static/assets/**` debt); `apps/lines` and `launcher-api` both build clean. ⏳ Reaches the live game on the Runtime release that merging this fires.

- 2026-08-21 — **The cascade only tumbled ONCE, so a board that still paid just sat there.** Owner spun the live `test5`, screenshotted the settled board and counted it: 10 miners and 8 pickaxes on a 6x5 grid with `minCount: 8`. Two more wins, and nothing happened. Correct reading — `cascadeSteps` exploded the dealt board's winners and stopped, and its own comment said so ("the mock does not re-evaluate the refilled board").

  **That was a defect, not a simplification — but only since yesterday.** The one-shot was right while the cascade was a presentation fixture: the mock paid PAYLINES and could not score a cluster or scatter board at all, so re-scoring would have blown up cells nothing paid on, which is the one thing the fixture was careful never to do. `evaluateClusters` / `evaluateScatterPays` landed on 2026-08-20 and made the refusal obsolete the same day. The lesson worth keeping: a deliberate limitation outlives the reason for it silently, because the comment explaining it still reads as sound.

  **Now it chains.** `evaluatePayWins` is the spin's win-model switch, lifted out so the loop cannot drift from it (a second implementation of "what pays" is how a mock stops describing the game), and `cascadeSteps` runs evaluate → explode → refill until a board pays nothing, capped at `CASCADE_MAX_STEPS` (12) with a WARNING rather than a silent truncation. The free-spin trigger is deliberately NOT re-scored: whether scatters landing mid-cascade retrigger is game math no capture has answered, and quietly saying yes would change how often the bonus fires.

  **Each step carries the wins of the board it PRODUCED**, not the ones it destroyed — which is what lets the facade narrate each payout on the board it belongs to. The dealt board's wins ride the ordinary spin flush; every later board's wins are flushed straight after the tumble that revealed it, reusing `flushWins` so `runningTotal` climbs across the whole chain instead of resetting per step. Getting this backwards would draw step 3's win frame over the board step 1 was still showing — the #404 failure, one layer up.

  **A dead spin no longer tumbles on a cluster/scatter game.** The most-common-symbol fallback explodes cells nothing paid on; that is fine as a DEMO for a lines game whose cascade was forced on via `CASCADE_GAMES` (the reason the fixture exists), and wrong for a game whose cascade is its mechanic — a real tumble game whose spin paid nothing just sits there. Gated on `nativeCascade`, mirroring `CASCADE_PROTOCOLS` in the test server. Those steps are marked `demo: true` and carry the round's running total rather than 0, because a SCAT-trigger-only win names no cells, cascades nothing, and would otherwise step the meter backwards.

  **The round pays the whole chain**, and the multiplier collect multiplies the whole chain rather than just the dealt board — which is also what makes that beat feel like a payoff.

  Verified: **745 mock-level assertions** over three configurations (a 6x5 `minCount: 8` scatter matching the owner's board, a lines demo, and a scatter with the cascade off) pinning that every chain ENDS on a board that pays nothing, that `runningWin` accumulates step by step, that a step only exists because its predecessor paid, that each reel refills exactly what it lost, and that `gameEnd` pays the chain (times the collect). 60 scatter rounds produced 20 multi-step chains, max depth 3, and **zero** tumbles on a losing spin. Plus the facade fixture extended to **1048 assertions**, adding that nothing pays after the final tumble (which is WHY the chain stopped) and that the win meter never steps back mid-chain. ⚠️ This materially changes the mock's payout distribution — chained wins pay far more than single ones — and cascade RTP is chain-dependent with nothing in the repo verifying it. Still a test approximation, just a far more realistic-looking one.

- 2026-08-21 — **Scatter's multiplier collect has a wire at last — and the facade path that carried it had never once been executed.** #377 shipped the collect PRESENTATION (`MultiplierBoard`, the `boardMultiplierInfo` handler, the six cues) against nothing: no mock emitted the event and no facade case translated one, so the beat was unreachable in exactly the way the cascade overlay was before #385. Now it runs end to end.

  **The mock lands multipliers where a scatter game actually lands them — in the REFILLS.** A cascade removes the winners and drops new cells in; some of those carry a value. Landing them on the initial reveal instead would have collected before anything tumbled, which is not the mechanic. When the chain ends, `collectStep` reads the board the cascade FINISHED on and emits `multiplierCollect`. Reading the final board rather than accumulating as it goes is what makes the event agree with `multiplierBoardInit`, which re-reads the settled board rather than trusting the positions it was sent — two independent reads of the same thing, so a disagreement would be a visible bug rather than a silent one.

  **Three gates, all facts rather than flags:** the win model is `scatter`, the cascade is on (no tumble ⇒ nowhere for a multiplier to land), and the PROJECT declares a multiplier symbol IN PLAY — `special_properties` contains `multiplier` and the symbol is on a strip. That last one reuses the `projectWild` shape exactly, and exists so a project with no multiplier art never has blank cells dealt at it. The committed `scatter.json` template already ships `M` as a multiplier symbol ON the strips, so a project seeded from it collects with nothing to configure.

  **The wire encodes the value ON the cell** (`MULT:5`), because the board is a `string[][]` and a refill moves rows: a side table of positions would have to be kept in step with every drop and would desync the first time one moved. ⚠️ Ours, not captured — same standing as `tumbleStep`, labelled at both ends, and `MULT` is declared in the boot `config` only when the fixture can deal it so the facade's unknown-symbol warning stays meaningful everywhere else.

  **The round pays what the beat announced.** `roundWin` is now separate from `totalWin` (what the wins add up to) so the two cannot drift — the meter, `gameEnd`, `gameRoundOver` and the balance all read the one number. On the facade side `runningTotal` moves with it, in BOOK-EVENT units rather than cents; that counter is fixed-point bet-multipliers and assigning cents to it would have ended the round on a figure ~10,000x wrong while every internal consistency check still passed.

  **The real finding: `packages/rgs-translator-eagaming` had no test harness at all.** #385 recorded its own translation as "prettier- and eslint-clean but never executed" — `apps/lines` does not import the package, so no build compiles those cases. `cascadeCollect.fixture.ts` closes it by standing the REAL mock on a port and driving the REAL public facade API (`requestAuthenticate` → `requestBet`) against it over 40 rounds, asserting on `round.state`: **512 assertions**, covering that a `MULT:5` cell arrives as a mapped name plus a value (never as a symbol literally called `MULT:5`), that `boardMult` is the sum of what it collected, that `totalWin` keeps the tumbleWin×boardMult RATIO through the cents→engine conversion (a skipped conversion stays self-consistent, so only the ratio catches it), that positions are shifted into PADDED board space, and that the collect falls after the last tumble and before the round total. No hand-written event stream anywhere in it — the two ends cannot agree in a fixture and disagree in production.

  Also verified: 40-spin mock-level probe (26 collects; multipliers in refills only, never on the reveal; `gameEnd`/`gameRoundOver` paying the multiplied total), a scatter project WITHOUT a declared multiplier symbol collecting nothing and never being dealt one, a cluster project ignoring the flag, and the #409 cascade truth table unregressed. ⏳ **Not browser-verified** — the beat needs a project with `M` art authored in `/symbols`; with none, the cells fly but render nothing.

- 2026-08-21 — **A scatter game did not tumble, and the reason was that we asked an env var to know what the game type already says.** Owner spun the live `test5` (scatter) and reported the defining behaviour missing: winners should leave and the symbols above should fall into the gap. Probing the live RGS settled it in one spin — `bet → gameStart → spinStart → playedSpin → gameEnd → gameRoundOver`, a `PIC6 count=9 mode=scatterPays pay=24` win, and **zero `tumbleStep` events** on winning and losing spins alike (the fixture's no-win fallback would have emitted some either way). So the mock's scatter EVALUATOR was working perfectly — `evaluateScatterPays` priced a count-anywhere win off the project's own table — and nothing cascaded because `CASCADE_GAMES` still named only `test4`.

  **The gate was the wrong shape, not mis-set.** `CASCADE_GAMES` was built (#385) to stop one process cascading the shipped Book of Borut, and per-game was the right answer to _that_ question. But a `cluster`/`scatter` game IS a tumble game — a cascade is its mechanic, not a fixture — so requiring an env var per project meant every new game of those types shipped visibly broken and waited for someone to remember. **`CASCADE_PROTOCOLS = {cluster, scatter}` now decides by default**, which keeps every bit of the safety the allowlist was for: `lines` and `book` still cannot cascade by accident, with no env set at all. `CASCADE_GAMES` survives as the override for the protocols that do not cascade on their own — the way to give a lines game the tumble overlay. A future tumble type is one entry in that set.

  **And the project can now say so itself**, which no env-only gate could express: `cascade` on the Game Config doc, authored in `/config` ("Winners tumble"), synced into the test-server manifest at publish, and read ahead of the protocol default. See [status/game-config.md](game-config.md) for the schema half.

  **Precedence, most specific first:** the project's authored `cascade` → the protocol default → `CASCADE_GAMES`. Only an EXPLICIT departure travels into the manifest — sending the resolved boolean for every project would have pinned every unauthored game and silently killed the env override.

  Verified end to end against the REAL `services/test-server/server.mjs` in `TEST_SERVER_LOCAL` mode, 8 spins per game across a six-game fixture: `scatter` and `cluster` tumble with no env set, `lines` and `book` do not, a scatter authored `cascade:false` does not, and a lines game authored `cascade:true` does. Plus 26 offline assertions (`packages/game-config/cascade.fixture.ts`) over the real normalizer, pinning that the field is stored ONLY when it departs from its type's default — the parity trap that would otherwise add a field to every existing doc. `check:game-config-defaults` reports all three committed templates byte-identical; `launcher-api` builds clean; `check:undefined-names` green over 857 files; eslint clean.

  **Still owed on the scatter side:** `boardMultiplierInfo` — the multiplier-collect beat — has a handler in the shared runtime and **no emitter anywhere**, neither the mock nor the facade. Scatter phases 1–2 shipped the presentation; that beat's wire was never written, so a scatter game tumbles but does not yet collect. ⏳ The `/config` control is compiled but NOT browser-verified.

- 2026-08-21 — **The engine is ours by name now: Stake branding is gone from code, comments and docs.** The fork still spoke its origin everywhere — a `stakeFacade.ts`, a `Stake-shaped` facade, `play4FunToStake`/`stakeToPlay4Fun`, `StakeBetRequest`/`StakeBetResponse`, `STAKE_AMOUNT_MULTIPLIER`, a `[stake-facade]` console prefix, Storybook viewports keyed `stake`, and ~150 comments calling this "the Stake Engine". All renamed to our own vocabulary (`engineFacade.ts` / `engine-facade.ts`, `play4FunToEngine`, `EngineBetRequest`, `ENGINE_AMOUNT_MULTIPLIER`, `[engine-facade]`).

  **The load-bearing part is what was NOT renamed, because "stake" carries three unrelated meanings here.** (1) The WAGER — `totalStake`, `interimStake`, `finalStake`, "total stake" — is the money vocabulary the RGS consistency gates assert on; renaming it would have broken the very checks that catch a client/server payout disagreement. (2) Live EXTERNAL references stay literal: `StakeEngine/web-sdk` is our actual `upstream` remote, and `engine.stake.com` / `stakeengine.github.io/math-sdk` / `stake.us` are real links that renaming turns dead. (3) The literal names of DELETED artifacts — `LoaderStakeEngine`, `stake-engine-loader.gif` — are kept inside backticks in the boot-splash migration note above, because a game repo carrying a frozen copy of `apps/lines/src` still imports them and needs to find that note by searching the OLD name.

  One honest origin line stays in `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and `docs/ONBOARDING.md`: this began as a fork of the Stake Engine web SDK. Upstream is MIT, so attribution is the correct posture, not a leftover. Also dropped a dead upstream vestige — `svelte.plugin.css.globals` in both `.vscode/settings.json` files pointed at `stake-kit`, a package in no `package.json` and no `node_modules`.

  **Two traps worth recording for the next sweep of this kind.** A blanket `sed -i` over every tracked file rewrites files it does not match, normalising line endings into a 1,775-file phantom diff — and it CORRUPTS binaries whose extensions are not excluded (`.ac3`, `.m4a`, `.wasm`, `.gz`, `.ico` were all hit and had to be restored from HEAD). Separately, `docs/design/invisible-editor.md` has MIXED CRLF/CR/LF terminators, so any line-based tool rewrites all 1,248 of its lines for a one-line edit; it needs a byte-exact replace instead.

  Verified: `check:path-imports`, `check:undefined-names` (855 files), `check:ways` and `eslint` all green; `apps/lines` builds with `PUBLIC_RGS_TRANSPORT=play4fun` and the renamed facade both resolves (all six exports) and ships in the built bundle (`[engine-facade]` present in the JS); launcher builds. **NOT swept:** `scripts/build-*.py`, three dated Italian client-deliverable PDF builders whose text describes the historical Stake `lines` → Hot Fruits comparison — rewriting those would falsify a delivered document.
