- 2026-08-21 — **The client and the RGS disagreed about MONEY on every non-5-line game, and nothing could have caught it.** Owner asked whether the scatter game was correct and whether the mock emulates Play4Fun closely enough that the front ends will still agree when the real server arrives. Checking rather than answering from the docs found two live defects on the SHARED path. **(1) The stake.** `requestBet` declared a hardcoded `betLinesOrConfig: 5` — right only for Hot Fruits, which really is a 5-line game. The server declares, evaluates and pays its OWN payline set, and the shared `lines` config authors **20**; the facade then normalised every win against `betPerLine × 20` while the round was charged `5 × betPerLine`, so the count-up showed **a quarter** of the multiplier the wallet credited (measured through the real test server: counts up to 14× while crediting 56×). A paylines-less model was worse — `cluster`/`scatter` author no lines, the empty list read as "absent", the stock 5×3 set was substituted, it failed `coversAllRows` on their taller boards and got **regenerated into 13 phantom lines**, which were then declared on the wire. Fix: the bet declares the server's own line count (`betLineCount`, from the captured boot `config`), a paylines-less model declares none and buys one unit, and the mock stops synthesising lines for a model that has none. **(2) The payout base.** The mock's base did not mirror the client's `payoutDivisor()` — `ways` priced per phantom LINE instead of per WAY (243 on a uniform 5×3), and `cluster` took the same per-line slice against a divisor of 1, so a win paid a multiple of what its own paytable quoted. `payoutBaseFor` now states all four next to the divisor they mirror, and lives inside `evaluateRawWins` — the ONE scorer the dealt spin and the cascade chain share, so a tumble step cannot price differently from the board that started it. It rides the per-reel `rowHeights`, not the bounding box, because a stepped grid's short columns offer fewer paths (a 3/4/5 column set pays 60 ways, not 125). **The gate is the real deliverable:** `pnpm check:rgs` — `check:stake` drives the REAL facade against the REAL mock for all four models and asserts `finalWin ÷ 100 === round.payoutMultiplier`, i.e. what the win presentation counts up to against what the wallet settles; they come from different fields via different code paths and only agree when the stake does. Plus `check:scatter` / `check:cluster` evaluator gates (`ways` had one; the other two shipped with none), and all four are now wired into CI, which `check:ways` never was. `scripts/ts-loader.mjs` is what makes this possible — a resolve hook so a plain node gate can import the TS facade; until now every check stopped at the mock's wire and took the client half **on trust**, which is exactly where both bugs lived. ♻️ **Rebased onto `main` on 2026-09-16, and two thirds of the original branch was dropped as already-landed:** the `{ positions }` SCAT wrapper it taught the facade to read was settled the other way by #441 (the mock emits a bare array; re-adding the reader would be dead code), and its whole-credit `toWholeUnits` rounding is #527's `roundPays`/`payCents` — same boundary, same argument, and #527's version is better because it FLOORS a sub-cent win at one cent instead of dropping it, and keeps the rounding out of the evaluators the RTP crosscheck tools import — the branch's whole-cent ASSERTIONS were kept, because they are exactly the invariant `payCents` guarantees and nothing was checking it. ⏳ Reaching the online games needs a `runtime:lines` release.

- 2026-08-20 — **A cascade game replayed its win line on the board the cascade had already destroyed — a frame drawn around symbols that never paid.** Reported on the online `test4` cluster game: the win registers, the winners explode, new symbols drop, and then the win line comes BACK, tracing cells that now hold something else entirely.

  **The replay, not the round, was drawing it.** `winSymbolCycle` is the resting-board narration: once the whole book has been presented it loops the round's recorded wins, drawing each win's line before lighting its symbols. It replays by POSITION, and its only invalidation seam was `reveal` — correct for a spinning game, where the board only changes on a new spin. A cascade changes the board MID-BOOK: `tumbleBoard` blows the paying cells out and drops new symbols into those exact seats, so by the time the cycle starts, every recorded win describes a board that no longer exists. `recordWinCycleWins` now clears on `tumbleBoard` as well as `reveal` (wins + the win-celebration dim, the same two clears). What survives is exactly the wins that landed AFTER the last cascade step — which for a chain that ends because nothing more pays is nothing at all, so the settled board simply rests. That is the correct read for a cascade: the resting board is not the board that paid.

  **The mock's cascade fixture was exploding the wrong cells, which is what made it unreadable.** `cascadeSteps` (`scripts/mock-rgs-server.mjs`, `CASCADE_GAMES`) picked the most COMMON line symbol on the board and blew that up twice, ignoring the spin's actual wins — so the frame stayed around survivors while unrelated symbols fell out from under it. Not cosmetic: the client narrates a win over the cells it was told paid. When the spin pays, step 1 now removes exactly this spin's paying cells (flat `{reel,row}` context for cluster/ways/scatter-pays; the leftmost `occurs` reels of a payline context — the same slice the client lights) and the chain stops there, because the mock does not re-evaluate the refilled board and a second step could only explode cells nothing paid on. The old most-common pass survives as the NO-WIN fallback, which is the only reason the fixture exists. Still not a protocol claim — `tumbleStep` is ours.

  **Verified live, A/B, on `apps/lines` against the cascade mock.** Traced `winLineShow`/`winLineHide` against the book-event dispatch. Before: `reveal → winInfo → winLineShow(5pts) → winLineHide → tumbleBoard → setWin → setTotalWin → finalWin → winLineShow(5pts, IDENTICAL points)` — the reported bug, the same line redrawn over the tumbled board. After: the trailing `winLineShow` is gone and nothing follows `finalWin`. Mock side checked across all three win models (lines / ways / cluster): exploding cells == paying cells, one step, per-reel refill counts == per-reel gaps, and dead spins still cascade. `check-undefined-names` clean, eslint clean, prettier clean.

- 2026-08-20 — **Two boot/win console warnings on the remake, one noise and one a real authoring gap: an unset asset key was reported as "missing", and a win tier had no way to name the count slot on its OWN spine.**

  **`Spine/Sprite: key "" is not found in loadedAssets` — noise.** The four missing-asset diagnostics (`Sprite`, `SpineProvider`, `SpriteSheet`, `Particles`) fired for an EMPTY key. An unset key is not a missing asset — it is a node the author has not pointed at anything yet, which the editor treats as legitimate ("an empty `spineKey` ⇒ no rig resolves ⇒ nothing renders"). Nothing renders either way, so the error only buried the real missing keys under noise no boot could ever clear. All four now gate on a shared `hasAssetKey` in `pixi-svelte/missingAsset.ts` — ONE rule, four readers, rather than the same test re-derived per component.

  **`[SpineSlot] no slot "slot_win_count"` — a real gap.** `WinVisual` resolves the big-win spine PER TIER (`<alias>Spine` ?? the config tier's `spineKey` ?? the shared `winSpine`) but applied ONE shared `slotName` to every tier. A tier pointing at its own rig almost never repeats the base bundle's slot name, and there was no per-tier knob to say so — so the count silently failed to mount on that tier and the warning was a dead end. Added `<alias>Slot` (`kind:'spineSlot'`, `spineParam:'<alias>Spine'`) to `winTierPresentationParams`, resolved in `WinVisual` as `<alias>Slot` ?? the shared `slotName` — the exact shape the spine already resolves by, on both the escalation-chain and single-tier paths. Empty ⇒ the shared value ⇒ parity, and `mergeBuiltinCodedParams` unions it onto pinned instances so no version bump is needed. The editor's dropdown lists the TIER spine's slots, falling back to `winSpine`'s when the tier spine is unset (`effectiveSpineBundle` already did this for the animation dropdowns).

  **And the warning itself now names the slots the skeleton DOES expose** (truncated at 20 — the rig checked live carries 54). Without them the author cannot see which name to type, which is why this one sat in the console instead of being fixed.

  **Verified:** `apps/lines` builds clean; `pixi-svelte` lints clean (the `engine-layout` errors are pre-existing, in files this does not touch); prettier clean. The Spine API the new warning uses (`skeleton.slots` → `slot.data.name`, and `findSlot` returning falsy for an unknown name) confirmed against a live 54-slot rig in the running game. **NOT verified end to end on the remake** — it needs the owner's session to boot, so the remaining `slot_win_count` warning there clears only once an author picks that tier's real slot in the new dropdown; the engine now shows them which names are available.

- 2026-08-20 — **CI now fails on an identifier that does not exist — the one type error `vite build` can never catch.** This closes the gap the free-spin hang exposed earlier today: an app's `build` is a bare `vite build`, esbuild TRANSPILES per file without type-checking, and typescript-eslint leaves `no-undef` off because it defers to a compiler nothing was running. So `#373`'s renamed-away `linePay` merged green, auto-released to every online game, and killed the first free spin of every Book-of round.

  **Narrow on purpose, which is what makes it need no baseline.** `scripts/check-undefined-names.mjs` runs `tsc --noResolve` over all 855 workspace `.ts` files and reports only `TS2304` / `TS2552` / `TS18004` — "this name does not exist". `--noResolve` is the trick: nothing loads across module boundaries, so the check is immune to the two things that make a full `tsc` unusable here (the ambient `*.svelte` shim, which shows plain `tsc` only a default export so every `import type { Props } from './X.svelte'` reports a phantom error; and unresolved JSON/asset imports). An import STATEMENT still declares its names, so a genuine dangling identifier is still caught. The repo is CLEAN under this rule today, so any hit is new and real — no baseline file, no ratchet.

  **Ambient names are seeded, not stubbed.** `--noResolve` also means no declarations for globals, so the program includes the repo's own `.d.ts` files (they declare `__IE_DEBUG__`) plus Svelte's REAL `types/index.d.ts` for the runes. A hand-written rune shim was the obvious shortcut and is wrong: a typo'd rune (`$stat`) is exactly what this should catch, and a shim would declare it away.

  **The gate's own first version had the bug it exists to prevent.** Checking 855 files needs more than Node's default heap; `tsc` OOM'd, printed nothing the filter recognised, and the script reported a clean pass — while silently missing the very regression it was written for. Caught by re-introducing `#373`'s exact diff and watching it pass. Fixed with `--max-old-space-size=8192` AND a hard exit-status check: `tsc` exits 0/1/2, so anything else means the check did not COMPLETE and must fail. Re-verified the same way (2 hits with the regression in, 0 with it out).

  **It found a real one immediately:** `packages/utils-sound/src/createSound.svelte.ts` called `new Howl({…})` while importing only `Howler`. It happened to work because howler's bundle assigns the global as a side effect of the import that IS there — a runtime coincidence, not a contract. Now imported properly.

  **A full type-check is still owed, and the debt is measured rather than guessed.** `apps/lines/tsconfig.json` no longer OOMs the compiler (it excluded only `dist`/`build`/`node_modules`, while `config-ts/base.json` sets `allowJs`/`checkJs`, so the multi-megabyte bundles under `.svelte-kit/output` were being type-checked); it now excludes build output, and `tsc -p apps/lines` completes. What it reports: 159 errors outside `node_modules` — **114 of them from `static/`** (asset-glue index files vite compiles with its own plugins), and of the 36 in `src/`, 14 are `.svelte`/module-resolution artifacts and **22 are genuine code-level type errors**. Most of the rest is configuration, not code: `resolveJsonModule` is off and the app does not extend `.svelte-kit/tsconfig.json`, so `$app/*` never resolves. `svelte-check` (not installed) is the other half and is bigger — the last measurement in this file records cluster 140 / price 153 / scatter 138 / ways 121 / number-picker 26. Gating any of that needs a baseline first, which is why it is NOT in this change.

- 2026-08-20 — **The count-up skip fix (#396) shipped an infinite `$effect` loop — reverted, and fixed at the actual root instead: a disabled button was still eating the pointer.** After the Runtime release the live Borut remake threw `Uncaught Error: effect_update_depth_exceeded` on a loop the moment a win count-up mounted (right after the `slot_win_count` SpineSlot warnings), on every flush.

  **The loop.** `<CountUpInteraction>`'s new `$effect` called `registerSkipGesture(...)`, whose body is `stateUi.skipGestureCount += 1`. `+=` READS the counter, and that read happened inside the effect's tracking scope — so the effect depended on the very `$state` it wrote. Run → +1 → self-invalidate → cleanup −1 → run → +1 → … The pre-existing `registerContinuePress` never had this: its counter bump lives in `PressToContinue`'s script BODY, not in an effect, and the registry itself is a plain array. **The lesson generalises — a register-in-an-`$effect` helper must not touch `$state` with a read-modify-write.**

  **The root cause the registry was working around is one line.** A disabled button kept `eventMode: 'static'`, so it still WON the hit test and swallowed the press — every handler early-returns on `disabled`, so it just did nothing with it. That is the whole reason a pointer parked on the spin/turbo button made a celebration unskippable: the overlay's own full-screen tap surface (`PressToContinue`, `CountUpInteraction`) sits BELOW the HUD and never saw the gesture. Fixed at every place a press surface decides it is inert — the three button paths `components-pixi/Button.svelte` (coded), `components-ui-pixi/ButtonFrame.svelte` (the parametric bound part) and `engine-layout/ComponentInstance.svelte` (the authored art-button path), plus the three bet-value tap targets `LabelBet` / `HudValue` / `HudReadout`, which go inert mid-spin and so were live-swallowing throughout the very window a celebration runs in. All now `eventMode={disabled ? 'none' : 'static'}`. `'none'` skips the container AND its children in the hit test, so the press falls through to whatever the overlay put under the chrome. No canvas-top mask, no gesture registry, no cross-component state.

  `<ContinuePressMask>` stays as it was before #396 (press-to-continue only) — it is load-bearing for authored screens and untouched by this.

  **Verified live** (`apps/lines` on the mock, real Pixi tree): with a Space-hold raising `stateBet.isSpaceHold`, all six surfaces flip `static` → `none` and none is left `not-allowed` while still hit-testing; the two buttons that stay live keep `static`. Console clean — no `effect_update_depth_exceeded`.

  **Watch for:** a disabled button no longer blocks what is BEHIND it. During a celebration that is the point. At idle there is no full-canvas tap surface mounted, so nothing catches a fall-through press — but a future full-screen surface under the HUD would now receive taps aimed at greyed chrome.

- 2026-08-20 — **Free spins died on the first reveal — `#373` renamed a local and left the Book-of reach reading the old name.** Owner triggered the feature on the live Borut remake: the intro played, the first free spin dealt, and the reels never stopped. Console: `Uncaught ReferenceError: linePay is not defined` thrown out of the `reveal` book event, so `playBookEvent` never resolved and the round hung with no way forward. Cause is a rename that missed one call site — `#373` (the ways-reach change, merged the same morning) replaced `buildReach`'s `const linePay` with `symbolPay` and dropped `const numLines = getNumLines()` in favour of calling it inline, but the **`bookReach`** block below still passed both as shorthand properties (`linePay,` / `numLines,`). Both now point at nothing.

  **Why only free spins, and only Book-of.** `bookReach` is built solely when `stateGame.specialSymbol` is set — the round's expanding symbol, assigned at feature start and cleared at `freeSpinEnd`. Base game never evaluates that branch, and neither does a non-Book-of game, so every spin outside the feature ran clean and the break looked like it appeared from nowhere. It also needs `anticipationMode` on, which the remake has.

  **Why the build was green.** `apps/lines`' `build` script is bare `vite build` — esbuild transpiles per file and never type-checks, and eslint's `no-undef` is off under typescript-eslint (it defers to the compiler). `tsc` catches it instantly: `TS18004: No value exists in scope for the shorthand property 'linePay'`, and the same on `numLines`. Nothing in CI ran `tsc`, so a hard runtime crash merged with a green tick. **Closed the same day** by the `no undefined identifiers` CI gate — see the entry below.

  **Blast radius, because this is the shared runtime.** Merging to `main` auto-fires the Runtime release workflow on any `apps/lines/**` or `packages/**` change, which re-points EVERY online game at the new bundle — so `#373` reached the remake within minutes of merging and stayed broken until this. Verified: `tsc` clean on the file, the built client bundle now carries `linePay` only as a property name and a `t.linePay(...)` member call with `numLines:O$()` (the `getNumLines` call) beside it — no free identifier left; `apps/lines` builds clean.

- 2026-08-20 — **`scatter` gets an evaluator too, and the boot warning is gone — all four win models are honoured end to end.** New `evaluateScatterPays` in the mock: every symbol pays on COUNT ANYWHERE, `minCount` from the project's declared win model, wilds substituting, positions as the flat `{reel,row}` list the facade already reads. **Unlike cluster, this does NOT approximate the payout.** A scatter game prices by count (8 → 3, 9 → 7.5, 10 → 15, 13+ → 60) and the sample config already carries exactly that table, while the mock's own is keyed by payline RUN LENGTHS (3/4/5) — clamping a count of 12 into a 5-run row would make every scatter win pay the same number, degenerate enough to be useless. So the project's table travels: `projectSymbolPaytable` maps it into the mock's server vocabulary through the same in-play gate + `linesMapping` translation `projectLineSymbols` uses, rides the manifest grid as `symbolPaytable`, and is shape-checked on arrival (a malformed table would leave every win silently unpriced). Priced against the TOTAL stake, matching `payoutDivisor` returning 1 for the model. **A bug the harness caught:** the first lookup was exact-match with a `Math.min(count, max)` clamp, so against a SPARSE table (`{8,9,10,13}`) a count of 11 resolved to an unpriced row and paid NOTHING — a win visible on the board scoring zero. Paytable rows are THRESHOLDS, so it now takes the highest tier at or below the count; that also does the clamping the fallback table needs. **Naming hazard worth knowing:** `evaluateScatters` already existed and is a different thing — the SCAT feature symbol that triggers free spins, which runs for every win model. The new one is the win MODEL, where an ordinary H1 pays because eight of them landed. Wired like cluster (`MockProtocol`, `protocolFor`, `projectGrid`, the test server's protocol allowlist — the same allowlist that silently forced `'lines'` during the cluster work). **The boot warning is REMOVED**, not narrowed: there is no declared-but-unhonoured model left. The comment says what to restore if a fifth is ever added, and records why this one had to be watched — it spent a while claiming every client surface fell back to line behaviour, which had stopped being true. Verified: 9-assertion evaluator harness (count-anywhere, threshold, exact tier pricing, non-adjacency, wild gating, sparse-table tier, fallback), then end to end through the REAL `server.mjs` — `PIC1 count=8 pay=300` (mult 3 × 100 stake), `PIC6 count=9 pay=120` (1.2 × 100), positions matching. Both apps build; eslint clean.

- 2026-08-20 — **A cluster game is PLAYABLE against the test server — and "no runtime for cluster" turned out to be the wrong diagnosis.** Owner asked why cluster still had no runtime when what they need is a working test setup, not a shipped one. Checking rather than repeating the warning: **the client was already correct for cluster** — `payoutDivisor` → 1, `winLineShapeFor` → `'cells'`, anticipation stands down, and the cascade overlay mounts. The ONLY thing making a cluster project play as lines was that the **mock had no cluster evaluator**, so it paid paylines. That is a test-server gap, not an engine one, and the boot warning claiming "every client surface falls back to line behaviour" was simply wrong. New `evaluateClusters` (`scripts/mock-rgs-server.mjs`): a flood fill per candidate symbol — per-symbol rather than one board partition, because a wild can join clusters of DIFFERENT symbols at once — honouring `minCluster` and `orthogonal`/`diagonal` adjacency straight from the project's declared win model, with positions as the flat `{reel,row}` list `engineFacade`'s `winPositions` already reads for a non-payline win. Wired end to end: `MockProtocol` += `'cluster'`, `protocolFor('cluster')`, `projectGrid` now runs for cluster (it previously bailed on `protocol !== 'lines'` AND on having no paylines — a cluster game has none, so it would have silently fallen back to the shared lines grid), the manifest grid carries `minCluster`/`adjacency`, and the test server's protocol allowlist accepts it. **⚠️ The payout is a TEST APPROXIMATION**: the mock's paytable is keyed by payline run lengths (3/4/5) and a cluster is 5+ cells, so size is CLAMPED to the largest priced row. That is enough to exercise presentation and is explicitly not a cluster paytable — real pricing is by cluster size and needs a math export, the same line held on the ways strips. Corollary worth knowing when configuring a test project: a cluster smaller than the lowest priced run never pays, so `minCluster` below the paytable's floor reads as "clusters don't work" (my own first test tripped on exactly that). The boot warning now names only `scatter`, and says what is actually true — "has no EVALUATOR … so the board is dealt LINE wins" — rather than claiming the client surfaces fall back. Verified: 9-assertion evaluator harness (L-shaped group, minCluster honoured, orthogonal vs diagonal grouping, wild substitution gated on a declared wild paytable), then end to end through the REAL `server.mjs` with a cluster manifest — `mode=cluster`, groups of 6 and 7 cells, positions matching. Both apps build; eslint clean.

- 2026-08-20 — **The cascade could deadlock the round — every symbol beat is now bounded.** Owner spun the live `test4` cascade: symbols dropped and refilled correctly, then the spin button would not re-arm — intermittently. Cause: `tumbleBoardExplode` and `tumbleBoardSlideDown` awaited each symbol's `oncomplete` unconditionally, and a symbol only reports completion when its state actually ANIMATES. `SymbolSprite` fires `oncomplete` from an `$effect` gated on `symbolInfo` CHANGING, so a symbol whose `explosion`/`land` state resolves to the same art it is already showing — the normal case for a project that authored neither, which `test4` has not — never reports at all. The `broadcastAsync` never settles, the book event never finishes, the round never ends. **Intermittent because the mock explodes whichever symbol is most common that spin**, so whether it hung depended on which symbol's art happened to differ. Fixed by RACING every beat against a 650ms cap (`CASCADE_BEAT_CAP_MS`, and `COLLECT_BEAT_CAP_MS` in `MultiplierBoard`, which had the identical unbounded wait in `multiplierBoardAnimate` and was fixed at the same time before anyone hit it): an authored animation still drives the timing because it resolves first, and an unauthored one costs a bounded beat instead of hanging forever. The slide-down also force-settles `symbolState` to `static` after the race, since a cap win would otherwise park the cell on `land` — a symbol frozen mid-animation once the cascade ends. Same reasoning as `Board.svelte`'s `STACKED_WIN_HOLD_MS` (a covered cell mounts no `<Symbol>` at all — different cause, identical failure), which is the precedent this mirrors. **This is the class of bug only a real spin finds:** the contract harness, the byte-identity checks and the mock-level verification all passed while the round deadlocked, because none of them run the presentation. `apps/lines` builds clean; eslint clean. ⏳ Needs a republish of `test4` to verify the fix live.

- 2026-08-20 — **The old vendor logo is gone: the boot splash is now a pipeline spine, in two tiers.** Every game opened on `stake-engine-loader.gif` (1.3 MB, copied into all six `apps/*/static/`) via `LoaderStakeEngine`, then on `LoaderExample`'s "Add Your Loader" placeholder — both hardcoded in each `+layout.svelte`. Replaced by `BootSplashSequence` + `LoaderSpine` (`components-shared`), which play **spine** marks produced by our own tools: an ENGINE mark (global, admin-owned) then the GAME's own (per project). No new asset class — both are ordinary spine bundles read out of the roots that already exist, `_shared/spines/<bundle>` and `<client>/<project>/spines/<bundle>`, and `resolveBundlePrefix` already falls back project→shared, so ONE resolution path serves both tiers.

  **Storage mirrors the tiers, deliberately.** The engine mark is named by `app_settings.bootSplashEngine` (admin-only write, `/admin` → Settings) and resolves through a NEW `resolveSharedBundlePrefix` / `exportSpineBundle({forceShared})` — the project-first resolver would have let a project owning a same-named bundle silently shadow the engine's own mark, which is the one thing the admin tier exists to prevent. The game mark is `GameSettings.bootLoader` on the editor doc (Scene Editor → Game Settings), and resolves project-then-shared for both its files and its `skeletons.json` entry.

  **Why a `deploy/_boot/` mirror rather than the runtime bundle:** the splash paints before `/api/editor/runtime` resolves, so it cannot be TOLD where its art is. `bootSplashExport.ts` mirrors both bundles to fixed stems (`_boot/engine`, `_boot/game`) and writes `_boot/boot.json` beside them; the splash resolves everything off `assetBase`, which is the page-relative `assets/` when baked and the launcher's PATH-form `/api/deploy/f/<token>/…/` in runtime mode — both preserve file extensions, which `Assets.load` needs to pick a spine parser. Runs inside `ensureDeployExports`, so Publish and the live assemble share the one export path. Deliberately does NOT use the shared `_pages/` store (`editorArtExport` prunes that against its own written set and would delete a boot page) and prunes only `_boot/`.

  **Ordering was the real finding.** `apps/lines` paints `#ie-boot` from the HTML shell at `z-index: 99999` and holds it until every asset loads; the old gif loaders sat UNDER it at `999` and burned their 2s timers unseen. So the vendor mark was already invisible online and only ever showed in the five dev games (whose `app.html` has no shell) — the "replace the logo" job was half-done by an overlay nobody had connected it to. Running a spine ANIMATION under that overlay would be pointless the same way, so `__ieBoot` gained `whenDone(cb)` and the sequence waits on it: progress bar → engine mark → game mark → game. Hosts with no shell start immediately.

  **Fail-open throughout, because this is the boot path.** No index, an empty index, a renamed bundle, a missing animation, no WebGL — each skips its tier and calls `oncomplete`; nothing here can stall a game. A named-but-unresolvable bundle logs a warning rather than failing a publish. `LoaderSpine` stands up its own `Application` (the game's does not exist yet) pinned to `preference:'webgl'` (WebGPU inits but paints nothing on iOS/Pixel) and **destroys it on teardown** — the GL context is capped and the game's renderer wants one moments later. An animation name that no longer resolves falls back to the first clip rather than the setup pose, which renders empty.

  **One trap caught by the offline fixture, not the build:** `normalizeGameSettings` in `editorStorage.ts` is a strict whitelist, so `bootLoader` round-tripped away on every save — the field would have appeared to save in the editor and never reached R2. 14 offline assertions over the REAL modules now cover `normalizeBootSplashRef` (blank/`..`/absolute rejected, trimming, nested bundles) and that round-trip, including "settings holding only a bootLoader isn't dropped as all-defaults". `launcher-api` + all six games build clean.

  **Migration note:** `LoaderStakeEngine` / `LoaderExample` are DELETED from the `components-shared` barrel (`LoaderBase`/`LoaderImage` stay — still generic and exported). A game repo carrying a frozen copy of `apps/lines/src` still imports them, so its next engine-submodule bump needs the `+layout.svelte` reconciled (`gotcha_game_repo_app_src_drift_from_apps_lines`). ⏳ **Not browser-verified** — needs a spine published to `_shared/spines/` and a publish; nothing is configured yet, and with both tiers unset boot is byte-identical to today minus the two gif splashes.

- 2026-08-20 — **The cascade fixture is per-GAME, because a global flag would have cascaded Book of Borut.** #385 shipped the fixture reading `process.env.CASCADE` inside the mock. One Invisible Test Server process serves EVERY game, so setting that env — the literal next step for seeing the cascade — would have made the shipped `bookofborutremake` tumble on every spin. Caught before enabling it, not after. Now `CASCADE_GAMES` is a comma-separated allowlist of game keys (`CASCADE_GAMES=test4`, or `*` for all, unset ⇒ nobody), read by the SERVER, which passes an explicit boolean per game into its mock. The mock's env read survives only for the standalone CLI, where there is one game and no ambiguity — and `opts.cascade ?? env` means an explicit `false` from the server always wins over a stray env. Verified by running two mocks side by side as the server configures them: `test4` emits tumble steps, `bookofborutremake` emits none. **To see the cascade:** set `CASCADE_GAMES=test4` on the test-server service, spin, and note the cells play their authored `explosion` state — a project with none authored in `/symbols` will show them vanish rather than explode.

- 2026-08-20 — **The cascade overlay was unreachable — mounted now, plus an opt-in cascade to drive it.** Owner asked how to see `TumbleBoard`; the answer was that you could not, and not because of a flag. #375 put `TumbleBoard`/`MultiplierBoard` in `registerBoundComponents` and stopped there — that registry only declares that a scene node MAY bind a component, and nothing did, so neither ever mounted, never subscribed to its cues, and the `tumbleBoard` handler broadcast into nothing. `SpecialBook` works because the reference layout carries a `specialBook` scene with a `bind` anchor; the cascade had no equivalent. **Two independent reasons nothing rendered, and #375 only reported the second** (no RGS sends `tumbleBoard`). The vocab harness passed throughout because it tests the CONTRACT — that the palette declares these surfaces — not that anything mounts; byte-identity and contract tests cannot catch "right component, never mounted". Fixed by mounting both DIRECTLY beside `<Board />` inside the `reelStack()` snippet, which is what they are (the tumble stands in for the reels between `boardHide`/`boardShow`; the collect flies multipliers to board centre) — and by REMOVING them from `registerBoundComponents`, since a bind anchor plus a coded mount would double-subscribe and play every cue twice (the `gotcha_coded_ui_doubles_hud_under_flowv2` shape). Neither needs placement: both position off `boardLayout()`. Both render nothing until their cue, so an un-cascading game is byte-identical with them mounted. **Something to look at:** the mock gains a `CASCADE=1` presentation fixture (same idiom as `FORCE_TRIGGER`/`BIG_WIN`/`STACKED`, off by default) emitting two deterministic `tumbleStep` events per spin — explode the most common symbol, refill from above — and `engineFacade` translates them to `tumbleBoard`, shifting rows by the `BOARD_PADDING_ROWS` the reveal adds. ⚠️ **`tumbleStep` is NOT a captured protocol.** Every other case in that switch was verified against a real Play4Fun session; this one is ours, invented so the presentation has input, gated off, and labelled at both ends — a real provider's cascade should REPLACE it, not be bent to fit. Verified: the mock emits 2 steps with per-reel refill counts matching the explosion counts, and with `CASCADE` unset emits none (parity). **NOT verified: the facade case is prettier/eslint-clean but never executed** — `apps/lines` does not import `engine-facade`, so no build compiles it, and the translator package has no test harness. First live cascade will exercise it. Also unverified live: the games host (`games.invisiblewall.org`) was 502ing across all four games during this work.

- 2026-08-20 — **The multiplier-collect mechanic lands too — scatter phases 1–2.** Scatter is the cascade PLUS one beat: multipliers landed during a tumble play their win state in place, fly to the board centre, and combine into a board multiplier. So it cost roughly a third of cluster, and `SCATTER_VOCAB` is built **ON** `CLUSTER_VOCAB` rather than beside it — the two share the whole cascade, and expressing that as inheritance is what stops the tumble surfaces being declared twice and drifting. New surfaces are exactly `boardMultiplierInfo` + six cues. **Two things came out of the reference rather than being written.** (1) `stateGame.multiplierBoard` and its `MultiplierSymbol` type were ALREADY in the shared board state — flagged as dead scaffolding when the cascade landed, they turn out to be scatter's model, lifted by the Phase A extraction and left waiting for these components, so nothing new had to be added to hold it. (2) The reference tested `rawSymbol.name === 'M'` to decide what collects — a hardcoded symbol id that would silently collect NOTHING in a project that named its multiplier anything else; the port tests `RawSymbol.multiplier !== undefined`, the property the value actually rides on. Also simplified deliberately: `MultiplierSymbol` renders through the ordinary `Symbol` state machine instead of the reference's bespoke `SymbolSpineMain`/`SymbolSpineBackground` pair, so its art is whatever `/symbols` authored for the `win` state rather than a second art path no tool reaches; and the beat's totals ride the game's ordinary win meter rather than a `tumbleWinAmount`/`multiplierTotal` display stack of its own. Fixed while porting: the upstream `boardMultiplierInfo` type declared `tumbleWin: 400; boardMult: 5; totalWin: 2000` — example values pasted in as LITERAL types — now plain numbers. Verified: `flowV2Vocab` over four templates, asserting in both directions that scatter INHERITS the cascade (so a later rebase onto the standard palette could not silently drop the beat it is mostly made of), adds exactly the collect surfaces, takes nothing away, and declares `M` where cluster does not; `v2drivenseed`/`v2runtime`/`v2mount`/`v2buyflow` unregressed; `gen-flow-vocabulary --check` green at 55 events / 16 book events; both apps build; eslint clean. **Same blockers as cluster** — no RGS sends `boardMultiplierInfo`, and the cascade wire has never been captured. One step better off: scatter ships a committed config default where cluster's upstream strips are empty. ⏳ Not browser-verified. **Engine change on `main` — reaches an online game only on a runtime republish + `engine` submodule bump.**

- 2026-08-20 — **The cascade (tumble) mechanic is in the shared runtime — cluster Phases 1–2.** Reverses the 2026-08-19 scope-out, which was right that a cascade is a board MECHANIC rather than a template and wrong about what that costs. Two findings did it: **(1) the tumble is an OVERLAY** — `TumbleBoard` mounts for the cascade and unmounts again (`boardHide` → show → explode → remove → slide → `boardSettle` → `boardShow`), so a game that never tumbles never mounts it and is unaffected; **(2) `explosion` was already an authorable symbol state** in `SYMBOL_STATES`, ungated, so the cascade's defining animation is authored in `/symbols` like any other state and the mechanic needed no symbol tooling. Landed: `stateTumble.svelte.ts` (the `base`/`adding` two-layer model — kept apart so `removeExploded` can filter survivors without touching symbols still falling), `TumbleBoard`/`TumbleBoardBase`/`TumbleSymbol` in `apps/lines` (which IS `_runtime/lines`, the same reason `SpecialBook` lives there rather than in `engine-game` — the tumble depends on the game's own `Symbol`/`SymbolWrap`, one of the Phase A slices still unextracted), three book events + handlers, seven cues, and a registered `/flow-v2` `cluster` vocabulary. **The one thing that did NOT port** is the geometry: the reference game seats symbols at `(index + 0.5) * SYMBOL_SIZE`, which this runtime outgrew when the reel grid became authorable, so **`getSymbolY` was added beside `getSymbolX`** as the shared resting-seat expression `createReelForSpinning` already computes — a cascade must drop a symbol on the SAME seat a settled reel would give it, and matching a constant would have held only until the first project resized its grid. Also fixed on the way past: keying `TumbleBoardBase`'s inner `{#each}` by the symbol OBJECT rather than its index, because `removeExploded` shifts every surviving index and index-keying would tear down the very `symbolY` Tween the slide is about to animate (the reference implementation has the un-keyed version). Verified: `flowV2Vocab` 25/25 over three templates incl. cluster adds EXACTLY the cascade surfaces and no book-of mechanic, and the book-of reveal flow is REJECTED against the cluster vocab; `v2drivenseed`/`v2runtime`/`v2mount`/`v2buyflow` unregressed; `gen-flow-vocabulary --check` green at 49 events / 15 book events; `apps/lines` + `launcher-api` build clean; eslint clean. **A cluster game still cannot be published** — no RGS sends `tumbleBoard` (the wire would be invented, not transcribed: there is no capture of a cascade game), and `apps/cluster` ships empty `paddingReels` so there is no config default to seed from. ⏳ Not browser-verified. **Engine change on `main` — reaches an online/remake game only on a runtime republish + `engine` submodule bump.**

- 2026-08-20 — **A cinematic could not be skipped while the pointer rested on the spin button — an inert button still eats the click.** Free-spin intro/outro and the big win put a full-screen tap-to-continue overlay up, and #153/#163 locked the spin button so a press could not slam past it. But locking only kills the press BODY: the button keeps `eventMode: 'static'`, so it stays the hit target, and `PressToContinue`'s own full-screen rect paints at the OVERLAY's z — below the HUD. A tap over the button therefore did nothing at all (button inert, overlay never reached) and the player had to move the pointer off the HUD to skip. Turbo was worse: not locked at all, so a tap there toggled turbo under a full-screen overlay instead of dismissing it. Fixed by inverting who masks whom. New `<ContinuePressMask>` (`engine-game`) mounts ONCE in `Game.svelte` at a new `LAYER_BAND_INPUT_MASK` (11_000, the one band above `LAYER_BAND_TOP`) and, while any press is live, covers the canvas and runs the NEWEST registered press body — so the overlay masks the chrome, a tap anywhere skips, and every HUD button (menu, buy, bet steppers, autoplay) goes untappable for exactly as long as the overlay is up, without per-button pointer surgery. The handler registry lives beside `stateUi.continuePressCount` (`registerContinuePress` / `runTopContinuePress`), which `PressToContinue` now feeds alongside the count it already kept; newest-wins mirrors what the per-overlay rects did by paint order across a fade-out/fade-in overlap. Nothing mounts when no press is live ⇒ the HUD is byte-unchanged the rest of the time. Greying rode along as the AFFORDANCE: `isCelebrationLocked()` is now exported from `utils-shared/spinStop` and the turbo button (coded + parametric) greys on the same read the spin button uses, so a dead button looks dead. Verified live on `apps/lines` + the book mock: turbo greys with spin the moment the lock raises, and the mask band mounts empty until a press exists. **Reaching online games needs a Runtime release** (they run the shared `_runtime/lines` bundle).

- 2026-08-20 — **Reel anticipation now runs on a ways game — the one place the client actually evaluates a board.** "Non-lines win evaluation in the client" sounded larger than it is: the client never DETERMINES wins (it receives `winInfo` with positions from the RGS), so the only surface that scores a board is reel anticipation, where `createLinesReach` answers "could this reel still complete a paying run". #357 made ways stand DOWN there rather than feed line maths a board it does not fit. New `createWaysReach` (`utils-slots/anticipationReach.ts`) closes it, and it is a genuinely different walk rather than lines math minus the paylines: (1) there is no row to walk — a reel either holds the symbol or it does not, and the locked prefix carries a running PRODUCT of per-reel counts; (2) symbols do not compete — a payline pays its single best interpretation, ways pays every completed run at once, so it SUMS across symbols exactly as `evaluateWays` does; (3) the optimistic bound must maximise `mult(r) x ways(r)` over the reachable lengths, because a longer run is not automatically worth more (`mult(r)` can be absent at some `r` while the ways product keeps growing) — the line walker can safely assume full width, this cannot. The divisor is the product of each reel's VISIBLE row count read off the board, so a stepped grid counts correctly (mirrors `activeWaysCount`). **`triggerBounds` turned out to be model-INDEPENDENT** and was extracted to a shared helper: a scatter triggers on a count anywhere, never along a line, which is why "tease the 3rd scatter" needed no ways variant at all. `anticipation.ts` picks the reach by `activeWinModel()`; `cluster`/`scatter` still stand down through the empty payline list, now stated in a comment so it doesn't read as an accident. **The boot warning was narrowed** — it claimed a ways game "is being played as LINES", which stopped being true across #357/#360 and this change, so it now fires only for `cluster`/`scatter`. Verified: new `node packages/utils-slots/anticipationWaysReach.fixture.ts` — bounds narrow monotonically AND converge on the board's REAL payout (computed independently in the fixture, since a reach that converges on the wrong number still passes every monotonicity check), a run broken among locked reels cannot be rescued, the ways product is honoured, trigger-reach matches lines; the existing lines fixture is unchanged and still passes after the shared-helper extraction; `apps/lines` builds clean; eslint clean. ⏳ Not browser-verified — anticipation is opt-in per flow and no ways project exists yet. **Engine change on `main` — reaches an online/remake game only on a runtime-bundle republish + `engine` submodule bump.**

- 2026-08-20 — **The game-type-templates plan is finally readable: `docs/design/game-type-templates.md` now exists.** It was cited as the authoritative numbered plan from ~14 code comments and both status files, and had never been written — so rule 7's "read the registered next step" pointed at nothing. Reconstructed from the shipped work (#355 / #357 / #360, plus the `engine/ways-authoring-kind` branch for Phase E), with a section per named phase — A0–A5, B, C, D, E — so every citation resolves. It also records the owner decision of 2026-08-19 that **`cluster` / `scatter` templates will NOT be built**: both need the tumble/cascade mechanic the shared runtime has never carried (`tumbleBoard`, `updateTumbleWin`, `updateGlobalMult`, plus `updateGrid` for cluster and `boardMultiplierInfo` for scatter), which makes them a board mechanic rather than a template; their win models stay declared and correctly priced. Open item 1 above was rewritten at the same time: its headline still said Phase A was IN PROGRESS with only A0/A1 landed, which its own body (A2–A5) and `packages/engine-game` on disk both contradicted.

- 2026-08-19 — **A ways win drew four disconnected vertical dashes instead of a win line — the cluster heuristic can't tell a ways win from a Book-of expansion.** `WinLine.svelte` has always had two shapes, picked by a `columnWin` flag that `winLineColumnWinFor` computed as "does any reel pay more than one cell". That is exactly right for the case it was written for (a **lines** game whose special symbol expands to fill whole reels — Book of Borut — where a connected polyline traces an unreadable criss-cross zig-zag) and exactly wrong for **ways**, where a duplicate reel is the normal case, not the exception. Project `test3` (`winModel: {type:'ways', direction:'ltr', minKind:3}`, 5×3, zero paylines) paid hearts on reels 1-4 with reel 4 contributing two cells — 2 ways — and the flag flipped, so the line rendered as one short bar per paying cell. The shape is now decided by the **declared win model**, not by counting reels: `winLineShapeFor()` returns `'reels'` for `ways`, `'cells'` for `cluster`/`scatter`, and for `lines` keeps the duplicate-reel heuristic (so the shipped Book-of expansion is byte-unchanged). The new `'reels'` shape merges a reel's winning cells into ONE bar spanning topmost→bottommost ± half a cell — a ways win pays by whole-reel participation, so the reel is the unit, and cells on the same reel join even when they are NOT adjacent. Reels are keyed by the point's `x` (`getSymbolX(reel)`, one exact value per reel), so the grouping is lossless without threading reel indices through the emitter event. `columnWin?: boolean` on `winLineShow` becomes `shape?: WinLineShape` — not in any FlowDoc or emitter vocabulary, so nothing authored changes — and all three dispatch sites (coded handler, `showWinLine` effect, resting win cycle) share the one helper as before. Both bar shapes still skip the head-trace reveal and the full-payline underlay, and still stamp the amount ONCE centred over the winning columns. `svelte-check` 0 errors. **Ships to online games with a Runtime release** (they run the shared `_runtime/lines` bundle).

- 2026-08-19 — **Every button in a fully authored HUD threw on press — the whole parametric HUD was inert.** `<ButtonFrame>`'s `onpress` read the param context from inside its Pixi `onpointerup` callback (`getComponentParams()['onpress']`), and `getComponentParams()` is `getContext`, which Svelte 5 permits only during component initialisation. So the press threw `lifecycle_outside_component` _before_ reaching either the Flow v2 route or the coded fallback — flow-owned or not, the button was dead. It read as "the flow isn't wired" rather than as a crash because the button still rendered, hovered and painted its states correctly (every other call site in the file sits inside `$derived`, which runs in the reactive context and was fine). The coded HUD never hit it — `ButtonBetProvider` and friends don't go through `<ButtonFrame>` — so this only bit projects driving a fully authored HUD, which is why it survived this long. Fix: hoist the params object to init; `<ComponentInstance>` provides a stable object whose engine-provided keys are live getters, so holding the reference keeps every read reactive. Verified by stash/un-stash against a real authored HUD (project `test3`, 7 buttons) on one dev server — before: all 7 threw and the bet never moved; after: none threw and the coded `increase` body ran. Then verified LIVE after the Runtime release: all 7 press clean, and the authored buy chain runs end-to-end (BUY BONUS ▶ `buyFeature`; card press ▶ `selectBetMode` ▶ `buyConfirm`; cancel ▶ back to `buyFeature`). #358.

- 2026-08-19 — **Game-type templates, Phase A0: `packages/engine-game` + a build gate that can actually prove "unchanged".** Phase A relocates ~80% of `apps/lines/src` into a shared package so a game type becomes a mechanic rather than an app fork; the invariant is that `lines` keeps emitting identical code at every slice. That claim needed a check, and the obvious one was worthless as-is: **two builds of an untouched tree produce different bundles** (`B-2n2O0P` → `BVP2bHQi`). Three sources, one root — the `ie-build-stamp`'s wall-clock `builtAt` changes every build, which changes the content hash, which changes the content-hashed filename the bundle embeds _self-referentially_; SvelteKit's per-build random app id (`__sveltekit_dhfji1`) is the third. New `scripts/bundle-fingerprint.mjs` normalizes exactly those three, after which two builds of an unchanged tree are bit-identical (verified over three runs). Baseline for `lines` at `9a3cfa97`: bundle `5adc7bc7bfcc44a1`. The A0 slice itself is deliberately **type-only** — `types.ts` splits, with the agnostic vocabulary (`SymbolName`, `RawSymbol`, `Position`, `SymbolState`, `SymbolCellInfo`, `SymbolInfoMap`) moving to the package and `BetMode`/`GameType` staying in the app (bound to the compiled config, shared vocabulary with the RGS). Types are erased at build, so a correct move MUST be bit-identical — it is, which proves the package resolves _and_ that the gate reports nothing when nothing changed. Package is source-entry (`main` → `index.ts`, no build step), modelled on `components-pixi` not `engine-layout`, because a pre-built `dist/` is what lets a game ship stale engine code. Note for later slices: `pnpm lint` (exits 2 for existing packages too — the eslint 9 flat-config migration was never done) and `tsc` (~53k pre-existing errors, no `svelte-check` installed) are NOT usable gates here; the fingerprint is.

- 2026-08-18 — **One home for the engine's coded UI strings, so the Localization tool can see them.** `BALANCE`/`WIN`/`BET`, the menu + info-page entries, the bet menu, the settings and autoplay modals and the info page's rules copy all already localized by source-as-key (every one goes through `stateI18nDerived.translate`), but the literals were inlined across two `i18nDerived` maps and `apps/lines`' `infoManifest` — invisible to `/localization`, so there was no row to translate and the chrome stayed in the source language for any language the code catalogs ship no entry for (`en`/`zh` only). New `engine-layout/uiText.ts`: `UI_TEXT` (the catalog keys, semantic-name → source literal), `UI_INFO_RULES` (the default info-page rules), and `collectUiTextStrings()` — the launcher harvests the SAME registry the components render from, so the two cannot drift. Literals unchanged, so no existing translation is orphaned. Two behaviour fixes rode along: `HudReadout` rendered an authored bet-mode `betAmountLabel` RAW while its `HudCaption`/`LabelBet` siblings translated it, and the social-build relabels (`SPIN`/`PLAY BONUS`) bypassed `translate()` entirely — both now localize. `pnpm build --filter=lines...` green. Detail in [localization.md](localization.md).

- 2026-08-18 — **The runtime fetch aborted at the instant the response arrived, silently serving STALE BAKED data on ~half of boots.** `RUNTIME_ATTEMPT_TIMEOUT_MS` was 30s, set against a then-measured ~17-19s assemble. Measured 2026-08-18 on Book of Borut Remake (183 KB bundle): TTFB **29.6s / 33.1s / 34.2s** over three fetches — the cap sat INSIDE the response spread. The retry usually rescued it (the endpoint single-flights, so attempt 2 joins the run in flight), which is why it read as "flaky" rather than broken; the only symptom was `live data fetch failed (signal timed out) — retry 1/2`. A boot that lost the coin flip is not merely "a bit stale": measured against the baked bundle via a temporary probe, `winText.toast.symbolAsImage` is `false`, `bakedSymbolNames()` is empty so a symbol renders as its raw id (`H4`), and the win-text template is the English default — several unrelated-looking wrongnesses at once on a game that looks healthy. Cap 30s→**60s**; budget 45s→**90s** and now a REAL deadline (each attempt clamps to the budget remaining, so a hang is bounded at exactly 90.0s instead of overrunning to ~121s). New near-miss warning: a SUCCESSFUL attempt spending >60% of its cap logs what to raise, so the next rot announces itself before it starts failing. Verified on a fake-clock harness over the patched control flow (34s → succeeds attempt 1; 50s → succeeds + warns; 502×2 → retries then succeeds; 401 → fails fast; hang → bounded 90.0s); `pnpm --filter lines build` green. **Still open: the ~30s assemble itself**, which every live-data boot pays.

- 2026-08-17 — **Win text lost its `{amount}`/`{count}`/`{symbolName}` — regression from the localization fix, same day.** `registerEditorTextLocalization`'s resolver returned `stateI18nDerived.translate(key)`, i.e. `i18n._()`. A runtime message compiler is installed, so `_()` evaluates the message as ICU — and with **no `values`** it substitutes EMPTY for every `{token}`. Win text is deliberately localize-THEN-interpolate (`formatWinText`), so the template's placeholders were consumed before the engine could fill them: `"You win {amount} with {count} {symbolName}"` rendered as a bare **"Vinci con"**. It also killed the inline symbol IMAGE, which keys off the interpolated `{symbolName}`. Latent until the catalog actually resolved — which is exactly what the fix above enabled, and it would have hit English too. The resolver now returns the RAW catalog string (falling back to `translate()` for a non-string value); plain strings are byte-identical either way. Verified live: `"Vinci 10,00  con 5"`.
- 2026-08-17 — **Currency renders its SYMBOL wherever the symbol is unambiguous.** `Intl`'s default (`currencyDisplay: 'symbol'`) prints the ISO code whenever CLDR judges the glyph ambiguous for the locale — Italian gives `€` and `£` but `1234,50 USD`, because a bare `$` doesn't say WHICH dollar. Forcing `narrowSymbol` globally wins back `R$`/`zł`/`₹`/`₺` but collapses USD/CAD/AUD/NZD/MXN/ARS/CLP into one indistinguishable `$`. So `numberToCurrencyString` now takes the narrow symbol **only when nothing else claims it** — uniqueness COMPUTED from `Intl.supportedValuesOf('currency')`, not a hand-kept table that would rot, cached per (locale, currency) and skipped entirely on a runtime without `supportedValuesOf`. In `it`: BRL `BRL`→`R$`, PLN→`zł`, CZK→`Kč`, HUF→`Ft`, RON→`lei`, TRY→`₺`, UAH→`₴`, INR→`₹`, IDR→`Rp`, VND→`₫`, ZAR→`R`, NGN→`₦`, NOK→`NKr`; USD/CAD/AUD/NZD/MXN/ARS/CLP/JPY/KRW/CNY unchanged, and `en` is unchanged throughout. **Caveat that bit immediately:** a BITMAP font only renders glyphs in its charset. Book of Borut's four fonts carry 94–95 ASCII glyphs — `$` yes, `€`/`£`/`¥` no — so a non-ASCII symbol renders as nothing where the ISO code used to render fine. Rebuild affected fonts in Font Maker with the `currency` preset (`0123456789.,$€£¥¢+- `) or a custom charset before selling a title in such a currency.

- 2026-08-17 — **A project's translations never reached the game — two independent bugs, both fixed.** Symptom: a fully-translated project rendered English in every locale while NUMBERS localised correctly (`5000,00 €` under `lang=it`), with no error anywhere. (1) **The merged catalog was snapshotted before the runtime bundle existed.** `apps/lines/src/i18n/messagesMap/index.ts` built `const messagesMap = mergeMessagesMaps([…, bakedLocalizationMessagesMap()])` at MODULE scope, but that getter only returns live strings once `prepareRuntimeBundle()` has run — and that is `+layout.ts`'s `load()`, which SvelteKit runs AFTER importing the route's modules. So it always captured the empty baked map and the fetched translations were dropped. Every other runtime-bundle consumer is a lazy function, which is why localization alone was affected. Now `getMessagesMap()`, called from `+layout.svelte` (`<LoadI18n>`) and `Game.svelte` (`registerEditorTextLocalization`) — both after `load()`. Deliberately NOT memoised: a cache re-introduces the same class of bug the moment something calls it early. (2) **Authored component `label` params were never run through the text resolver.** A parametric HUD readout carries its caption as `params.label` (`"Balance"`), which the Localization tool harvests as a key — but `resolveLocalizedText` only covered text nodes and `textBox.text`, so the three coded consumers (`HudReadout`, `HudCaption`, `ButtonLabel` in `components-ui-pixi`) rendered the literal. Resolved at the consumer rather than in `resolveComponentParams`, so a frozen/saved component def can't shadow the fix and enum-ish string params (`source`, `variant`, `icon`) are untouched. Verified live against the real Book of Borut Remake runtime bundle: `lang=it` → `Saldo` / `Vincita` / `Puntata` / `Fai clic sul pulsante di giro per iniziare`; `lang=en` byte-identical to before. **Reaching online games needs a Runtime release + republish.** Related: `/localization` ships REVIEWED strings only — an unreviewed translation reaches an `ie_authoring=1` launch but never a player or the bake.

- 2026-08-17 — **`?currency=` is now read, and OVERRIDES the RGS's currency.** New `stateUrlDerived.currency()` (`packages/state-shared/src/stateUrl.svelte.ts`) + `Authenticate.svelte` taking `launchCurrency || authenticateData.balance.currency`. Previously the launch param was declared in the `Key` union but had no getter, so every game formatted amounts with whatever the RGS echoed — always `USD` from the mock and from the Play4Fun facade. The picker that drives it lives in the launcher; see [launcher.md](launcher.md) for the full entry.

- 2026-08-11 — **Authorable layout profiles — the layout-bucket set is now author-defined, not the hardcoded four.** New `constants-shared/layoutProfile.ts` defines a `LayoutProfile` (ordered buckets, each with `id`/`label`/`box`/`rule{minRatio,maxRatio,minSide,maxSide}`/`stacked`) + `selectBucket`/`resolveBucketBox`/`normalizeLayoutProfile`. `DEFAULT_LAYOUT_PROFILE` reproduces the legacy `desktop/tablet/landscape/portrait` decision tree EXACTLY (proven byte-identical across a device grid in `packages/engine-layout/scripts/test-layout-profile-parity.mjs`), so a game shipping no profile is unchanged. `utils-layout/createLayout`: `layoutType()`/`isStacked()` now derive from `selectBucket(activeProfile)`; new `setAuthoredLayoutProfile()`/`getActiveLayoutProfile()` (twin of `setAuthoredMainSizesMap`); the STANDARD frame box is profile-driven via `resolveBucketBox` (crash-safe fallback for an author-added bucket a coded game has no box for). `LayoutType` widened to `string`; `LayoutDoc` gained optional `layoutProfile`. Authored in Admin (pipeline default, `app_settings.layoutProfileDefault`) + the Scene Editor (per-project override, sparse on the doc) — see `docs/status/editor.md`. The publish bundle bakes the effective profile onto the doc; `apps/lines/src/editor-scenes.ts` adopts it at boot via `setAuthoredLayoutProfile(doc.layoutProfile)`. Reaches online games via a **Runtime release + republish**. NOT browser-verified (needs the live launcher). Files: `packages/constants-shared/{layoutProfile.ts,layout.ts}`, `packages/utils-layout/src/createLayout.svelte.ts`, `packages/engine-layout/src/lib/{types.ts,index.ts}`, `packages/game-spec/src/schema.ts`, `apps/lines/src/editor-scenes.ts`.
- 2026-08-10 — **Per-layoutType overrides now carry TEXT STYLE + COMPONENT PARAMS at runtime** (see `docs/status/editor.md` for the full write-up). `NodeOverride` gained sparse `style?: Partial<TextStyle>` + `params?: Record<string, unknown>`; `<LayoutNodeView>`'s `resolvedStyle` folds the per-ratio style (`resolveOverrideTextStyle`) under bound-param overrides, and `<ComponentInstance>` overlays per-ratio params as REACTIVE getters (keyed on the override-union keys, read `getContextLayout().layoutType()` so a device rotation re-resolves; defined before the live-feed getters so `value`/`label` still win; the init-stable structural reads that use `staticParams` directly are untouched). Additive + sparse ⇒ a doc with no per-ratio override is byte-identical (parity). Reaches online games via a **Runtime release + republish** (schema rides in the doc; the reader code ships with the runtime). Files: `packages/engine-layout/src/lib/{types.ts, resolveTransform.ts, componentParams.ts, LayoutNodeView.svelte, ComponentInstance.svelte}`.
- 2026-08-10 — **Flow v2 §6.3 in-game TEXT MESSAGE overlay — GAME-SIDE RENDER (`apps/lines`).** The schema/interpreter/handle were already built (`packages/engine-flow-v2` `TextMessageNode`; `flowV2Runtime.svelte.ts` now exposes `textMessages` + reactive `messageShown`). Added the Pixi render: new `apps/lines/src/components/FlowV2Messages.svelte` renders one localized `<Text>` per authored `textMessage` node at its normalized `place`, INSIDE `<MainContainer>` (design box `mainLayout.width`×`height`, read via `getContextLayout()` — so a place of {0.5,0.5} is dead-centre and lands identically across layouts). Visibility per node = `gateMatches(node.visibleWhile) || flow.messageShown(node.id)` — the OR serves BOTH use cases with one rule: a state-gated prompt ("Click spin to start", `visibleWhile:'idle'`) shows while its round-phase gate is on, a flow-driven prompt ("Good luck", bare `show` exec + `autoHideMs`) shows while the interpreter's reactive `SvelteSet` flag is raised. `gateMatches`: `idle`→`stateXstateDerived.isIdle()`, `spinning`→`isPlaying()`, `always`→true, `none`/undefined→false, and **`freeSpins`→`stateGame.gameType === 'freegame'`** — the game MODE flag (set by the freeSpin book events, cleared to `basegame` when the feature ends), chosen over `stateUi.freeSpinCounterShow` because the counter's a HUD-visibility flag the intro/outro celebration screens flip off MID-feature. Text is run through `resolveLocalizedText` (localize-then-render); style defaults to readable white 40px `proxima-nova` with a dark stroke + drop shadow, overridable by `node.style.size`/`.color`. Wired into `Game.svelte` near `<FlowV2Mount>`, mounted UNGATED by `flowV2DrivesScreens` (a book-events-only flow can still author messages); the handle is mirrored into a `flowV2Handle` `$state` at load, alongside `flowV2Containers`. Independent of the shared single-slot `stateMessage` HUD toast. Renders nothing when no v2 flow / no message nodes (parity). Verified: `pnpm --filter lines build` green; `svelte-check apps/lines` **0 errors** (2 pre-existing tsconfig warnings only); Prettier clean on the new file. NOT browser-verified (needs a flow authoring a `textMessage` node). Reaches online games only via a `Runtime release (runtime=lines)` + republish; standalone `bookofborut` picks up the `packages/*` half on an engine submodule bump but must mirror the `apps/lines/src/components/{FlowV2Messages.svelte, Game.svelte}` wiring into its own frozen `src/` copy. Files: `apps/lines/src/components/FlowV2Messages.svelte` (new), `apps/lines/src/components/Game.svelte`.
- 2026-08-03 — **Fix + polish: fast-forward / tap-to-skip and win-tier escalation.** The count-up (`WinGate` `WinCountUpProvider`, accelerated by `holdToSpeedUp` / slammed by `tapToSkip`) runs on a SEPARATE clock from the spine idle-completes that advance the escalation chain (`WinAnimation`). When the count-up finished mid-chain the gate concluded ~300ms later while the tiers were still walking ⇒ the escalation was cut off. Three parts: (1) **SMOOTH RAMP** — while the player HOLDS to fast-forward, `WinAnimation` runs the tier intro/idle spines at the gate's live `interactionSpeedScale` (published as `winState.escalationSpeedScale`, applied as the `SpineTrack`/`TrackEntry.timeScale` via `propsSyncEffect`), so the tiers visibly accelerate in lockstep with the count-up; release ⇒ 1×. Only the HOLD drives it (a `tapToSkip` slam stays instant). (2) **COLLAPSE SAFETY NET** — if the count-up still completes before the (now-faster) chain reaches the final tier, `WinAnimation` jumps `stepIndex` to the last step + plays its outro (`animationState='outro'`), so the player lands on the biggest tier's art as it exits. (3) The GATE defers conclusion until that outro finishes — `WinGate.concludePresentation()` awaits `winState.escalationOutroComplete` on the escalation path (`escalationActive`, published by `WinVisual`) before resolving the round-block, routed from BOTH the count-up `OnMount` and the coded `PressToContinue` post-count-up tap. Coordination = `winState` latches (`escalationActive`/`escalationOutroComplete`/`escalationSpeedScale`) + a `WinAnimation.onOutroComplete` callback; empty-outro safety fires completion immediately (a mis-authored tier can't hang the now-awaiting gate); per-win rewind + `interactionSpeedScale` reset on `winShow` (no stale fast-start on a repeat win). NON-escalating single-tier path (no `chain`) byte-identical: ramp/collapse/rewind/outro-wait all guarded on `escalating`, `timeScale` resolves to 1 (= spine default), and the un-escalating gate concludes as before incl. tap-to-slam. Files: `apps/lines/src/game/winState.svelte.ts`, `components/{WinAnimation,WinVisual,WinGate}.svelte`. `lines` build + `svelte-check apps/lines` (0 errors). Escalation is config-authored (not the dev default), so live-verify is owner-owed. Ships in `_runtime/lines` ⇒ needs a **Runtime release + republish**.
- 2026-08-03 — **Desktop launcher build broke: `pixi-svelte` `svelte-package` fails (`No Lingui config found` → `src\app.html does not exist`).** The game build (`pnpm --filter "<game>^..." run build`) died at `pixi-svelte`'s `svelte-package`. Root cause: `svelte-package` only loads the app-level `vite.config.js` (`config-vite` → `sveltekit()` + `lingui()`) when it has to **preprocess a `<style>` block** — and #181 added `MemoryHud.svelte`, the FIRST styled `.svelte` in `pixi-svelte/src/lib`, newly triggering that path. It then hit two pieces of standard `svelte-package`-library scaffolding pixi-svelte never had: no `lingui.config` (every other `config-vite` consumer ships one → `lingui()` factory aborts) and no `src/app.html` (SvelteKit `sync` aborts). In-repo turbo cache masked it; the game repo runs `svelte-package` cold. **Fix ([PR #192](https://github.com/Invisible-Wall-SL/Invisible-Engine/pull/192)):** add `packages/pixi-svelte/lingui.config.ts` (re-exports `config-lingui`) + `packages/pixi-svelte/src/app.html` (minimal skeleton; `svelte-package` only packages `src/lib`, so it never enters `dist`). Same class as the `new-game.mjs` bug (4) below. Verified: clean `pnpm run build` in pixi-svelte green; `pnpm --filter lines... run build` (dist wiped) builds end-to-end. Reaches the desktop launcher only after the game repo's engine submodule pin advances to this commit.
- 2026-08-03 — **Win-tier presentation split (config = structure, `win` component = presentation).** The big-win presentation (spine bundle / intro-idle-outro / duration / sfx-bgm) moved off the `/config` panel onto the `win` component, per tier keyed by ALIAS, generated from the active config's big tiers. Engine: `engine-layout` `winComponentDef`/`winTierPresentationParams`/`DEFAULT_WIN_TIERS` (built-in `WIN_DEF` regenerated); `WinVisual` resolves per-tier animation/spine from live params (alias-keyed, replaced `TIER_PREFIX`, **closed the single-tier `spineKey` gap** — `activeSpine = <alias>Spine ?? winLevelData.spineKey ?? winSpine`); duration/sound bridge out-of-tree via `bakedWinPresentationParams()` → `publishWinPresentation()` (Game.svelte boot) → `gameConfig.withWinPresentation` overlay on `activeWinLevelData`/`ByAlias`/`Chain`. Launcher: `/editor` server passes `winTiers`, client `withConfigWinTiers` rebuilds the def; `/config` panel slimmed to name/threshold/escalation. Un-authored ⇒ byte-identical (empty overlay + `DEFAULT_WIN_TIERS` = the coded `winLevelMap` big rows + live params all empty). Verified: `game-config` typecheck/spike, `engine-layout`/`lines`/`launcher-api` builds, `svelte-check apps/lines` 0 errors. Launcher render + live spin owner-verify-owed. **Canvas preview landed** (focused-param → live spine preview of the tier's bundle + animation, `EditorProperties`→`EditorCanvas`→`EditorSpineLayer`, preview-only). **Reaches online games only via a `Runtime release (runtime=lines)` + republish** (the reading code ships in `_runtime/lines`); standalone Borut needs an engine submodule bump + mirroring `apps/lines/src` win wiring. See `docs/status/game-config.md`.
- 2026-07-30 — **Free-spin OUTRO hang on a real large win under a v2-driven, author-rebuilt outro (double `freeSpinOutroCountUp` subscriber).** On the Book of Borut remake (online `_runtime/lines`, v2 flow owns `load`, author-rebuilt `freeSpinOutro`) the `freeSpinEnd` chain HUNG at `action freeSpinOutroCountUp` — the awaited `broadcastAsync` never resolved, so it never reached `showContainer{awaitComplete}`/`exitFreeSpinOutro`; on-screen the outro count text (`freeSpinOutroTotalWin` ← `freeSpinOutroState.countUpAmount`) rose, DROPPED, then rose again at a different speed. Root cause: `resolveFreeSpinOutroMount` (`apps/lines/src/game/freeSpinOwnership.ts`) computed `top: ctx.ownsOutro ? 'driver-transfer' : …` — reading v1 `ownsOutro` BEFORE `flowV2DrivesScreens`. `resolveFlowOwnsFreeSpins` reads the v1 `bakedFlowDoc`, which on a MIGRATED v2 game can COEXIST with the v2 doc and still report `ownsOutro` true (the outro screen + `freeSpinEnd` edge survive in the stale v1 doc). That mounted a SECOND `<FreeSpinOutroDriver holdUntilComplete>` (top band) alongside the v2 `band` `driver` ⇒ TWO `freeSpinOutroCountUp` subscribers (each returns a pending `waitForResolve` from `broadcastAsync`'s `Promise.all`) + TWO `WinCountUpProvider`/`OutroStatePublisher` instances fighting over `freeSpinOutroState.countUpAmount` (the count drop / double-speed). The v2 `band` driver self-resolves, but the v1 `holdUntilComplete` driver waits for an active-set `true→false` edge that never fires under a v2 `showContainer` outro ⇒ `Promise.all` never resolves ⇒ the round hangs (the "invariant" the fs7 test ASSUMED — it SKIPPED the `flowV2DrivesScreens && ownsOutro` combo as "impossible by construction" — was never enforced in `resolveFreeSpinOutroMount`). Fix: `top: ctx.flowV2DrivesScreens ? null : ctx.ownsOutro ? 'driver-transfer' : 'gate'` — v2 forces `top` null UNCONDITIONALLY (v1 `ownsOutro` is inert under v2, now enforced not assumed), so exactly one `freeSpinOutroCountUp` subscriber for EVERY combination. Headless guard: `tools/flow-spike/fs7Outro.ts` B no longer skips that combo (all 16 combos assert one subscriber) + a REGRESSION spot-check; it FAILS on the old ternary (`{band:'driver', top:'driver-transfer'}` = 2) and PASSES on the fix (`pnpm --filter flow-spike run fs7outro`). Reaches the online remake ONLY via a `Runtime release (runtime=lines)` (NOT triggered here); standalone `bookofborut` picks it up on an engine submodule bump.
- 2026-07-30 — **Ambient board smoke fades out under a full-screen tap-to-continue overlay** (retrigger celebration, free-spin intro/outro gates). The signal is `stateUi.continuePressCount > 0` (bumped/decremented by `PressToContinue.svelte`). Two coverage paths: **(1) coded atmosphere** — `apps/lines/src/components/Background.svelte`'s drifting `dust` spine (on both `foregroundAnimation` + `foregroundFeatureAnimation`) is split into its own `FadeContainer`s gated on `show && showDust` where `showDust = stateUi.continuePressCount === 0`, so the dust fades for the duration of the gate and the static `idle` background is untouched; no tap overlay ⇒ `showDust` true ⇒ byte-identical to before (dust always played). **(2) authored atmosphere** — a new visibility source **`tapOverlayIdle`** (`VISIBILITY_SOURCE_KEYS` + label in `packages/engine-layout/src/lib/componentCatalog.ts`, registered in `apps/lines/src/components/Game.svelte` as `tapOverlayIdle: boolSource(() => stateUi.continuePressCount === 0)`) lets an author bind an authored background-smoke node's `visibleSource` to it so it hides during any tap celebration (the coded `Background` is suppressed when authored background art exists, so the authored path is the only lever for an authored spine). Additive — an author who never binds it, and any game with no tap overlay active, is byte-identical. Reaches online games via a runtime release (`runtime:lines`); standalone `bookofborut` picks the engine-layout catalog entry up on a submodule bump, and must mirror the `Background.svelte` dust split + the `Game.svelte` registration into its own `src/*` copies.
- 2026-07-29 — **FS-7 outro follow-up (engine-side): count-up-complete signal + opt-in `WinCountUpProvider` speed-scale; outro coin knobs removed.** Detail in [docs/status/flow.md](flow.md); the engine-side pieces: `ENGINE_SIGNAL_CATALOG` gains `freeSpinOutroCountUpComplete` (`componentCatalog.ts`), fired via a new payload-less emitter event the outro driver broadcasts on count-up completion — an author arms a tap/prompt with `tapArmAfterSignal`/`hiddenUntilSignal`. `WinCountUpProvider` (`components-pixi`) gains an OPT-IN `speedScale` prop for continuous hold-to-fast-forward acceleration: it re-targets the svelte `Tween` on speed change and owns completion via a value-reached watch (because `Tween.set` aborts the previous task without resolving its promise); OMITTED ⇒ the original single-tween path, so the WIN overlay + fallback outro gate are byte-identical (only the lines outro driver passes it). The outro coin-fountain knobs on `boundComponentCatalog.ts` `FreeSpinOutroVisual` (`showCoins`/`coinsX`/`coinsY`/`coinsLevel`) + their `OutroCoinsConfig`/`freeSpinOutroState.coins` plumbing were REMOVED (owner: the driver never emits coins; authors place their own). The separate WIN-visual `showCoins` (`builtinComponents.ts`/`WinVisual`) is UNAFFECTED. Reaches the Book of Borut remake on a runtime-bundle republish + `engine` submodule bump.
- 2026-07-28 — **FS-7 free-spin OUTRO authoring parity (engine-side surfaces).** The Flow feature (headless `FreeSpinOutroDriver` split, full-primitive outro authoring) is logged in [docs/status/flow.md](flow.md); the engine-side additions it needed: `ENGINE_PARAM_CATALOG` gains `freeSpinOutroTotalWin` (the live count-up value source) and `ENGINE_SIGNAL_CATALOG` gains `freeSpinOutroBigWin`/`freeSpinOutroSmallWin` (`componentCatalog.ts`); `ComponentInstance.svelte`'s per-instance fired-signal bus now also subscribes to registered signals named by a `hiddenUntilSignal`/`tapArmAfterSignal` gate (not only spine cues), so plain art can gate on a game signal — parity-safe (no gate ⇒ no new subscription); `boundComponentCatalog.ts` `FreeSpinOutroVisual` gains the coin-fountain knobs (`showCoins`/`coinsX`/`coinsY`/`coinsLevel`). Additive across every game (byte-parity when unused). Reaches the Book of Borut remake on a runtime-bundle republish + `engine` submodule bump.
- 2026-07-27 — **An authored `win` Overlay renders WYSIWYG on ANY screen space (the residual position/size mismatch after the earlier size fix).** Owner report: after the `WinAnimation` natural-size fix landed, the game STILL drew the win overlay smaller AND offset to the upper-left of the board vs the Scene Editor (which shows it board-centred at full size). Root cause was a **coordinate-space mismatch driven by the host screen's SPACE**, not the two BASE sizes (that was the prior fix). The `win` componentInstance sits on a **`canvas`-space** screen ("Base game overlays"), but `BOUND_COMPONENT_DEFAULTS.WinVisual` declares `space:'game', placement:'boardCentre'` — it's a **board-relative** overlay. A `game`-space screen wraps its nodes in `<MainContainer>` (main→window scale + centre, the SAME mapping the reel board uses), so board and overlay scale together and editor==game at any window. A `canvas`-space screen has **no `<MainContainer>`**, so the runtime drew the overlay at **raw window pixels** (`WinVisual` boundToInstance renders `content()` at LOCAL origin, relying on a game-space MainContainer the canvas screen never provides), which does NOT adapt to window size — while the board (game space) scales up with the window via `mainScale`. Net on any window larger than the editor's reference frame: the raw-placed win is smaller than, and above-left of, the scaled-up board. Editor and runtime actually AGREED on the (raw-canvas) formula; they diverged from each OTHER only because the editor's fixed reference frame ≠ the player's window, and canvas space is the one space that doesn't adapt. (Free-spin intro is "perfect" for the same reason inverted: it's a `centre`-**placement** coded gate that self-centres/self-scales to the live canvas, never using raw x/y.) **Fix — one shared notion of "effective space", applied on BOTH surfaces so they can't drift:** new `engine-layout` `hostedComponentSpace(def)` returns the catalog `space` of the FIRST bound component in a componentInstance's def tree (WIN_DEF → `win-anim` bind → `WinVisual` → `'game'`). (a) RUNTIME `LayoutNodeView` componentInstance branch: when the hosted space is `'game'` but the host scene is not `game`/`background`, it re-applies the exact `<MainContainer>` mapping inline (`mainLayout()` centre + scale) to the instance's `x`/`y`/`scale` — a single `<Container>`, so z-order is untouched. (b) EDITOR `EditorCanvas` `nodeTransform`: the same override for a hosted-`game` instance on a non-`game`/`background` scene, mapping via `mainToWorld`/`mainScale` — which the 2D canvas, the spine overlay (`worldTransformOf`=`nodeTransform`) and the selection box all consume, so the preview follows. (c) EDITOR `isGameSpaceNode` gains the same predicate so drag/scale writeback inverts the main mapping and the value round-trips. **Parity:** gated to `hostedComponentSpace === 'game'` AND host scene ≠ `game`/`background`, so a win/free-spin visual on a `game` scene (the reference layouts + the free-spin visuals today) is byte-identical, a `background` scene still cover-fits, and every non-`game`-catalog instance (HUD/buttons/scenery, Background/Transition/LoadingScreen at `canvas`) is untouched. The coded **OFF path** (`bind:Win`, `boundToInstance={false}`) is the Game.svelte mount, never a doc componentInstance — it doesn't hit `LayoutNodeView`'s instance branch and `WinVisual` still self-wraps in `<MainContainer>`; byte-identical. A game not authoring `win` has no such instance. **Free-spin visuals share the risk and are fixed by the SAME generic path** (`FreeSpinIntroVisual`/`OutroVisual`/`ExpandingSymbol`/`FreeSpinIntroSymbolReveal` all declare `space:'game'`): placed on a game scene today ⇒ no change; if ever dropped on a canvas scene they now game-frame automatically. **Verified:** `pnpm --filter engine-layout build` + `pnpm --filter lines build` + `pnpm --filter launcher-api build` green; `svelte-check apps/lines` **0 errors / 0 warnings** (pinned `typescript@5.8.3` + `svelte-check@4.3.0`); Prettier clean.
